# Elifba architecture

T3 (2026-11-19) put 36, 37 and 42 and the sound to the owner again, who chose: C, «Rəqibə toxun», for 36 (no tray; the rival's side of the strip throws); twelve new phrases, two of them the owner's, for 37; for 42 the screen draws each taunt in its middle and says it; and a voice — the board's, Nigar, one clip per taunt — on the target's phone and the screen (ADR 029 "Amended 2026-11-19 (T3)", §12 "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)").

This document is the contract the code follows. It was written in roadmap step A1, before any code existed, so that steps A2–A11 can be built without re-deciding. Change this file first when the model changes; a step that has to depart from it updates it in the same change.

Roles, parents' children and the picture and number games were designed the same way in roadmap step R1 (2026-11-11), before their code: text marked R2–R7 describes what that step builds ("What each step builds"), and is the contract for it. Until the step lands, the code is what the unmarked text says.

The adults' area — rooms, where account holders race each other at memory, logic and word games — was designed in roadmap step B1 (2026-11-17), again before its code: §12 and the text marked B2–B7 are the contract for those steps. Nothing in it changes a rule of a class game, a home game or a child.

The adults' area was turned games-first in roadmap step G1 (2026-11-18), after the owner chose among three clickable prototypes: [026 games first](decisions/026-games-first.md). §12 "Games first" and the text marked G2–G3 are the contract those steps built (G2 and G3 landed on 2026-11-18); where it amends the B-marked text, it is the area now. A game's motion, sound and final are [025](decisions/025-room-motion-sound-and-final.md); its sound set, chosen by ear, is [028](decisions/028-room-sound-set.md); and at the final the screen says the winner's first name from the name list's clip, [030](decisions/030-winner-name-at-the-final.md).

How a room's link is sent and opened was designed in roadmap step I1 (2026-11-19), before its code: [033 the invitation link](decisions/033-invitation-link.md). §12 "The invitation link (I2)" and the text marked I2 are the contract for I2 — a share row beside the QR and the code («WhatsApp», «Paylaş» or «Linki kopyala»), a link opened by hand that joins at once, and a door where a newcomer makes an account with the player role in the same step. Every player is still an account with a confirmed address. I2 landed on 2026-11-19 (§12 "The invitation link (I2)", "As built (I2)").

Every decision below is written as **Decision** (the choice), **Why** (the reason) and **Rejected** (the alternative and why not).

## 0. The product

Elifba is an open-source (MIT) web platform for primary-school teachers in Azerbaijan, and for parents at home. It teaches children to read Azerbaijani — letters, syllables (heca), words and sentences — through short games played on the classroom wall; children who cannot read yet play the same way with pictures: animals, fruit, colours, numbers and counting (R5, R6).

```
 classroom wall (projector)                     teacher's phone
┌────────────────────────────┐                 ┌──────────────┐
│  "Ə hərfini tap!"          │   tap "Ə"       │  [Ə] [E] [A] │
│   (E)   (Ə)   (A)   (O)    │ ◀────────────── │  [O]         │
│          ✸ burst ✸         │   ≤ 300 ms      │  At the board│
└────────────────────────────┘                 │  🐼 Aylin     │
   a child touches "Ə"                         └──────────────┘
```

1. A **teacher** registers, creates a **class** and adds **students** by pasting a list of names. Students have no accounts.
2. The teacher opens a **game** on the laptop connected to the projector — or, on a shared school computer, sends it there from the phone: the **board**. A game is one **mechanic** (find, read and tap, build a syllable, build a word, build a sentence) dressed in one **theme pack** (train, balloons, animals, fruit, our cities, national symbols) at one **level**.
3. A child goes to the wall and touches an item. At the same moment the teacher taps the same item on the phone (the **remote**). The board bursts the item and moves on — to the class it looks as if the wall reacted to the child. On a touch screen or with a mouse the board also works without a phone.
4. Every solved round gives the student at the board points. Each class has a **daily** and a **weekly** ranking; places 1–3 get a **podium** page that the teacher saves as a 1080×1920 image and posts to WhatsApp status.

At home (R2–R6):

5. Right after registration an account chooses a **role**: «Müəllim» (teacher) or «Valideyn» (parent). One account may hold both and switch between the teacher area and the parent area (§1 "Accounts and roles", ADR 010).
6. A **parent** adds their own **children** — a first name and an avatar, no class — and starts a game for one child on whatever screen is at hand: the phone or tablet the child touches, or the television with the phone as the remote. A 3–6 year old plays the **picture and number games** (the parent says «Pişik hansıdır?» and the child taps the picture; «Neçə alma var?» and the child taps the digit); a 6–10 year old plays the reading games (§2, ADR 011, ADR 012).
7. Every solved round gives the child **stars**. The parent sees each child's stars; there is no ranking and no podium between siblings (§5).

Among adults (B2–B7; §12), in an area of its own that is switched off until it is launched:

8. An account takes a third role, «Oyunçu» (**player**), and opens a **room** or joins one with the room's 6-digit code. Everyone in a room is an account, under its own name; there is no class, no child and no board.
9. The room's host starts a game — «Yaddaş» (memory), «Məntiq və ardıcıllıq» (logic and sequences) or «Söz oyunları» (word games), at a difficulty — and everyone answers **the same rounds at the same time, each on their own phone**. A big screen is optional. The server alone knows the answers, keeps the time and gives the points; being fast counts.
10. After every round the room sees its **ranking**. A closed room's results stay with its players' names for the retention period; a player can take their name out, the host can delete the room.

Owner decisions (fixed): name `elifba`; interface Azerbaijani first, bilingual foundation (az default + en) from day one; MIT unless the owner changes it (roadmap step O1); public GitHub repository; reuse ready-made packages where they fit.

Owner decisions of 2026-11-11 (fixed; the records say where each one lives): two roles, `teacher` and `parent`, chosen right after registration by e-mail or with Google (ADR 010); the roles come from `spatie/laravel-permission` (ADR 010); one account may hold both (ADR 010); a parent plays with their own children without a class, the 3–6 year olds with pictures and the 6–10 year olds with the reading games (ADR 011, ADR 012); stars and progress per child, no ranking or podium between siblings (ADR 011, §5); no recorded voice, the parent reads the prompt aloud (§3 "Voice"; reopened the same day after R1 and replaced by ADR 015: the board says the prompt and the praise in a prerecorded synthetic voice, and never a name; names reopened on 2026-11-12, below); the picture games are open to both roles (ADR 012); the numbers 1–10 mean recognising a digit and counting (ADR 012); colours are plain shapes drawn as our own SVGs (ADR 012, §8); the landing page and the READMEs get a parent section last (R7).

Owner decisions of 2026-11-12 (fixed; ADR 016, built in the step called V2): the board says a student's or a child's first name only when it is on Elifba's built-in name list of common Azerbaijani first names, voiced once, offline, in the board's voice and committed as content; any other name stays silent and is still shown; no name from the database ever reaches a speech provider, and an instance needs no key. The name is said before the praise after a solved round (a chosen student, or the home child), before «Salam!» at the start of a home game and before «Əla oynadın!» at the end of a game; never when «Kim oynayır?» picks a student, and never in rankings, on the podium or on the share card. A name's clip is the name alone, said before the phrase, the order the owner chose by ear (§3 "Voice").

Owner decisions of 2026-11-17 (fixed; ADRs 019–024, §12, built in B2–B7): games for adults live in a separate area — not in the teacher or the parent area, nothing of it on a class, student, child or board page or in the game catalog — behind a switch that is off by default, so that it is hidden from teachers and parents too until launch (ADR 019); no age, date of birth or age group: a room chooses a difficulty (ADR 023, ADR 024); a live race in which everyone plays the same rounds at the same time on their own phone, a big screen optional and never required (ADR 021); the first game families are memory, logic and sequences, and Azerbaijani word games, without quick arithmetic (ADR 023); a room is opened by an account that holds a third role with its own agreement sentence (ADR 019); every player joins with an account, not with a code and a typed name (ADR 020); a room's results are kept with names for the retention period (ADR 022).

### Stack

Versions checked 2026-11-09. Replace one only with a concrete reason, recorded here.

| Layer   | Choice                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Server  | Laravel 13, PHP 8.4, the official React starter kit (Laravel Installer 5.31.1) with its built-in authentication                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Pages   | Inertia 3 + React 19.2 + TypeScript + Tailwind 4, shadcn/ui from the starter kit, Wayfinder typed routes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Live    | Laravel Reverb 1.x (Pusher protocol), `laravel-echo` + `@laravel/echo-react` 2.6.3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Board   | Phaser 4.2.1, loaded only on the board page                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Extras  | `qrcode.react` 4.2.0 (pairing QR), `modern-screenshot` 4.7.0 (share card), `canvas-confetti` 1.9.4 (podium; a room's final, §12), `jsqr` 1.4.0 (Apache-2.0; the code field's scanner where the browser has no `BarcodeDetector`, §12 "Games first"), `livekit-client` 2.24.1 (Apache-2.0, pinned exactly; the live voice, imported only by `resources/js/rooms/voice.ts` when the page first joins the voice — by itself for an admitted player whose account agreed, or on the tap of «Səsə qoşul» (ADR 031 as amended 2026-11-19) — its own chunk from the app's host, §12 "Live voice"), `zod` 4.6 (the round contract and client-event validation in TypeScript, §2, §4) |
| Roles   | `spatie/laravel-permission` 8.5 (R2; checked 2026-11-11: PHP ^8.3, Laravel 12 and 13, Larastan 3.9; roles only, ADR 010)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Tests   | Pest (PHP); Vitest for pure TypeScript modules from A6, run as `vp test` (Vite+ ships Vitest 5; `npm run test`, configured in `vite.config.ts`'s `test` block); PHPStan level 8 (Larastan), Pint, `tsc`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Runtime | Node 22 for builds; dev served by Laravel Herd; production as docker compose (§10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

## 1. Domains and data model

**Decision.** Code lives in `app/Domain/{Language,Content,Classroom,Family,Play,Rankings}` (`Family` from R3). A domain may use the ones before it in that list, never the ones after. Each domain has `Actions/`, `Models/`, `Enums/`, `Data/`, `Events/`, `Support/` and `Policies/` as needed. Only Actions write. Controllers, commands and channel callbacks validate, call an action and shape the response.

**Why.** It is the owner's proven shape (the owner's earlier Laravel project): agents find code by domain, a one-way order prevents cycles, and "only Actions write" gives every write one place to test.

**Rejected.** Laravel's flat `app/Models` + fat controllers: fine for a CRUD app, but the game rules (judge, points, calendar) would end up spread over controllers. A package per domain (modules with their own service providers): too much ceremony for six small domains.

| Domain      | Owns                                                                                                                                     | Tables                                                                          | Depends on                        |
| ----------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| `Language`  | The 32-letter alphabet, Azerbaijani casing, normalization, syllabification, name sorting, ordinals                                       | none                                                                            | —                                 |
| `Content`   | Theme packs, levels, prompts, avatars, asset credits; teacher words; `ContentRepository`, `ContentValidator`, `TeacherPacks`             | `custom_words`, `hidden_words` (built-in content: files, §3)                    | Language                          |
| `Classroom` | Classes and students                                                                                                                     | `school_classes`, `students`                                                    | Language, Content (avatar keys)   |
| `Family`    | A parent's children (R3)                                                                                                                 | `children`                                                                      | Language, Content, Classroom      |
| `Play`      | Game sessions, rounds, answers, guest boards, remembered setups; round generation, the judge, points, pairing codes, the school calendar | `game_sessions`, `game_rounds`, `game_answers`, `guest_boards`, `setup_choices` | all four before it                |
| `Rankings`  | Derived scores: daily and weekly class rankings, the podium, a child's stars (R3)                                                        | none (reads Play)                                                               | Language, Classroom, Family, Play |
| `Rooms`     | Adults' rooms (B2, B3): rooms, players, room games, rounds, answers; the room code, the timetable, the judge, the points, the ranking    | `rooms`, `room_players`, `room_games`, `room_rounds`, `room_answers`            | Language, Content, `DenseRank`    |

`Family` stands before `Play` because a game may be for a child (below); it uses `Classroom` only for the display-name rule. `Rankings` stays the read-only end of the children's side: it derives everything from Play's rounds, for a class or for a child.

`Rooms` (B2, ADR 023) is a seventh domain, last in the order — `Language → Content → Classroom → Family → Play → Rankings → Rooms` — so no domain may use it. Its own rule is stricter than the order: it uses `Language`, `Content` and the pure `Rankings\Support\DenseRank`, and nothing of `Classroom`, `Family` or `Play`. A room game is never a `game_sessions` row, and "a game is for a class or for one child" keeps its two cases (§12).

The account is the starter kit's `App\Models\User` (table `users`), kept where the starter kit puts it, with its authentication code. It was the teacher and nothing else until R2; from R2 it holds the role `teacher`, `parent` or both ("Accounts and roles" below), and R2 adds three columns: `area` (string, nullable: `teacher` | `parent`, the area an account with more than one role is in), `teacher_agreed_at` and `parent_agreed_at` (timestamps, nullable: when the account accepted each role's sentence, §7). B2 adds the role `player`, a third value of `area` and one column, `player_agreed_at` (ADR 019); `name` is what a room shows of the account (§12). C3 adds `voice_agreed_at` (timestamp, nullable: when the account accepted the live voice's sentence, stamped by `JoinRoomVoice`; §7 "Agreements", §12 "Live voice", ADR 031 decision 14). Before R2 it gained five columns: `locale` (`az` | `en`), `terms_accepted_at` (§7), `phone_remote` (boolean, not null, default true: whether the teacher board offers the phone remote, §4 "Pairing"), `board_sounds` (JSON, nullable: the sounds the account chose for its boards, a `content/sounds.json` key per moment, §3 "Sounds"; hidden from the page props), `google_id` (string, nullable, unique: the OpenID `sub` of the teacher's Google account, §7 "Google sign-in", ADR 008; hidden from the page props) and `email_changed_at` (timestamp, nullable: when the teacher last changed the address in their profile, so Google sign-in never takes over an account whose new address is unconfirmed). Its `password` is nullable: a teacher who signed up with Google has none until they set one. It is not a domain: every domain may reference `User`, and `App\Enums\Role` with it.

The share card (§6) is front-end only and needs no domain.

### Tables

All public identifiers are ULIDs (`HasUlids`), so URLs and channel names cannot be enumerated. Timestamps are stored in UTC (`APP_TIMEZONE=UTC`).

**`custom_words`** (`Content\Models\CustomWord`) — a teacher's own word, played in every theme pack (§3 "Teacher words", ADR 007, ADR 009)

| Column      | Type              | Notes                                                                                                  |
| ----------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`        | ulid              | how routes address the word; its text never goes into a URL                                            |
| `user_id`   | fk users, cascade | the teacher; the word plays in all of their classes                                                    |
| `text`      | string(32)        | normalized, as written: lowercase, or capitalised as a proper noun; ≤ `elifba.custom_words.max_length` |
| `word_key`  | string(40)        | `word:{lower}` (`Az::lower`), the content key the word plays under                                     |
| `syllables` | json null         | the teacher's override; null = `Syllabifier::split()`                                                  |
| timestamps  |                   |                                                                                                        |

Unique: `(user_id, word_key)`. Azerbaijani casing is computed in PHP and stored in `word_key`, so the database enforces "one word per teacher, case-insensitively" on every engine; `AddCustomWord` and `UpdateCustomWord` still check first, under a lock on the teacher's `users` row, to answer with a message.

**`hidden_words`** (`Content\Models\HiddenWord`) — a built-in word a teacher has hidden from their own games

| Column       | Type              | Notes                                          |
| ------------ | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| `id`         | bigint            | never in a URL                                 |
| `user_id`    | fk users, cascade |                                                |
| `pack`       | string            | a built-in theme pack key                      |
| `word_key`   | string(40)        | `word:{lower}` of a built-in word of that pack |
| `created_at` | timestamp         |                                                |

Unique: `(user_id, pack, word_key)`. A row whose word a later pack version removed does nothing.

**`school_classes`** (`Classroom\Models\SchoolClass`; `class` is a reserved word)

| Column            | Type              | Notes                                                                    |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`              | ulid              |                                                                          |
| `user_id`         | fk users, cascade | the owning teacher                                                       |
| `name`            | string(40)        | e.g. "1A"; the teacher's own label                                       |
| `grade`           | tinyint null      | 1–4                                                                      |
| `learned_letters` | json null         | lowercase letters the class has learned; null = all 32 (§3)              |
| `archived_at`     | timestamp null    | hidden from pickers; deleted automatically after `elifba.retention_days` |
| timestamps        |                   |                                                                          |

**`students`** (`Classroom\Models\Student`)

| Column            | Type                       | Notes                                                             |
| ----------------- | -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`              | ulid                       |                                                                   |
| `school_class_id` | fk school_classes, cascade |                                                                   |
| `display_name`    | string(40)                 | the name as the teacher writes it, first name first (§7, ADR 018) |
| `avatar`          | string(32)                 | key from `content/avatars.json`                                   |
| `position`        | int                        | order in the class (turns, lists)                                 |
| `archived_at`     | timestamp null             | left the class; out of pickers and rankings                       |
| timestamps        |                            |                                                                   |

Unique among active students of a class: `display_name`, compared case-insensitively with Azerbaijani casing (`AddStudents`, `UpdateStudent` and `RestoreStudent` enforce it; the database cannot, because of the casing rules). Each of them locks the class row (`Classroom\Support\ClassLock`, `lockForUpdate` through the query builder) inside its transaction before it compares, so two requests cannot add the same name at once; `ReorderStudents` takes the same lock.

**`children`** (`Family\Models\Child`, R3) — a parent's own child, without a class (ADR 011, ADR 013)

| Column            | Type              | Notes                                                                         |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`              | ulid              | route parameter `{child}`                                                     |
| `user_id`         | fk users, cascade | the parent                                                                    |
| `display_name`    | string(40)        | a first name (§7); never a surname or its initial                             |
| `avatar`          | string(32)        | key from `content/avatars.json`                                               |
| `learned_letters` | json null         | lowercase letters the child has learned; null = all 32 (§3 "Learned letters") |
| `position`        | int               | the parent's order; lists are never ordered by stars                          |
| timestamps        |                   |                                                                               |

Unique among one parent's children: `display_name`, compared case-insensitively with Azerbaijani casing by `AddChild` and `UpdateChild` under a lock on the parent's `users` row (the lock `AddCustomWord` takes), which also counts the children: at most `elifba.children.max` (10). There is no `archived_at`: a child is deleted, not archived (§7). Nothing joins a child to a `students` row.

**`roles`, `model_has_roles`** and the three permission tables of `spatie/laravel-permission` (R2, ADR 010), as the package's migration creates them. `roles` holds exactly `teacher` and `parent` (guard `web`), created by the migration — and from B2 `player`, added by B2's migration whatever the switch says (ADR 019); `model_has_roles` has one row per role an account holds, with `model_type` `user` (a morph alias) and `model_id` = `users.id`. The permission tables stay empty.

**`game_sessions`** (`Play\Models\GameSession`)

| Column                                       | Type                       | Notes                                            |
| -------------------------------------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`                                         | ulid                       | also the channel name `game.{id}`                |
| `user_id`                                    | fk users, cascade          | owner; equals the class's teacher                |
| `school_class_id`                            | fk school_classes, cascade | nullable from R3: null for a home game           |
| `child_id`                                   | fk children null, cascade  | R3: the child a home game is for                 |
| `mechanic`                                   | string                     | `Mechanic` enum (§2)                             |
| `pack`                                       | string                     | theme pack key                                   |
| `pack_version`                               | int                        | the pack version the rounds were built from      |
| `level`                                      | string                     | level key                                        |
| `letter_case`                                | string                     | `upper` \| `lower`                               |
| `letters`                                    | json null                  | the letters asked for; null = every learned one  |
| `own_words`                                  | boolean, default false     | only the owner's own words (§3 "Teacher words")  |
| `seed`                                       | int                        | makes round generation reproducible in tests     |
| `status`                                     | string                     | `GameSessionStatus` (below)                      |
| `board_epoch`                                | int                        | fencing token of the board allowed to write (§4) |
| `join_code`                                  | char(6) null, unique       | remote pairing (§4); cleared when the game ends  |
| `join_code_expires_at`                       | timestamp null             |                                                  |
| `started_at`, `ended_at`, `last_activity_at` | timestamps                 |                                                  |

**`game_rounds`** (`Play\Models\GameRound`)

| Column            | Type                        | Notes                                                                              |
| ----------------- | --------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`              | ulid                        |                                                                                    |
| `game_session_id` | fk game_sessions, cascade   |                                                                                    |
| `school_class_id` | fk school_classes, cascade  | copied from the session, for ranking queries; nullable from R3 (a home game)       |
| `child_id`        | fk children null, cascade   | R3: copied from the session when the game starts, for star queries (§5)            |
| `position`        | int                         | 0-based                                                                            |
| `payload`         | json                        | the round snapshot (`RoundData` without `id`/`index`/`total`, §2)                  |
| `progress`        | json                        | item ids hit correctly so far, in order (sequence mechanics; an id may repeat, §2) |
| `misses`          | int                         | wrong hits in this round                                                           |
| `solved_at`       | timestamp null              |                                                                                    |
| `skipped_at`      | timestamp null              |                                                                                    |
| `student_id`      | fk students, null on delete | who solved it                                                                      |
| `points`          | tinyint                     | 0 until solved (§5)                                                                |
| `played_on`       | date null                   | Baku date of `solved_at` (§5)                                                      |
| `week_start`      | date null                   | Monday of `played_on`                                                              |

Indexes: `(school_class_id, played_on)`, `(school_class_id, week_start)`, `(game_session_id, position)` unique; from R3 also `(child_id, played_on)` and `(child_id, week_start)`.

**A game is for a class or for one child** (R3, ADR 011): exactly one of `game_sessions.school_class_id` and `child_id` is set, by `StartGame`, and the rounds copy the same one. The owner (`user_id`) is the class's teacher or the child's parent. In a home game `student_id` stays null on rounds and answers: the game's child is the one at the board (§4).

**`game_answers`** (`Play\Models\GameAnswer`) — every hit, correct or not.

| Column          | Type                        | Notes                                                                  |
| --------------- | --------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`            | bigint                      |                                                                        |
| `event_id`      | ulid, unique                | generated by the device that produced the hit; makes writes idempotent |
| `game_round_id` | fk game_rounds, cascade     |                                                                        |
| `student_id`    | fk students, null on delete | at the board when the hit happened                                     |
| `item_id`       | string(8)                   | id inside the round                                                    |
| `content_key`   | string                      | e.g. `letter:ə`, `word:alma`, for later "what is hard" statistics      |
| `correct`       | bool                        | judged by the server                                                   |
| `source`        | string                      | `HitSource`: `remote` \| `board`                                       |
| `board_epoch`   | int                         |                                                                        |
| `client_at`     | timestamp null              | the sender's clock, to the second, for latency diagnostics only        |
| `created_at`    | timestamp                   | server time                                                            |

**`guest_boards`** (`Play\Models\GuestBoard`) — a board on a shared computer, claimed from the teacher's phone (§4).

| Column            | Type                                  | Notes                                                      |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`              | ulid                                  |                                                            |
| `code`            | char(6) null, unique                  | pairing code while unclaimed; cleared on claim             |
| `code_expires_at` | timestamp null                        |                                                            |
| `secret_hash`     | string                                | hash of the secret that only the board's browser tab holds |
| `user_id`         | fk users null, cascade                | the teacher who claimed it                                 |
| `game_session_id` | fk game_sessions null, null on delete | the game it shows now                                      |
| `claimed_at`      | timestamp null                        |                                                            |
| `expires_at`      | timestamp                             | creation + `elifba.guest_board_hours` (12)                 |
| `closed_at`       | timestamp null                        |                                                            |
| timestamps        |                                       |                                                            |

A guest board is **active** while it is neither closed nor past `expires_at`; an unclaimed one also needs its code to be valid, so it dies with the code (`GuestBoard::isActive()`, scope `active`). The secret is 32 random bytes (URL-safe base64, 43 characters); `secret_hash` is its SHA-256 — a fast hash is enough for a random secret of that size — compared with `hash_equals`. `secret_hash` is hidden from every serialization.

**`setup_choices`** (`Play\Models\SetupChoice`) — what a class or a child last chose on «Yeni oyun» (§2 "Remembered setup")

| Column            | Type                                    | Notes                                                                    |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`              | bigint                                  | never in a URL                                                           |
| `school_class_id` | fk school_classes null, cascade, unique | the class, or null                                                       |
| `child_id`        | fk children null, cascade, unique       | the child, or null; exactly one of the two is set                        |
| `mode`            | string                                  | `SetupMode`: `easy` \| `normal`                                          |
| `levels`          | json null                               | unit → the level key last started                                        |
| `letter_cases`    | json null                               | unit → `upper` \| `lower` last started                                   |
| `letters`         | json null                               | the letters last asked for at the letter level; null = every learned one |
| `own_words`       | boolean null                            | the last «Yalnız mənim sözlərim» where it applied; null = never chosen   |
| timestamps        |                                         |                                                                          |

A row holds no name and nothing about a child (ADR 013): a mode, level keys, cases, letters and a flag. It belongs to its class or child and goes with it (cascade); its owner is theirs, so the setup routes' scoped binding guards it and no policy asks for a role.

**The five tables of `Rooms`** (B2, B3; ADRs 020–022) — six from T2, with `room_taunts` (ADR 029) — reference `users` and each other only — never a class, a student, a child or a game. No column holds a name or anything a person typed. Moments that scoring reads are Unix milliseconds (`bigint`), because engines differ in sub-second timestamps.

**`rooms`** (`Rooms\Models\Room`, B2)

| Column             | Type                 | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------ | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`               | ulid                 | route parameter `{room}`; also the channel name `room.{id}`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `user_id`          | fk users, cascade    | the host: the account that opened the room                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `code`             | char(6) null, unique | the room code (`RoomCode`, §12); cleared when the room closes                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `locked_at`        | timestamp null       | set: no new player comes in                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `revision`         | int, default 0       | raised by every action that changes what the room sees; the signal carries it (§12)                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `last_activity_at` | timestamp            | a join, a game started, an answer; idle for `elifba.rooms.idle_hours` → closed                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `closed_at`        | timestamp null       | final                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `setup_mechanic`   | string(32) null      | G2: the host's choice of the next game (`RoomMechanic`), §12 "Games first"                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `setup_difficulty` | tinyint null         | G2: its difficulty, 1–4                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `setup_rounds`     | tinyint null         | G2: its rounds, inside `elifba.rooms.rounds`; all three null in a room before G2                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `taunts`           | bool, default true   | T2: the host's switch for taunts (`SetRoomTaunts`, §12 "Taunts (T2)", ADR 029)                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `screen_seen_ms`   | bigint null          | when the big screen last read the state while shown, Unix ms (`RecordScreenRead`); `room.screen` is derived from it (§12 "A room without a screen")                                                                                                  |
| `voice_at`         | timestamp null       | C3: the room's voice is on since this moment — set by `OpenRoom` at the opening while the instance's voice is on (ADR 031 as amended 2026-11-19) and by `SetRoomVoice`; null: off (cleared by `SetRoomVoice` and a close; §12 "Live voice", ADR 031) |
| `voice_epoch`      | int, default 0       | C3: in the voice room's name `{id}.{voice_epoch}`; raised to close the voice room for everyone (the voice off, a removal, a close, the sweep)                                                                                                        |
| timestamps         |                      | `created_at` is what the retention period counts from                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**`room_players`** (`Rooms\Models\RoomPlayer`, B2) — an account in a room

| Column              | Type                          | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`                | ulid                          | route parameter `{member}`; the only id of a player a device ever gets                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `room_id`           | fk rooms, cascade             |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `user_id`           | fk users null, null on delete | the account; null = the account is gone or took its name out: the row is «Oyunçu» without a name                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `joined_at`         | timestamp                     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `left_at`           | timestamp null                | left; may come back with the code and keeps the row                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `removed_at`        | timestamp null                | removed by the host: no way back, out of the ranking                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `seen_ms`           | bigint null                   | when the player's own room page last read the state of a running game, Unix ms (`RecordRoomRead`, at most once a round, never for the big screen); never served (2026-11-18, §12)                                                                                                                |
| `voice_admitted_at` | timestamp null                | C3: let into the room's voice — the host's row by `OpenRoom`, a player who comes in before a game by `JoinRoom` (ADR 031 as amended 2026-11-19), and by the host (`SetRoomVoice` on, `SetRoomPlayerVoice`); null: waiting. Never served; the state says the standing it gives (§12 "Live voice") |
| `voice_muted_at`    | timestamp null                | C3: muted by the host until the host lifts it (`SetRoomPlayerVoice`); never served                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| timestamps          |                               |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Unique: `(room_id, user_id)`. A player is **present** while `user_id` is set and `left_at` and `removed_at` are null. A round **waits for** a present player who answered it or whose page has read the state since the round before it closed (`seen_ms`; §12 "The race").

**`room_games`** (`Rooms\Models\RoomGame`, B3) — one game played in a room; a room plays several, one at a time

| Column            | Type              | Notes                                                                                   |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| `room_id`         | fk rooms, cascade |                                                                                         |
| `position`        | int               | order in the room; unique with `room_id`                                                |
| `mechanic`        | string            | `Content\Enums\RoomMechanic`                                                            |
| `difficulty`      | tinyint           | 1–4; with `mechanic`, the level of `content/rooms/levels.json` (B3, below)              |
| `content_version` | int               | the version of the room content the rounds were built from                              |
| `seed`            | int               | makes round generation reproducible in tests                                            |
| `reveal_ms`       | int               | copied from `elifba.rooms.reveal_ms` at the start, so a live game keeps its timetable   |
| `started_ms`      | bigint            | when round 0 opens: the start plus `elifba.rooms.countdown_ms`                          |
| `ended_ms`        | bigint null       | a fact: the host ended the game, or the room closed under it; null = the timetable ends |
| `created_at`      | timestamp         |                                                                                         |

**`room_rounds`** (`Rooms\Models\RoomRound`, B3)

| Column         | Type                   | Notes                                                                      |
| -------------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `id`           | ulid                   |                                                                            |
| `room_game_id` | fk room_games, cascade |                                                                            |
| `position`     | int                    | 0-based; unique with `room_game_id`                                        |
| `payload`      | json                   | what players may see: the study material and the question (§12)            |
| `solution`     | json                   | what the judge reads; hidden from every serialization                      |
| `study_ms`     | int                    | 0 except in memory games                                                   |
| `answer_ms`    | int                    | the time to answer                                                         |
| `closed_ms`    | bigint null            | a fact: every present player had answered before the bell; null = the bell |

**`room_answers`** (`Rooms\Models\RoomAnswer`, B3) — one per player per round

| Column           | Type                     | Notes                                                  |
| ---------------- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`             | bigint                   |                                                        |

| `room_player_id` | fk room_players, cascade |                                                        |

| `correct`        | bool                     | judged by the server (`RoomJudge`)                     |
| `elapsed_ms`     | int                      | the server's receipt − the moment the question opened  |

Unique: `(room_round_id, room_player_id)`. The current round, its phase, the ranking and whether a game is over are derived (`RoomTimetable`, `RoomRanking`), never stored.

**`room_taunts`** (`Rooms\Models\RoomTaunt`, T2, ADR 029) — a ready-made taunt one player threw at another during a game

| Column             | Type                                 | Notes                                                                                            |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `id`               | ulid                                 |                                                                                                  |
| `event_id`         | ulid, unique                         | made by the device; makes the write idempotent                                                   |
| `room_game_id`     | fk room_games, cascade               |                                                                                                  |
| `room_player_id`   | fk room_players, cascade             | the thrower, who paid `RoomPointsRule::TAUNT_COST` (1)                                           |
| `target_player_id` | fk room_players null, null on delete | whom it was aimed at; emptied by `PruneOldRooms` a day after the throw (`elifba.rooms.taunts.*`) |
| `taunt`            | string(32)                           | a key of `content/rooms/taunts.json`; never text                                                 |
| `sent_ms`          | bigint                               | the server's moment of the throw (`RoomClock`); from it the close at which the cost counts       |

Index: `(room_game_id, room_player_id)`. A game's points are its answers' points over closed rounds less the taunts counted at those closes, and the balance a player may spend is derived the same way (§12 "Taunts (T2)"); neither is stored.

### Status lifecycle

`GameSessionStatus::allowedTransitions()`:

```
live ──▶ paused ──▶ live
live | paused ──▶ finished    (last round solved or skipped, or the teacher ends it)
live | paused ──▶ abandoned   (no activity for elifba.abandon_after_hours, by the scheduler)
finished, abandoned: final
```

A session is created `live` by `StartGame`. Only `TransitionGameSession` changes the status; it locks the game row, sets `ended_at` and clears the join code when the game ends, and dispatches `GameSessionTransitioned` after commit (the event implements `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit`). Asking for the status a game already has changes nothing and dispatches nothing, so a repeated pause or end is harmless; any other change outside the diagram throws `Play\Exceptions\InvalidTransition`. The current round is derived (the first round neither solved nor skipped), never stored. `game_sessions` has no `created_at`/`updated_at`: `started_at`, `ended_at` and `last_activity_at` (set by the start, every claim, every applied sync event and every status change) say what happened. `seed` fits a signed 32-bit integer, the same on every engine.

### Actions

| Domain    | Actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| --------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Content   | `AddCustomWord`, `UpdateCustomWord`, `DeleteCustomWord`, `HideWord`, `ShowWord` (teacher words, §3); `WriteContentCache`, `DeleteContentCache`, `WriteCredits` (files)                                                                                                                                          |
| Classroom | `CreateClass`, `UpdateClass`, `ArchiveClass`, `RestoreClass`, `DeleteClass`, `UpdateLearnedLetters`, `AddStudents` (pasted list), `UpdateStudent`, `ReorderStudents`, `ArchiveStudent`, `RestoreStudent`, `DeleteStudent`, `PruneArchived`                                                                      |
| Family    | `AddChild`, `UpdateChild`, `UpdateChildLetters`, `ReorderChildren`, `DeleteChild` (R3)                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Account   | `App\Actions\Roles\GrantRole`, `SwitchArea` (R2), next to the kit's `App\Actions\Fortify\*` and `App\Actions\Settings\UpdateLocale`, `UpdatePassword`, `LogOutOtherDevices` (ADR 017)                                                                                                                           |
| Play      | `StartGame`, `ChangeRoundCount`, `ReplayGame`, `ClaimBoard`, `IssueJoinCode`, `RecordBoardEvents`, `TransitionGameSession`, `AbandonStaleGames`, `PruneOldGames`, `OpenGuestBoard`, `ClaimGuestBoard`, `ShowGameOnGuestBoard`, `CloseGuestBoard`, `PruneGuestBoards`, `UpdatePlaySettings`, `RememberSetupMode` |
| Rooms     | B2: `OpenRoom`, `JoinRoom`, `LeaveRoom`, `LockRoom`, `RemoveRoomPlayer`, `CloseRoom`, `CloseIdleRooms`, `DeleteRoom`, `ForgetRoomPlayer`, `PruneOldRooms`; B3: `StartRoomGame`, `EndRoomGame`, `RecordRoomAnswer` (§12)                                                                                         |

Classroom actions take the `Classroom\Data` objects (`ClassData`, `StudentData`, `AddStudentsData`, `LearnedLettersData`, `ReorderStudentsData`; each with `rules()` and `validate()`, normalizing names before they are checked) and report a conflict the teacher can fix (a taken name, a restore blocked by a name, an order that is not exactly the class's active students) as a `ValidationException` on the field, with a translated message that never contains the name. The paste preview is `Classroom\Support\NameListParser` (§7); it writes nothing.

The teacher-word actions take `Content\Data\CustomWordData` (`pack`, `text`, `syllables`) and `HiddenWordData` (`pack`, `word`), each with `prepare()` (normalizing with `Az::normalize`), `rules()` and `validate()`, and report the same way: a `ValidationException` on `text` or `syllables` with a `words.errors.*` message that never contains the word (§3 "Teacher words").

The Family actions (R3) take `Family\Data\ChildData` (`display_name`, `avatar`), `ChildLettersData` and `ReorderChildrenData`, and report a taken name or the limit as a `ValidationException` on the field (`children.errors.taken`, `children.errors.too_many`), never with the name. `AddChild` gives the next avatar of `content/avatars.json` not yet used among the parent's children when none is sent. `DeleteChild` deletes the row; the child's games, rounds and answers go by cascade.

Events dispatched after commit: `GameSessionTransitioned` (its listener `Play\Listeners\BroadcastSessionStatus`, registered in `AppServiceProvider`, broadcasts `session.status` on the game channel so a device that is still connected learns the status; a failed broadcast is reported with ids only and never fails the change, §4), `RoundSolved` (no listener in v1; it is the hook for future statistics). Both carry ids only; from R3 `RoundSolved` carries the class id or the child id, the other null.

Scheduled (one `scheduler` process, §10; the commands are in `routes/console.php`): `AbandonStaleGames` (`play:abandon-stale`; it checks the idle time again under the row lock, so a board that synced a moment ago keeps its game) and `PruneGuestBoards` (`play:prune-guest-boards`: closed boards, boards past `expires_at` and boards never claimed before their code expired; games are untouched) hourly; `PruneOldGames` (`play:prune-old-games`, games whose `started_at` is older than `elifba.retention_days`, with their rounds and answers by cascade) and `PruneArchived` daily; expired join codes are simply ignored (no job), and `TransitionGameSession` clears the code of a game that ends. From B2: `CloseIdleRooms` (`rooms:close-idle`) hourly and `PruneOldRooms` (`rooms:prune`, rooms created longer than `elifba.retention_days` ago, with everything under them) daily; nothing is scheduled for a round or a game of a room, whose timetable is derived (§12).

### Accounts and roles

**Decision** (R2, ADR 010). An account holds the role `teacher`, `parent` or both. The roles come from `spatie/laravel-permission` (`HasRoles` on `User`), as roles only: no permission is defined, `register_permission_check_method` is off and teams are off. `App\Enums\Role` is the one list of names; code passes the enum.

A role opens an **area**; ownership still decides rows. Every authenticated route is in one of four groups:

| Group   | Middleware                                                | Routes                                                                                                         |
| ------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Teacher | `auth`, `role.chosen`, `verified`, `role:teacher`         | classes, students, class games (`/classes/{class}/games…`), rankings, the podium, teacher words                |
| Parent  | `auth`, `role.chosen`, `verified`, `role:parent`          | children, home games (`/children/{child}/games…`) (R3)                                                         |
| Shared  | `auth`, `role.chosen`, `verified`, `role:teacher\|parent` | the board page, the join code, the remote, `/pult`, the next game of a guest board                             |
| Account | `auth` (and what each route has today)                    | `/role`, `/start`, `/area`, the profile; security, appearance, play and roles settings also take `role.chosen` |

From B2 (ADR 019) there is a fifth group, and the play settings move: **Player** — `auth`, `role.chosen`, `verified`, `role:player`, `rooms.enabled` — holds every room route (`/rooms…`, `/otaq…`, `routes/rooms.php`) and nothing else — from I2 (ADR 033) every one but the invitation's door, `GET` and `POST /oyun/qeydiyyat`, a guest route behind `rooms.enabled` that reads no room (§12 "The invitation link (I2)"); Settings → «Oyun» (`play-settings.*`) joins the shared group, since only a teacher or a parent has a board. The shared group stays `role:teacher|parent`: the player role opens no board, remote or `/pult`. While `elifba.rooms.enabled` is off the role is not offered, not granted and not listed among an account's roles, and the group answers 404 (§12 "The area and the switch").

The board endpoints a guest board's bearer may call (`auth:board,web`: claim, state, sync, status, closing a board) and the public routes name no role: a bearer is not an account, and `GameSessionPolicy::play` and `GuestBoardPolicy` decide (§4).

- **`role`** is the package's `RoleMiddleware`, which routes write as `Role::middleware(Role::Teacher)` (`role:teacher`; `role:teacher|parent` for the shared group), never as a string. `User::roleList()` gives the roles an account holds in the enum's order, `User::area()` the area it is in. A wrong area is 403; the exception handler turns a page visit (GET, not JSON) into a redirect to `/start` — from B2, a page of the player area into a redirect to Settings → «Rollar» with «Oyunçu» preselected, keeping the page to come back to (§12 "The area and the switch"). Route binding resolves first, so another account's id is a 404 whatever the role.
- **`role.chosen`** (`App\Http\Middleware\EnsureRoleChosen`) sends an account with no role to `GET /role` (from B2, from a page of the player area with «Oyunçu» preselected and the page kept); a JSON or non-GET request gets 409 `{error: "role_required"}`. A guest board's bearer passes.
- **`GET /role`, `POST /role`** (`RoleController`, page `auth/choose-role`, `auth` only — before e-mail confirmation): «Müəllim» or «Valideyn» — from B2 also «Oyunçu» while the rooms area is on (`Role::available()`) — each with its sentence (§7). `GrantRole` takes `App\Data\RoleChoiceData` (`role`, `agreement` accepted), gives the role, stamps `{role}_agreed_at` and sets `area`. An account that already has a role is sent on to `/start`.
- **`GET /start`** (`StartController`): no role → `/role`; one role → its home (`/classes`, `/children`, from B2 `/rooms`); more → the home of `users.area`. Fortify's `home`, `redirectUsersTo`, the `/dashboard` redirect, the Google callback and the logo all point here.
- **The second role**: Settings → «Rollar» (`GET /settings/roles`, `POST /settings/roles` → `GrantRole` again). Roles are never removed.
- **The switch**: `PUT /area` (`AreaController` → `SwitchArea`; `App\Data\AreaData`: `area` must be a role the account holds) stores `users.area` and redirects to that area's home. Only an account with more than one role sees it, in the user menu.
- **Pages** get `auth.roles` and `auth.area` as shared props: `area` is the route's own area inside the teacher, parent or (B2) player group, otherwise `users.area` (or the one role) — except that a page of the shared group is never in the player area: it shows the first of the account's teacher and parent areas. The shell draws one navigation per area from one definition (`components/app/nav-areas.ts`).
- **Registration** creates an account with no role, by the form and by Google alike; `terms` there is the privacy note only (§7). From I2 (ADR 033) one way gives a role in the same step: the invitation's door, and the Google completion page while a room invitation is under way, give the player role with its own sentence beside the note's, through `GrantRole` (§12 "The invitation link (I2)"); `/register` and its form do not change.
- **Existing accounts**: the R2 migration makes every one a teacher (`teacher_agreed_at` = `terms_accepted_at`, `area` = `teacher`), with the query builder.
- **Factory**: `User::factory()->create()` is a verified teacher with the role, as every test before R2 assumes; `parent()`, `teacherAndParent()` and `withoutRole()` are states. A created account gets the roles whose agreement it has, the invariant `GrantRole` keeps. `tests/TestCase` forgets the package's cache before every test, as its testing guide says.

**Why.** Areas by middleware make the role a property of the route, which a test can enumerate; rows by ownership keep isolation where it already is and tested. One `/role` page and one `GrantRole` serve registration by form, registration with Google, an account without a role and the second role.

**Rejected.** A role system of our own; the role as a registration field; role checks in policies; permissions; one navigation for both areas; the area in the session (ADR 010 has the reasons).

`tests/Feature/Auth/RoleAreasTest.php` enumerates every route with `auth` and fails for one that is in no group — five groups from B2.

---

## 2. Game model

**Decision.** A game is **mechanic × theme pack × level**:

| Part           | Is                                                                                                                    | Where                                                                                                  | Adding one needs                   |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------- |
| **Mechanic**   | The rules of a round: what is asked, how many hits solve it, in which order                                           | Code: a round builder in `Play\Support\Mechanics\*` and a Phaser scene in `resources/js/game/scenes/*` | Code                               |
| **Theme pack** | The look and the vocabulary: item style, backdrop, palette, effect, sounds, words with pictures, sentences            | Data: `content/packs/{key}.json` + `content/assets/{key}/`                                             | A folder and a JSON file — no code |
| **Level**      | Which unit (letter, syllable, word, sentence; picture, number from R5), how hard, how many items, which mechanics fit | Data: `content/levels.json`                                                                            | A JSON entry — no code             |

The server generates all rounds of a game when it starts, stores each as a snapshot (`game_rounds.payload`) and sends the same snapshots to the board and the phone. Neither client invents a round.

**Why.** Six themes × five mechanics × six levels give dozens of valid games; written as separate games that is unmaintainable, written as data it is one engine. Snapshots keep old games, answers and rankings valid after a pack changes, make the board and phone agree by construction, and let a reloaded board resume exactly where it was.

**Rejected.** A theme as code (one Phaser game per theme): every new theme would need a developer, and the owner wants teachers to contribute content by pull request. Generating rounds on the board: the server could not judge answers or resume a game, and the phone would need the same generator.

### Mechanics (code)

`Content\Enums\Mechanic` (with `units()`, `supports()` and `builds()`). The enum lives in Content, not Play: `levels.json` and `prompts.json` name mechanics and `ContentValidator` must check them, and Content may not use Play (§1). The rules of each mechanic stay code in Play (`Play\Support\Mechanics\*`) and in the scenes.

| Key              | UI name (az)                                          | Units                  | Round                                                                                                       | Solution                  |
| ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `find`           | Tap! («find!»; "Şarı partlat" with the balloons pack) | letter, syllable, word | A prompt names a target; 3–6 items float on the board, one is the target                                    | `any`, one item           |
| `pop`            | Oxu və vur («read and tap»; for beginners)            | letter, syllable, word | No target: the level's items float on the board, the child reads any one aloud and hits it; all are right   | `any`, every item         |
| `first_letter`   | İlk hərfi tap! («first letter»)                       | letter                 | The prompt shows a picture and asks which letter its word starts with; 4 letters float on the board         | `any`, one item           |
| `build_syllable` | Heca qur                                              | syllable               | Build the shown syllable from letters into slots (train wagons in the train pack)                           | `sequence`, its letters   |
| `build_word`     | Sözü yığ                                              | word                   | A picture; assemble the word (2+ syllables) from its syllables (+1 decoy)                                   | `sequence`, its syllables |
| `read_word`      | Oxu və şəkli tap! («read and match»)                  | word                   | The prompt writes a word without reading it aloud; 4 picture tiles stand on the board, one matches the word | `any`, one item           |
| `build_sentence` | Cümlə qur                                             | sentence               | Put 2–4 words (the level decides) in order, +1 decoy; the final punctuation stays fixed after the last slot | `sequence`, its words     |
| `count`          | Say («count»; R5)                                     | number                 | The prompt shows one picture N times; the items are numbers, one of them N                                  | `any`, one item           |

From R5 `find` and `pop` also take the units `picture` and `number` ("Units" below); at those levels the setup form names `pop` «De və vur» («say and tap»), since nothing is read.

**Units.** `Content\Enums\Unit` says what an item of a level is (ADR 012):

| Unit       | An item is                         | `text`                         | `image`     | `content_key`      | `reads()` |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ----------- | ------------------ | --------- |
| `letter`   | a letter                           | the letter, cased              | null        | `letter:{lower}`   | yes       |
| `syllable` | a syllable of the pack's words     | the syllable, cased            | null        | `syllable:{lower}` | yes       |
| `word`     | a word of the pack                 | the word, cased                | null        | `word:{lower}`     | yes       |
| `sentence` | a sentence's words (tiles)         | each word                      | null        | `word:{lower}`     | yes       |
| `picture`  | a word of the pack, as its picture | the word as the pack writes it | its picture | `word:{lower}`     | no (R5)   |
| `number`   | a number of the level's range      | its digits ("7", "10")         | null        | `number:{n}`       | no (R5)   |

`Unit::reads()` separates the reading units from the two for children who cannot read yet. Learned letters, the letter case, chosen `letters` and `own_words` apply only to a reading unit; a picture or number level ignores them all, so a class that has learned three letters can still play it. The board draws a picture item as the picture alone; its `text` is for the phone and for "item texts are unique".

Rules shared by all mechanics:

- Item texts are unique within a round, so a tap on the phone names exactly one item on the board.
- In a `sequence` round an item is a reusable **tile**: it stays on the board and a copy flies into the slot, so a solution may name the same item more than once: with the tile `nə` as item `a`, the word `nənə` is `["a", "a"]`; the syllable `nən` uses the `n` tile twice. Common first words (`ba-ba`, `nə-nə`, `də-də`) need this. The judge expects `solution.item_ids[progress.length]`. In an `any` round (`find`, `pop`) an item hit correctly bursts and is gone, and a wrong item wobbles and stays. Each wrong hit counts as a miss. A `pop` round has no wrong item: every item is in its solution, so it is never missed and is solved when every item is gone.
- Decoys for letters come first from the teacher's chosen letters, when given (so a round shows only chosen letters whenever at least the level's `items` are chosen), then from the letter's confusables in `content/alphabet.json` (e/ə, i/ı, o/ö, u/ü, s/ş, c/ç, g/ğ…) among the learned letters; for syllables and words, from the same pack and level; for a sentence's words, from the level's other sentences in the same pack ("The build builders" below).
- At a reading level only content whose letters are all in the learned letters — the class's, or the child's in a home game (§3) — is used; a game whose level has too little such content cannot start (`GameSetupData::rules()` says why). Picture and number levels do not read them.
- The same target never appears twice in a row.

### Item styles and effects (code, chosen by data)

A pack picks its look from closed lists implemented in `resources/js/game/styles/`: item style `balloon` | `wagon` | `card` | `bubble` | `animal` | `fruit` | `picture` (R5) | `dino`; slot style `wagon` | `frame`; effect `burst` | `sparkle`. A new style is code; a new theme that reuses the styles is data only.

**`picture`** (R5, ADR 012; `styles/picture.ts`; restyled for ADR 014's art in its session 3) is a white tile standing on a ledge of a palette colour — the tile's thickness, darker at its foot — with a soft shadow under it in the colour's deep shade, a face that fades to a tint of the colour at the bottom and a thin rim in that tint, drawn once per colour and size into a canvas texture. The item's picture fills it as far as the corners allow (0.84 of the side; Fluent's pictures fill their square) over a soft oval of shade that grounds it, since Fluent draws no shadow of its own; an item without a picture shows its text. The tile pops in and its picture follows with a bounce; while it waits the picture breathes — rising a little and swelling as its shade shrinks — each tile out of step with the others; a miss wobbles the tile gently (±4°) while the picture shakes its head; a correct hit makes the picture hop as the round's effect takes the tile (`burst`: the tile swells for 90 ms; `sparkle`: it lifts and fades whole in about a second, as every item does); when the round is over the tiles float away. A tile is at most 500 px, 0.86 of its column. `scenes/count.ts` draws the pictures it asks about in the same tile (`picture.draw()`), in the pack's first colour, so a counting round and a picture round look alike. `stage.ts` draws every round of the `picture` unit in it, whatever `pack.style.item` says — a picture item on a balloon or a wagon would hide the picture — and a pack made for pictures may name it as its own style (`colors`). It has no figures (`hasFigures()` is false), and its layout gives fewer, larger tiles: a picture level shows 2–4.

The code (A6, A8.2, A8.3): one registry, `styles/registry.ts`, maps each key to its implementation — `ITEM_STYLES` (`balloon.ts`, `wagon.ts`, `card.ts`, `bubble.ts`, `animal.ts`, `fruit.ts`, `dino.ts`), `SLOT_STYLES` (`slot-wagon.ts`, `slot-frame.ts`) and `EFFECTS` (`burst.ts`, `sparkle.ts`); a scene takes the item style from `pack.style.item`, the slot style from `pack.style.slot` (both through `stage.ts`) and the effect from `round.effect`, and asks the registry, never a style module directly.
