With the `voice` profile (C2; `COMPOSE_PROFILES=voice` in `.env`, or `ELIFBA_SMOKE_VOICE=1`, which names the profile for the run and takes the address 127.0.0.1 unless `.env` has one) it also checks: `voice` healthy; only 7882/udp and 7881/tcp published beside the proxy's ports; `/rtc/v1` through Caddy answering as the voice server does (401 to a WebSocket without a ticket), the older `/rtc` and `/rtc/validate` with `publish=` answered 404 by Caddy (C5), and a Twirp path from outside not answered by it (the app's 404) — nor any path that only cleans to `/rtc/v1` (`--path-as-is`, with a valid `roomList` token; C5); `app` reaching `voice:7880` inside; the config the server runs with holding `use_external_ip: false`, `node_ip` = the address, a `stun_servers` list of that address only, `auto_create` and `enable_remote_unmute` false, `logging.level: warn` and `enabled_codecs` Opus and RED only (C5), and no `webhook`, `turn`, `redis` or tracing; **the STUN proof** — `app` makes a voice room through the API with a token minted from the key and the secret (curl and openssl, as `handshake()` does for Reverb), for audio codecs only (C5), a ticket opens `/rtc/v1` through Caddy with the smallest join request (101), and the join response offers no ICE server but `stun:{address}:7882` and names neither Twilio nor Google; a ticket for a room the app did not make is refused (404); neither Caddy's nor the voice server's log holds the ticket, and the voice server's holds nothing of the join — no browser, phone model or SDP (C5); **the voice server stopped** (C5): a page's join and its check through Caddy fail (502), Caddy logs the failure with the path, without the ticket, the join request, the browser or the page it came from, and with the address cut to /16, and the server comes back healthy; and, on a Docker network created `--internal` (checked to have no route out), the service refusing to start without its address and healthy with it, with no lookup of its own. Without the profile it also checks that `/rtc/v1` with a ticket's place in its query is Caddy's 404 and reaches no log (C5). `ELIFBA_SMOKE_BUILD=0` starts the image already built: CI's `docker` job runs the smoke without the profile, then with it, building once.

No queue worker and no Redis in v1: broadcasts are `ShouldBroadcastNow`, mail is sent synchronously, the share card is made in the browser. `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` in every environment, so anything a package queues still runs instead of waiting for a worker that does not exist. The starter kit's `composer dev` entry for `queue:listen` is removed (A2, `DevCommands::except('queue')`); Reverb is in `composer dev` since A7. Add a `queue` service the day something needs one; add Redis only when Reverb has to scale across servers.

**Installing Elifba.** A teacher can install Elifba from Chrome as an app with its own window and icon (desktop, Android). Nothing in it is offline: it is the same site in a window.

- **Manifest.** `GET /manifest.webmanifest` (`ManifestController`, `App\Support\WebApp::manifest()`): `id` `/` (the app keeps its identity if the start page moves), `name` and `short_name` `app.name`, `description` `welcome.meta.description`, `lang` APP_LOCALE (az), `start_url` `/start` (where an account lands by role, §1 "Accounts and roles"; a guest is sent on to the login), `scope` `/`, `display` `standalone`, `theme_color` the logo's green `#15803D`, `background_color` `#FFFFFF` (the light theme's, behind the icon while the app starts), `categories` `education`, and the icons. Chrome fetches the manifest without cookies, so its text is in APP_LOCALE and the route runs outside the `web` middleware group: no session (one per fetch would only fill the sessions table), no cookie, no CSRF. It answers `application/manifest+json` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` and an ETag.
- **Head.** Every page (`app.blade.php`) links the manifest, `apple-touch-icon.png`, `<meta name="theme-color">` (the same green) and `apple-mobile-web-app-title` (the app's name, not the page title, under the icon on an iPhone), and carries the inline script that catches Chrome's install offer for the banner (frontend.md rule 11).
- **Icons.** `public/icons/elifba-192.png` and `elifba-512.png` (`purpose: any`: `tile.svg` as it is, the rounded tile), `public/icons/elifba-maskable-512.png` (`maskable`: full-bleed green, the mark at 80 % so a launcher's circle never cuts it) and `public/apple-touch-icon.png` (180×180, full-bleed: iOS rounds it, and asks for this path even when a page names none). `php scripts/app-icons/generate.php` draws them from `resources/images/brand/tile.svg` with headless Chrome (no dependencies; `CHROME` names the binary); run it again whenever the mark changes. `WebAppTest` checks the head, the manifest and that every icon is a PNG of the size it states.
- **No service worker.** Chrome no longer asks for one: installing from the menu stopped needing a service worker with a `fetch` handler in Chrome 108 (Android) and 112 (desktop), Chromium's installability check (`InstallableParams`) has no service-worker criterion any more, and Chrome 157 fires `beforeinstallprompt` for Elifba with the manifest alone (checked 2026-11-10 over the DevTools protocol: `Page.getInstallabilityErrors` empty). So Elifba registers none: nothing sits between the page and the network, nothing is cached on a shared school computer, a deploy is never hidden behind a stale copy, and Vite's HMR is untouched. If one is ever needed (offline play, push), it is hand-written (no Workbox, no CDN), network-only unless this section says otherwise, and never caches HTML, Inertia JSON or anything with class or student data (§7); that change starts here.
- Safari and Firefox have no `beforeinstallprompt`; their own menus add Elifba (Safari's Add to Home Screen or Add to Dock reads the manifest and the touch icon).

**Database.** PostgreSQL 17 in production; SQLite in development and tests. Queries stay on the query builder with no engine-specific SQL (dates are computed in PHP, §5; names are sorted in PHP, §9). CI runs the Pest suite on both (§11). A self-hosting school may run SQLite in production — the same tests cover it.

**Why.** One domain for the app and Reverb removes CORS and mixed-content problems and lets phones reach the socket through port 443. PostgreSQL is the safer engine for a public instance with many classrooms writing at once (row locks, no single-writer file lock, `pg_dump`, a path to more servers). SQLite keeps `composer setup` free of services for contributors and makes tests fast.

**Rejected.** SQLite in production for the public instance: a single writer and a single host; moving data off it later is the hard migration this avoids. MySQL: no advantage here over PostgreSQL. A managed platform (Forge/Vapor/Cloud): the repository must stay self-hostable with plain `docker compose up`.

---

## 11. Repository layout, CI gate and rules for agents

### Layout

```
app/
├── Domain/
│   ├── Language/   Support/{Alphabet,Az,Syllabifier,AzCollator,Ordinal,DateLabel,Plural}.php
│   ├── Content/    Actions/{WriteContentCache,DeleteContentCache,WriteCredits,AddCustomWord,UpdateCustomWord,DeleteCustomWord,HideWord,ShowWord}.php Data/ Enums/{Unit,Mechanic,ItemStyle,SlotStyle,Effect,Sound,License,WordProblem}.php
│   │               Models/{CustomWord,HiddenWord}.php Policies/CustomWordPolicy.php
│   │               Support/{Avatars,ContentAssets,ContentRepository,ContentValidator,CreditsDocument}.php Console/ (content:validate, content:cache, content:clear, content:credits)
│   ├── Classroom/  Actions/ Models/ Data/ Enums/ Policies/ Support/{NameListParser,DisplayName,ClassLock}.php
│   ├── Family/     (R3) Actions/{AddChild,UpdateChild,UpdateChildLetters,ReorderChildren,DeleteChild}.php Models/Child.php Data/{ChildData,ChildLettersData,ReorderChildrenData}.php Policies/ChildPolicy.php
│   ├── Play/       Actions/ Models/ Enums/ Data/ Events/ Exceptions/ Listeners/ Policies/ Support/{RoundJudge,PointsRule,SchoolCalendar,Playable,StartableGame,BoardState,BoardProps,PairingCode,GuestBoardGuard,GameChannel,Requester,Mechanics/*}.php
│   ├── Rankings/   Enums/Period.php Data/{PeriodData,RankedStudentData,PodiumPlaceData,ShareCardData}.php Support/{ClassRanking,DenseRank,Podium}.php (R3: Data/ChildStarsData.php, Support/ChildStars.php)
│   └── Rooms/      (B2, B3; §12) Actions/ Models/{Room,RoomPlayer,RoomGame,RoomRound,RoomAnswer}.php Data/ Events/RoomChanged.php Policies/RoomPolicy.php
│                   Support/{RoomCode,RoomChannel,RoomClock,RoomTimetable,RoomState,RoomJudge,RoomPointsRule,RoomRanking,RoomRoundClose,RoomSeed,RoomRandomEngine,RoomPlayable,Mechanics/*}.php
├── Http/Controllers/  (thin: validate → action → Inertia/JSON), Http/Controllers/Auth/ (Google sign-in), Http/Middleware/EnsureGoogleSignInIsEnabled.php, Exceptions/GoogleSignInRefused.php
├── Models/User.php    (the account, from the starter kit; `HasRoles` from R2), Models/Concerns/BelongsToOwner.php (scoped binding, §7; `BelongsToTeacher` until R3)
├── Enums/Role.php, Actions/Roles/{GrantRole,SwitchArea}.php, Data/{RoleChoiceData,AreaData}.php, Http/Middleware/EnsureRoleChosen.php  (R2, §1 "Accounts and roles")
├── Providers/ContentServiceProvider.php  (the repository, the content:* commands, optimize hooks)
└── Support/Translations.php, GoogleSignIn.php, GoogleProfile.php, PasswordConfirmation.php (§7 "Google sign-in"), WebApp.php (the manifest, §10 "Installing Elifba")
config/elifba.php      (timezone, locales, retention_days, pairing_code_ttl_minutes, guest_board_hours, abandon_after_hours, rounds, custom_words.max_length, custom_words.max_per_teacher, operator.name, operator.email, source_url; R3: children.max; B2: rooms.enabled, rooms.max_players, rooms.idle_hours; B3: rooms.rounds, rooms.countdown_ms, rooms.reveal_ms)
config/permission.php  (R2: the roles package, published; teams and the Gate hook off)
content/               (§3)
scripts/city-maps/     generate.php + cities.csv: the cities pack's maps (§8)
scripts/app-icons/     generate.php: the app icons from the logo's tile (§10 "Installing Elifba")
docs/                  architecture.md, frontend.md (A2), glossary.md, decisions/
lang/                  az.json, en.json, {az,en}/*.php
resources/js/
├── pages/             classes, students, games/setup, games/board, games/remote, lovhe, pult/{index,guest-board}, rankings/show, podium, words/index, auth/google-register, credits, privacy, welcome
│                      R2: auth/choose-role, settings/roles; R4: children/{index,show}; B2: rooms/{index,join,show}; B4: rooms/screen
├── components/ui      (shadcn/ui from the starter kit)
├── components/app     (the shared kit, as in the precedent)
├── components/{classes,students,games,rankings,share,words,welcome}   (R4: children)
├── game/              contract.ts (+ .test.ts), engine.ts (+ .test.ts), live.ts, outbox.ts, scenes/, styles/
├── rooms/             (B2, B3) contract.ts (+ .test.ts), clock.ts (+ .test.ts), live.ts — no judge and no solution; components/rooms (B4: mechanics/*)
└── lib/               (R3: game-players.ts; R4: setup-levels.ts, replaced by setup-choices.ts for frontend.md rule 13)
routes/web.php (+ auth.php, classroom.php, play.php, rankings.php, settings.php, words.php; R3: family.php; B2: rooms.php), channels.php, console.php
tests/Feature, tests/Unit, tests/Fixtures/contract/, tests/Fixtures/content/ (a valid content root + broken.json), tests/Support/ (ContentFixture, Games, FakeGoogle)
B2, B3: tests/Feature/Rooms, tests/Unit/Rooms, tests/Fixtures/rooms/, tests/Support/Rooms.php
```

Routes (names in English; `/pult` is the one Azerbaijani path because teachers type it — and from B2 `/otaq`, which players type):

| Route                                                                                                                                   | Purpose                                                                       |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/classes`, `POST /classes`, `/classes/{class}` (GET, PATCH, DELETE), `POST`/`DELETE /classes/{class}/archive` (archive / restore)      | classes; one class page with students, learned letters and settings           |
| `PUT /classes/{class}/learned-letters`                                                                                                  | learned letters (`UpdateLearnedLetters`)                                      |
| `POST /classes/{class}/students/preview`                                                                                                | the paste preview (`NameListParser`, JSON, writes nothing)                    |
| `POST /classes/{class}/students`                                                                                                        | add the confirmed names, from the class page or a board (`AddStudents`, back) |
| `PUT /classes/{class}/students/order`                                                                                                   | reorder (`ReorderStudents`)                                                   |
| `/classes/{class}/students/{student}` (PATCH, DELETE), `POST`/`DELETE …/{student}/archive`                                              | edit, delete, archive / restore a student (scoped bindings)                   |
| `/classes/{class}/games/create`, `POST /classes/{class}/games`                                                                          | game setup → `StartGame`                                                      |
| `/games/{game}/board`, `POST /games/{game}/board/claim`, `POST /games/{game}/sync`, `GET /games/{game}/state`                           | the board (claim, sync, state: the teacher or the guest board's bearer, §4)   |
| `POST /games/{game}/join-code`, `PATCH /games/{game}/status`                                                                            | pairing card (teacher), pause / resume / end (teacher or bearer)              |
| `PATCH /games/{game}/rounds`, `POST /games/{game}/replay`                                                                               | round count before «Başla» (teacher or bearer), «Yenidən oyna» (teacher)      |
| `/pult`, `/pult/{code}` (GET; POST claims a guest board and sends it a game), `/games/{game}/remote`                                    | the remote                                                                    |
| `/lovhe`, `POST /guest-boards`, `GET /guest-boards/{board}`, `DELETE /guest-boards/{board}`, `GET`/`PUT /guest-boards/{board}/game`     | the guest board; who reaches each route: §4                                   |
| `/classes/{class}/rankings`, `/classes/{class}/podium` (both `?period=day\|week&date=YYYY-MM-DD`, `PeriodData`)                         | rankings and the podium with the share card                                   |
| `/words` (`?pack={key}`), `POST /words`, `/words/{word}` (PATCH, DELETE), `POST`/`DELETE /words/hidden` (hide / show, word in the body) | the teacher's words per theme pack (§3 "Teacher words")                       |
| `/settings/play` (GET, PUT)                                                                                                             | the phone remote on or off (`UpdatePlaySettings`, §4 "Pairing")               |
| `/settings/play/sounds` (PUT)                                                                                                           | the sounds of the account's boards (`UpdateBoardSounds`, §3 "Sounds")         |
| `/role` (GET, POST), `/start`, `PUT /area`, `/settings/roles` (GET, POST)                                                               | R2: the role choice, home by role, the area switch, the second role (§1)      |
| `/children`, `POST /children`, `/children/{child}` (GET, PATCH, DELETE), `PUT /children/order`, `PUT /children/{child}/learned-letters` | R3: a parent's children (§7 "Parents and their children")                     |
| `/children/{child}/games/create`, `POST /children/{child}/games`                                                                        | R3: home game setup → `StartGame` for the child                               |
| `/rooms`, `POST /rooms`, `/otaq`, `/otaq/{code}` (GET, POST), `/rooms/{room}…`                                                          | B2, B3: rooms; every route and who reaches it: §12; 404 unless switched on    |
| `/auth/google/{redirect,confirm,callback}`, `/auth/google/register` (GET, POST, DELETE)                                                 | Google sign-in (§7 "Google sign-in"); 404 unless configured                   |
| `/`                                                                                                                                     | the welcome page (`WelcomeController`, frontend.md rule 10)                   |
| `/credits`, `/privacy`                                                                                                                  |                                                                               |
| `/manifest.webmanifest`                                                                                                                 | the web app manifest, without a session (§10 "Installing Elifba")             |

### The CI gate

`composer ci:check` is the one gate, locally and in CI:

1. the front-end lint and format check the starter kit ships (in the precedent `vp check`);
2. `tsc --noEmit`;
3. Vitest (`vp test`, `npm run test`) for `resources/js/game/*` pure modules: the contract fixtures against the zod schemas and the shared judge cases (A6), the engine's state machine, the outbox and the board controller (A6.2), the 800 ms rule (A7), the guest board's tab and bearer mode (A7.3);
4. Pint (`--test`);
5. PHPStan level 8 with Larastan;
6. Pest: unit and feature tests, including translation parity, `ContentValidator` over all packs, the syllable set, the contract fixtures, the judge cases, credits coverage and teacher isolation.

GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, actions pinned by SHA as in the precedent): job `ci` — PHP 8.4, Node 22, `composer setup`, `composer ci:check` on SQLite; job `pgsql` — a `postgres:17` service and `php artisan test` with `ELIFBA_TEST_PGSQL_URL` (`phpunit.xml` forces in-memory SQLite over every `DB_*`, and `tests/bootstrap.php` switches to PostgreSQL only on that opt-in), failing when the suite is not on `pgsql` (`DatabaseEngineTest`, and a check that the run migrated the service database); `composer test:pgsql` runs the same suite locally on a throwaway `postgres:17` container; job `docker` — `scripts/docker-smoke.sh` against the Docker stack from the checkout (§10), then again with the profile `voice` on the same image (`ELIFBA_SMOKE_VOICE=1`, `ELIFBA_SMOKE_BUILD=0`).

### Rules the `CLAUDE.md` will give later agents (A2 writes it)

- **Read first:** `docs/architecture.md` (this contract), `docs/glossary.md` (names), `docs/frontend.md` (UI rules). Change the contract first when the model changes.
- **Domains** `Language → Content → Classroom → Play → Rankings`; a domain uses only the ones before it. (`Family` joins before `Play` in R3; `CLAUDE.md` is the living copy of these rules and carries the lines R2–R6 change.)
- **Only Actions write.** Controllers, commands and channel callbacks validate, call one action and shape the response. Validation lives in `*Data::rules()` and is reused by every entry point.
- **Statuses** change only through `TransitionGameSession` (`GameSessionStatus::allowedTransitions()`); derived things (the current round, rankings, places) are never stored.
- **Facts are events after commit** (`GameSessionTransitioned`, `RoundSolved`); listeners never run inside the writing transaction.
- **The board is the only writer of results.** The phone never posts hits; it sends client events. Every result goes through `RecordBoardEvents`, idempotent by `event_id` and fenced by `board_epoch`.
- **Two board modes, one protocol.** A teacher board uses the teacher's session; a guest board uses its bearer, which reaches only the game it shows — never classes, rankings or other games. Codes come only from `PairingCode` and never authorize anything.
- **One judge, one points rule.** `RoundJudge` (mirrored by `engine.ts` against the shared fixtures) and `PointsRule` (PHP only). The round contract changes in the PHP DTOs, the zod schemas in `contract.ts` and the fixtures together; TypeScript types are inferred from the schemas, never written by hand.
- **Content is data.** Never hard-code words, letters, prompts or theme looks in PHP or TypeScript; add them to `content/` and run the validator. Text is cased, syllabified and ordinal-suffixed on the server (`Language`), never in TypeScript. Teachers' own and hidden words are rows (`custom_words`, `hidden_words`); Play reads a pack only through `TeacherPacks` wherever it builds or checks rounds, and a word rule lives once, in `WordRules`.
- **Children's privacy.** A student is a display name and an avatar. Do not add fields about children. Never put a student's name in a log, an exception, a broadcast or a URL, nor a teacher word in a log, an exception or a URL. New routes with `{class}`, `{student}` or `{game}` go into the isolation test. Nothing on the share card but §6's list.
- **No third-party requests** from pages: no CDNs, external fonts, analytics or trackers.
- **Assets** enter only with an entry in `content/credits.json` and an allowed license; Fluent Emoji files stay in `content/assets/fluent/` and never on the share card; state symbols follow §8.
- **Two languages.** Every interface string goes into `lang/az.json` and `lang/en.json`; the parity test fails otherwise. Content stays Azerbaijani.
- **Database portability.** Query builder only; no engine-specific SQL; Baku dates come from `SchoolCalendar`. Tests must pass on SQLite and PostgreSQL.
- **Tests** use Pest; fake time with `travelTo` around midnight and Monday boundaries; never call a real Reverb server in a test: tests run the `reverb` broadcaster with test keys and `tests/Support/FakeReverb` (`$this->reverb->sent`, `->down()`) in place of its HTTP client; or fake the event.
- **Gate:** run `composer ci:check` before calling work done.

---

## 12. Rooms: adults' games (B2–B7)

Designed in B1 (2026-11-17, ADRs 019–024) and built by B2–B7; a game's motion, its sound and its final in ADR 025 (2026-11-18), its sound set in ADR 028 (2026-11-18); games first in ADR 026 (G1–G3); the rivals' strip in ADR 027 (P1, 2026-11-18; built by P2); live voice between the players in ADR 031 (C1, 2026-11-18; "Live voice" below, built by C2–C5); the room's page without the app's shell, in a game frame of its own, in ADR 032 (F1, 2026-11-19; "The game frame (F2)" below, built by F2); until a step lands, nothing of what it is marked with exists. The section is whole on purpose: everything about rooms is here, and §1–§11 only point to it, so the contract of class and home games reads as before.

A **room** is opened by an account and entered by other accounts with its code. In it, everyone plays the same rounds at the same time, each on their own phone, and the room sees a ranking. The games are memory, logic and sequences, and Azerbaijani word games, at a difficulty the host picks.

### Three rules that have a scope now

`CLAUDE.md` states three rules without a scope. Each stays exactly as it is for class and home games; a room has its own.

| The rule                                                 | For a class game and a home game: unchanged                                                                       | A room's own rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The board is the only writer of results                  | `RecordBoardEvents`, `event_id`, `board_epoch`; the phone sends client events and never posts a hit (§4, ADR 002) | There is no board. Each player's device posts that player's own answer, and only `RecordRoomAnswer` writes it: one per player per round, idempotent by `event_id`, judged and timed by the server. No client event counts (ADR 021).           |
| Codes come only from `PairingCode` and authorize nothing | A pairing code says which board; authority is the owner's session or a guest board's bearer (§4)                  | A room code comes only from `RoomCode`, a code space and an entrance (`/otaq`) of its own. It admits a signed-in, confirmed account that holds the player role into that one room — nothing without that account, nothing else ever (ADR 020). |
| A game is for a class or for one child                   | `GamePlayers` has two cases; `game_sessions`, `game_rounds` and `game_answers` hold nothing else (§1, ADR 011)    | A room game is a `room_games` row of the `Rooms` domain, never a `game_sessions` row. `Rooms` uses nothing of `Classroom`, `Family` or `Play`, and nothing uses `Rooms` (ADR 023).                                                             |

Three more follow from them: `RoundJudge`, `engine.ts` and `PointsRule` stay the one judge and the one points rule of the children's games, and a room has `RoomJudge` and `RoomPointsRule`, in PHP only; a child still has stars and never a ranking, and a room can never hold a student or a child; the guest board's bearer still reaches only the game its board shows, and never a room.

### The area and the switch

**Decision** (B2, ADR 019). Adults' games are an area of their own, opened by a third role and hidden behind a switch.

- **The role.** `App\Enums\Role::Player` (`player`, «Oyunçu»): a third `roles` row, `users.player_agreed_at`, home `rooms.index`. One role for the account that opens a room and the accounts that join it; the **host** is the room's `user_id`, not a role. Taken like the others — `/role`, or Settings → «Rollar» — with its own sentence (§7 "Agreements").
- **The group.** Every room route is in the player group — `auth`, `role.chosen`, `verified`, `role:player`, `rooms.enabled` — and in no other (§1 "Accounts and roles"); from I2 (ADR 033) every one but the invitation's door, `GET` and `POST /oyun/qeydiyyat` (`rooms.enabled`, `guest`), which reads no room ("The invitation link (I2)"). `users.area` may be `player`; `components/app/nav-areas.ts` gets a third navigation with one entry, «Otaqlar».
- **The switch.** `config('elifba.rooms.enabled')` ← `ELIFBA_ROOMS`, false unless set; `.env.example` and `.env.production.example` carry it switched off. `App\Support\RoomsArea::enabled()` is the one reader. While it is off: `rooms.enabled` (`EnsureRoomsAreEnabled`) answers 404 on every room route; `Role::available()` leaves the role out, so it is neither offered nor granted (`RoleChoiceData::rules()`); `User::roleList()` and `User::area()` leave it out, so no page prop, navigation or switch shows it and `/start` ignores it; the room channel refuses. The tables and the `roles` row exist either way.
- **Nothing on the children's side**, with the switch on as well: no class, student, child, ranking, podium, words, setup, board, remote, `/pult` or guest board page gets a prop, link or request about rooms, and the game catalog lists no room game. Outside the player area an account sees of rooms only the «Oyunçu» card where roles are taken and, once it holds the role, «Otaqlar» in its own user menu's area switch.
- **An invited account.** `/otaq/{code}` keeps its address as the intended one through login, registration, e-mail confirmation and the role: an account without the player role is sent to `/role` (no role yet) or Settings → «Rollar», with «Oyunçu» preselected, and `GrantRole` redirects on to it. From I2 (ADR 033) a visitor who opened the link by hand comes to the invitation's door instead of the login and takes the role there, and the end of every such path joins at once (§12 "The invitation link (I2)").
- **Factory.** `User::factory()->player()` and `->teacherAndPlayer()`; the default stays a teacher. `tests/TestCase` leaves the switch off; `tests/Support/Rooms.php` turns it on for a test (`Rooms::on()`) and builds rooms (`Rooms::open()`, `Rooms::playing()`).

**Why.** A role is how Elifba already says what an account may enter, with a test that enumerates the routes; a switch in `.env` is how an instance already turns an optional thing on (Google sign-in). An instance for schools only never shows the area.

**Rejected.** Rooms for any teacher or parent; an account with no role; a host role beside a player role; a permission; a separate app (ADR 019).

### Rooms and joining

**Decision** (B2, ADR 020). Every player is an account, shown under the account's name; a room code admits such an account into one room.

- **Opening.** `POST /rooms` → `OpenRoom` (`Rooms\Data\OpenRoomData`: `plays` boolean, default true — the host is the first player): a `rooms` row with a code from `RoomCode`. One open room per host, checked under a lock on the host's `users` row; a second is refused with `rooms.errors.already_open`.
- **The code.** `Rooms\Support\RoomCode`: 6 digits, unique among open rooms by the column's unique index (a collision is retried in its own transaction, as `PairingCode` does), valid while the room is open, cleared when it closes. `RoomCode::resolve(code)` finds an open room or nothing. It shares nothing with `PairingCode`: the same six digits may be a room and a board at once, and each is unknown at the other's entrance.
- **Joining.** `GET /otaq` is the code field (`GET /otaq?code=482 913` → `/otaq/482913`; spaces and dashes dropped; anything but 6 digits is a validation error on `code`). `GET /otaq/{code}` shows page `rooms/join` — the code, the account's own name as the room will show it, «Qoşul» — or redirects a present player to the room. _(From I2, ADR 033: opened by hand, or at the end of a path such an open began, it joins at once through `JoinRoom`; the join page and «Qoşul» stay for every other request — §12 "The invitation link (I2)".)_ `POST /otaq/{code}` → `JoinRoom`: under the room row's lock it refuses a locked room and a full one (`rooms.errors.locked`, `rooms.errors.full`, shown on the join page with nothing else about the room), brings back a player who had left (the same row, `left_at` cleared) or adds a row, raises the revision and signals. An unknown code, a closed room (an idle one too) and an account the host removed get the same refusal, a validation error on `code` with `rooms.errors.unknown` and never a 404 page: `GET /otaq?code=` answers it where the code was typed, as it does a malformed code; `GET` and `POST /otaq/{code}` — a link, a QR, or a room that closed between the page and «Qoşul» — redirect to `/otaq` with it; a JSON client gets one 422 body for every reason. Nothing in the answer tells the three apart, and each attempt counts against `room-join`; over that limit the answer is the same, with `rooms.errors.too_many` (JSON: the 429). A `{code}` that is not 6 digits matches no route (404).
- **Who reaches what** is ownership, as everywhere: `{room}` (`Room::resolveRouteBinding`) finds only a room the current account hosts or has a row in that is not removed, so any other id is a 404 before controller code; `{member}` is scoped under `{room}` (`scopeBindings()`); `Rooms\Policies\RoomPolicy` is the second layer — `view` (the host; a player, present or past), `play` (a present player of an open room), `host` (the host) — on every such route. A player who left an open room sees its page without the live state, and comes back with the code.
- **Names.** A room shows `users.name` of each player, read when the page props or the state are built; no room table holds a name. A row without an account (`user_id` null) is «Oyunçu». Two players with one name are two rows; the page marks the viewer's own («siz»).
- **The host's tools.** `PATCH /rooms/{room}` `{locked}` → `LockRoom`; `DELETE /rooms/{room}/members/{member}` → `RemoveRoomPlayer` (not the host's own row); `POST /rooms/{room}/close` → `CloseRoom`. A player leaves with `POST /rooms/{room}/leave` → `LeaveRoom`.
- **Limits.** `elifba.rooms.max_players` (20) present players; `elifba.rooms.idle_hours` (3) without activity closes a room (`Room::isOpen()` says so at once, `CloseIdleRooms` writes it within the hour). Limiters in `AppServiceProvider`: `rooms-open` (6 a minute per account), `room-join` (10 a minute per account, for every request that tries a code: `GET /otaq?code=` and both `/otaq/{code}`; `/otaq` without a code is not counted, so the code field always opens), `room-state` (120 a minute per account), `room-answers` (30 a minute per account).
- **Closing** sets `closed_at`, clears the code, ends a running game (`room_games.ended_ms`) and is final. A closed room is its results page.

Routes (all in the player group; `{room}` and `{member}` are ULIDs):

| Method and path                         | Reaches it (policy)                         | Answer                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `GET /rooms`                            | any player account                          | page `rooms/index`: the account's open rooms, the code field, «Otaq aç», past rooms  |
| `POST /rooms`                           | any player account, `throttle:rooms-open`   | redirect to the room / `already_open`                                                |
| `GET /otaq`, `GET /otaq/{code}`         | any player account, `throttle:room-join`    | the code field; page `rooms/join`, a redirect to the room, or `/otaq` with `unknown` |
| `POST /otaq/{code}`                     | any player account, `throttle:room-join`    | redirect to the room / the join page with the reason / `/otaq` with `unknown`        |
| `GET /rooms/{room}`                     | `view`                                      | page `rooms/show`: the lobby and the game while open, the results once closed        |
| `GET /rooms/{room}/state`               | `play` or `host`, `throttle:room-state`     | `RoomState` (JSON, `no-store`)                                                       |
| `POST /rooms/{room}/leave`              | `play`                                      | redirect to `/rooms`                                                                 |
| `PATCH /rooms/{room}`                   | `host`                                      | lock or unlock; back                                                                 |
| `DELETE /rooms/{room}/members/{member}` | `host`                                      | remove a player; back                                                                |
| `POST /rooms/{room}/close`              | `host`                                      | close; back to the results                                                           |
| `DELETE /rooms/{room}`                  | `host`, closed room                         | `DeleteRoom`; redirect to `/rooms`                                                   |
| `DELETE /rooms/{room}/name`             | `view`, closed room, a player's own row     | `ForgetRoomPlayer`; redirect to `/rooms`                                             |
| `GET /rooms/{room}/screen`              | `screen`: `host`, open room                 | page `rooms/screen` (B4)                                                             |
| `POST /rooms/{room}/games`              | `start`: `host`, open room, no game running | B3: `StartRoomGame`; 204                                                             |
| `DELETE /rooms/{room}/game`             | `end`: `host`, a game running               | B3: `EndRoomGame`; 204                                                               |
| `POST /rooms/{room}/answers`            | `play`, `throttle:room-answers`             | B3: `RecordRoomAnswer`; `{status, revision}` (JSON, `no-store`)                      |
| `POST /broadcasting/auth`               | the host or a present player, open room     | the `room.{room}` signature / 403                                                    |

**Why.** An account behind every name means a name cannot be borrowed, a removed player stays removed, and results can be kept with names. A code of its own keeps `PairingCode` and `/pult` exactly what they were.

**Rejected.** A code and a typed name without an account (the advisor's recommendation; the owner chose accounts); both ways in; a nickname; reusing `PairingCode`; host approval of every joiner; a presence channel (ADR 020).

### The race

**Decision** (B3, ADR 021). Each player's device writes its own answer over HTTP; the server alone judges, keeps the clock and holds the solution until the round is closed; the timetable is derived and the channel carries signals only.

```
 phone A        phone B        server (PHP)                         Reverb
   │              │   GET state  │  (at each moment of the timetable)  │
   │ ◀── the question, no solution ──│                                 │
   │ POST answer ─────────────────▶ │ RecordRoomAnswer: judge, time,   │
   │ ◀──────────── accepted ─────── │ points; stored once              │
   │              │ POST answer ──▶ │ everyone answered → closed_ms    │
   │              │                 │ ── room.changed {revision} ────▶ │ ─▶ A, B
   │ GET state ───────────────────▶ │ the solution, results, ranking   │
```

**Starting a game.** `POST /rooms/{room}/games` → `StartRoomGame` with `Rooms\Data\RoomGameSetupData` (`mechanic`, `difficulty`, `rounds` between `elifba.rooms.rounds.min` and `.max`; `rules()` accepts only a level of `content/rooms/levels.json` whose mechanic `RoomPlayable` lists). Under the room row's lock it refuses a closed room and a room whose game is still running, builds every round with the mechanic's builder from a fresh `seed`, stores each as `payload` and `solution`, sets `started_ms` to now + `elifba.rooms.countdown_ms` (3 000) and keeps `elifba.rooms.reveal_ms` (3 000; 5 000 until 2026-11-18, which a game started before keeps) as the game's `reveal_ms`. `DELETE /rooms/{room}/game` → `EndRoomGame` sets `ended_ms`.

**The timetable** (`Rooms\Support\RoomTimetable`, pure; `RoomClock::nowMs()` is the only source of "now", so tests move time with `travelTo`):

```
opens(0)        = started_ms
answer_from(i)  = opens(i) + study_ms(i)
bell(i)         = answer_from(i) + answer_ms(i)
closes(i)       = closed_ms(i) ?? bell(i)
opens(i + 1)    = closes(i) + reveal_ms
over            = ended_ms ?? closes(last) + reveal_ms
```

`RoomTimetable::at($game, $nowMs)` gives the round and its phase — `countdown`, `study`, `answer`, `reveal` — or `over`. Nothing stores them. A change that time causes needs no trigger: every device holds the moments and asks again when one comes, and whoever asks later is answered from the later phase. Only facts are written: the start, an answer, an early close (`closed_ms`), the host's end (`ended_ms`) — and, for whom a round waits, each player's page's last read (`seen_ms`), which never moves a moment by itself.

**The state.** `GET /rooms/{room}/state` → `Rooms\Support\RoomState::for($room, $account)` as `Rooms\Data\RoomStateData`, the one thing a room page reads after its first props:

```
RoomState {
  v: 1, revision, server_ms,
  room:    { id, status: open | closed, locked, code | null },         // code: while open
  me:      { player_id | null, host: bool },                           // a host who does not play has no player_id
  players: [{ id, name | null, present, host }],                       // never an account id or an address
  game:    { id, mechanic, difficulty, rounds, phase: live | over } | null,
  round:   RoomRound | null,                                           // the current round, while the game is live
  ranking: [{ player_id, points, place | null }]                       // the game's, over its closed rounds; the room's once it is over; [] before the first game
}
RoomRound {
  id, index, total, phase: countdown | study | answer | reveal,
  opens_ms, answer_from_ms, bell_ms, closes_ms | null, next_ms | null,  // the close and the next opening: only in phase reveal
  study:  { layout: row | grid, grid: {cols, rows} | null, items: Item[] } | null,   // only in phase study
  ask:    { shape: choice | sequence | set, pick, layout, grid, shown: Item[], items: Item[] } | null, // only from phase answer; shown: B5
  answered, answered_count, present_count,                             // present_count: the present players the round waits for; answered_count: those who answered; false and 0 before answer
  reveal: { solution: string[], word: string | null,                    // only in phase reveal
            mine: { picked: string[], correct, points, elapsed_ms } | null,
            results: [{ player_id, correct, points }] } | null
}
Item { id: string(8), kind: digit | letter | word | number | shape | cell, text | null, asset | null, cell: [col, row] | null }
```

`ask.items` are what an answer is made of, and nothing else: `RecordRoomAnswer` checks `picked` against them. `ask.shown` (B5) holds the items shown with the question that are not answers — the row of numbers `next_in_sequence` continues — with ids of their own, never picked; it is `[]` for every other mechanic, and a round stored without it reads as `[]`.

What is withheld is the rule: nothing of a round before it opens; the study material only while it is studied (a reload during `answer` does not show it again); the question only from `answer`; the solution, anybody's right or wrong and the round's points only from `reveal`. The schemas are split by phase on both sides (`RoomRoundData`'s constructor, zod's union by `phase`), so a key in the wrong phase fails; the first props of `rooms/show` carry the same state, byte for byte, and `place_labels` beside it. `server_ms` lets the page estimate the server's clock (`resources/js/rooms/clock.ts`: the answer's `server_ms` against the midpoint of its own send and receive times) and ask again at `opens_ms`, `answer_from_ms`, the bell and `next_ms`, each request sent half a round trip ahead (`ServerClock.trip()`, at most 250 ms) so that it reaches the server 60 ms after the moment; a page that asked a little early is told the same phase and asks again once the time the state's `server_ms` says was left has passed (amended 2026-11-18, ADR 021 "Amendment of 2026-11-18: when a page asks").

**An answer.** `POST /rooms/{room}/answers` `{event_id, round_id, picked}` (`Rooms\Data\RoomAnswerData::rules()`: a ULID, a ULID, 1–36 item ids). `RecordRoomAnswer`, in one transaction with the room row and then the round row locked, always in that order:

1. the `event_id` is already stored → `duplicate`; this player already answered the round → `answered`;
2. the round is not the game's current round in its `answer` phase by the server's clock — before the question, after the bell or the early close, a round of another game, a game that is over, a closed room, a player who left or was removed → `late`, nothing written. Nothing is accepted after the bell: the reveal is served from that moment, and the receipt's moment is read under the room's lock;
3. `picked` is not made of the round's own `ask` item ids in the count its shape asks for → 422, nothing written;
4. otherwise it is judged (`RoomJudge`), timed (`elapsed_ms` = receipt − `answer_from`), scored (`RoomPointsRule`), stored, and the room's `last_activity_at` touched → `accepted`;
5. the revision is raised and `RoomChanged` is dispatched after commit, since the count of answers the screen and the phones show has changed; if everyone the round waits for has now answered, `closed_ms` = this moment, in the same revision. `LeaveRoom`, `RemoveRoomPlayer` and a return (`JoinRoom`) make the same check.

The answer is `{status: accepted | duplicate | answered | late, revision}` — never whether it was right. The player is the session's account: a body cannot name another.

**Whom a round waits for** (amended 2026-11-18, ADR 021 decision 7). A present player who has answered the round, or whose own room page has read the state since the round before it closed — for round 0, since the game was started (`Rooms\Support\RoomWaits`: `since()` is `room_games.created_at` for round 0 and `closes(i − 1)` for every other; `waitsFor()`). The early close (`RoomRoundClose`) and the state's `present_count` and `answered_count` read the same rule, so a full count is a closed round, and a round nobody is waited for — nobody answered, nobody read — runs to its bell.

- **The read** is `room_players.seen_ms`, written by `Rooms\Actions\RecordRoomRead` when `rooms/show`'s props (`RoomController::show`) or `GET /rooms/{room}/state` are served to a present player of an open room, before the state is built, so the reader counts in it. It writes only when the read changes what a round waits for — the stored moment is older than the `since()` of the round in its countdown, study or answer, or of the next one during a reveal (`RoomWaits::readCountsFrom()`) — so at most once a round, never in the lobby, the last reveal or after the end; one conditional update of the player's own row, without the room's lock, no revision, no signal, no close. A live page reads at every moment of the timetable, the close included, and the reveal gives it `reveal_ms` before the next round opens.
- **The big screen is not the host's phone.** `RoomScreenController` records nothing, and `RoomStateController` records nothing for a request whose `Referer` path is the room's `/rooms/{room}/screen`; a request without a `Referer` counts as the room page's — at worst the host's rounds wait for the host, as before.
- **The migration** (`2026_11_26_000001_add_seen_ms_to_room_players`, additive and nullable) sets `seen_ms` of the present players of open rooms to its own moment, so a game running at the deploy waits for everyone in its current round, as before, and by the reads from the next.

**The channel.** `private-room.{roomId}`:

```php
// routes/channels.php — an account's session only; never a guest board's bearer
Broadcast::channel('room.{room}', static fn (User $user, string $room): bool
    => RoomChannel::allows($user, $room), ['guards' => ['web']]);

// Rooms\Support\RoomChannel::allows(): the switch is on, the account holds the
// player role, the room exists and is open, and the account is its host or a
// present player.
```

The server broadcasts one event on it, `room.changed` `{v: 1, revision}` (`Rooms\Events\RoomChanged`, `ShouldBroadcastNow`, after commit), for a join, a leave, a removal, a lock, a game started or ended, an accepted answer (the count of answers; the last one is also the early close), an early close and the room's close. A page that sees a revision above its own fetches the state. Nothing else travels on the channel — no content, name, answer or result — and no client event means anything in a room: the pages send none and listen for none (`resources/js/rooms/live.ts` has no whisper). From C4 a page in the live voice also holds a connection to the instance's voice server: it is not this channel, carries no game fact, and nothing of a round reads it ("Live voice" below). A broadcast that fails never fails the write; it is reported as `Rooms\Exceptions\RoomChangeNotBroadcast` with the room id and the cause's class. Without a socket a page polls the state every 1.5 s in the lobby and while it waits for the others in a question — once it has answered, and always on the big screen, which answers nothing (`waitsForSignal()`, `waitsForOthers()`); with a socket it still asks every 3 s in the lobby and while it waits, since a socket can stay open and hear nothing (`WAIT_POLL_MS`), and every page asks at once when it is shown again (`visibilitychange`, `onShown()`: a phone woken from its lock, a tab brought back), and pusher-js pings after 10 s of silence and closes after 5 s more without a pong (`SOCKET_TIMEOUTS`), so a deaf socket is found within about 15 s; the countdown, the study and the reveal end at their moments, which every page asks about anyway.

**Late, lost and gone.**

| What happens                                   | The room                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| A player joins during a game (room not locked) | gets the state like everyone and is waited for from their room page's first read; may answer the open round in the time that is left, has nothing for earlier rounds                                                                                         |
| A phone reloads or reconnects                  | the state is everything; an answer sent twice is stored once                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A player vanishes without leaving              | the round they vanished in runs to its bell; no later round waits for them until their page reads again; the host can remove them                                                                                                                            |
| A phone's socket is open but hears nothing     | the page still asks at every moment and every 3 s in the lobby and while it waits for the others, so it learns of a start within 3 s, reads in each reveal and is waited for; the socket is found dead within about 15 s, then the page polls and reconnects |
| A phone wakes from its lock, a tab comes back  | the page asks at once, whatever its timers and its socket say; a round that opened and closed while it slept is still lost, and it is waited for again from that read                                                                                        |
| The host's phone is gone                       | the game goes on by its timetable, and a playing host is waited for as any player — the big screen is not the host's phone; a new game, the lock, a removal and closing wait for the host; an idle room closes by itself                                     |
| Reverb is down                                 | the phases still change by the timetable; joins and early closes are seen within 1.5 s                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Nobody is watching                             | nothing runs and nothing needs to: the game is over when its timetable says, the room closes when idle                                                                                                                                                       |

There is no pause, and the host is never replaced.

**The big screen** (B4). `GET /rooms/{room}/screen`, page `rooms/screen`, for the host's own session on a laptop or a television's browser: the code and its QR (`qrcode.react`, `{APP_URL}/otaq/{code}`) while the room waits; in a game what every player sees of the round, the count of answers, then the reveal and the ranking. It reads the same state and writes nothing — not even a read: it is not the host's phone ("Whom a round waits for"); a room never needs it. It is not the guest board: `/lovhe`, the `board` guard and its bearer are untouched and never show a room.

**Why.** Twenty people answering the same round against each other leave no device that may hold the solution or be trusted to judge. A timetable every device can compute needs no queue and no worker, which Elifba does not have, and cannot drift with whoever looks late.

**Rejected.** One writing device, as in class; a hashed solution for a local judge; rounds and results in broadcasts; client events between players; delayed jobs or a ticking worker; a `GET` that settles the round; the device's clock; a grace period after the bell; the guest board as the screen (ADR 021).

### Scoring and what is kept

**Decision** (B3, B4; ADR 022).

- **Points belong to an answer** (`Rooms\Support\RoomPointsRule`, PHP only): a right answer gives **10**, plus **0–10** for speed — `ceil(10 × (answer_ms − elapsed_ms) ÷ answer_ms)` — so 20 at once and 10 at the bell; a wrong answer and no answer give 0; nothing is negative. A round is all or nothing and one attempt. From T2 a taunt costs its thrower `RoomPointsRule::TAUNT_COST` (1), counted from the first close after it — an answer's points never change, but a game's total can go down from one reveal to the next, never below 0 (ADR 029, "Taunts (T2)").
- **The ranking is derived** (`Rooms\Support\RoomRanking`): `ofGame()` sums `room_answers.points` per player over the game's closed rounds, never the open one — from T2 less the taunts the player threw that those closes count (ADR 029 decision 2); `ofRoom()` over the room's games. Dense places (`DenseRank`), no tie-breaker; inside a place names in Azerbaijani order (`AzCollator`), a nameless player last; a player with no points is listed after the places without one; a removed player is not listed. A row is `{player_id, points, place}`, and the page labels a place from its props: `place_labels`, from `Ordinal::label()`, the label of place n at n − 1.
- **Who sees it:** the host and the players of that room, on the room page and the screen — each round's results, the game's ranking after every round, the room's standing between games. All places are shown.
- **Kept:** the room with its players, games, rounds and answers (from T2 its taunts, whose target is emptied after a day), for `elifba.retention_days` from the room's `created_at` (`PruneOldRooms`). `/rooms` lists an account's past rooms (the date by `DateLabel`, the games, the number of players, the account's own place); `/rooms/{room}` shows a closed room's games and rankings, names read from the accounts as they are now.
- **Deleted:** a player takes their name out of a closed room (`ForgetRoomPlayer`: `user_id` null; the points stay as «Oyunçu»); the host deletes a closed room (`DeleteRoom`, cascade); an account is deleted (its rooms by cascade, its rows elsewhere nameless); time. A player's taunts stay when their name is taken out or they are removed, so nobody's place moves (T2).
- **Never:** a total, rating, level, streak or profile across rooms; a ranking of anything but one room; a share card of a room; who taunted whom, or how often, after a game (T2).

**Why.** A race the players chose may reward speed, where a class's turn-taking may not (§5). Ten coarse steps keep network time from deciding more than a point or two.

**Rejected.** No speed; a bonus by order of arrival; time as a tie-breaker; partial credit; stored totals; a copied name; keeping nothing, or results without names (the advisor's recommendation; the owner chose names) (ADR 022).

### Room games

**Decision** (B3–B6, ADR 023). A room game is **mechanic × difficulty**, with a number of rounds; there is no theme pack.

| Part           | Is                                                                      | Where                                                                                                             | Adding one needs |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------- |
| **Mechanic**   | What a round shows and asks, and the shape of its answer                | Code: `Rooms\Support\Mechanics\*` (a builder), `RoomJudge` (the shape), `components/rooms/mechanics/*` (the view) | Code             |
| **Difficulty** | How long, how many, how much time: «Asan», «Orta», «Çətin», «Çox çətin» | Data: `content/rooms/levels.json`, one entry per mechanic and difficulty 1–4                                      | A JSON entry     |
| **Material**   | Words, groups of words, shapes                                          | Data: `content/rooms/{words,categories,shapes}.json`, `content/assets/rooms/`                                     | Data             |

`Content\Enums\RoomFamily` (`memory`, `logic`, `words`) and `Content\Enums\RoomMechanic` name them; they are separate from `Mechanic`, `Unit` and the children's `levels.json`, which do not change. `RoomFamily` holds the three families from B3; `RoomMechanic` grows with the step that builds each mechanic — B3 `recall_order`, B4 `recall_cells`, B5 the two logic games, B6 the two word games — so every case has a builder and the validator's rules for its levels. `Content\Enums\RoomItemKind` names the six kinds of `Item.kind`, and `RoomMechanic::kinds()` the kinds a level of the mechanic may name, as far as their material exists. `Rooms\Support\RoomPlayable` lists the mechanics whose view exists; the setup offers only those.

| Family   | Mechanic           | A round                                                                                      | Shape      | Built in |
| -------- | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- | -------- |
| `memory` | `recall_order`     | a row of digits, letters, words or shapes is shown, then hidden: tap them in the order shown | `sequence` | B3       |
| `memory` | `recall_cells`     | cells of a grid light up, then go dark: tap the cells that were lit                          | `set`      | B4       |
| `logic`  | `next_in_sequence` | a row of numbers follows a rule: choose what comes next among four                           | `choice`   | B5       |
| `logic`  | `odd_one_out`      | one of the numbers or words does not belong: choose it                                       | `choice`   | B5       |
| `words`  | `anagram`          | the letters of a word, shuffled and never in their own order: tap them into a word           | `sequence` | B6       |
| `words`  | `spelling`         | one word written four ways, the wrong ones by swapping confusable letters: choose the right  | `choice`   | B6       |

**The judge** (`Rooms\Support\RoomJudge`, PHP only; cases in `tests/Fixtures/rooms/judge.json`) knows three shapes. `choice`: the one picked id is the solution's. `set`: the picked ids are the solution's, in any order. `sequence`: what the picked items say, in order, equals one of the solution's accepted readings — so two tiles with the same letter are interchangeable, and an anagram accepts every word of the list that its letters spell. A stored solution is `{shape, ids}`, a `sequence`'s also has `accept: string[][]`, and a word game's also has `word` (B6): an anagram's drawn word in its tiles' capitals, a spelling's right spelling — the reveal's `word`, which the judge never reads; a round stored without it reads null. `ids` are the canonical ids — a choice's one, a set's, a sequence's in the order to tap — and `accept` what the judge compares. An id picked twice makes an answer wrong. The solution never reaches a device before the reveal, where it is shown as the canonical ids and, for a word, the word.

**The builders** take the level, the count and a seeded random source and return rounds; each has its unit test.

- `recall_order` — level: `kinds` (of `digit`, `letter`, `word`, `shape`), `length` {min, max} (3–12, and no longer than the kind has items: 10 digits), `study_ms`, `answer_ms`. One kind per round; digits are 0–9; letters come from `Alphabet`, cased by `Az::upper`; shapes from `shapes.json` (B4), each its picture (`asset`) with no text, so a row of shapes is read by its pictures; words of 3–5 letters of `content/rooms/words.json` (B6, `RoomWords::forRow()`), as the list writes them — a kind no level names yet (B6 "As built"). `study` is the row in order; `ask` the same items shuffled, never in the shown order. No item comes twice in a row (B3 part 2: a repeated item let the question alone rule orders out — {1, 1, 2} with no item beside its equal can only be 1 2 1), so the question says nothing of the order but that it is not the one shown; a shuffle that reads like the row is turned by one place, which cannot. A row has at least three items: two could only be asked reversed. The question's items have ids of their own, never the row's.
- Item ids, for every mechanic, are 8 characters of a–z and 0–9 from the seeded source (`Rooms\Support\Mechanics\RoomItemIds`), none twice in a round: they carry no position, so neither an id nor the order of the ids tells what a round shows or asks.
- `recall_cells` — level: `grid` {cols, rows} (each 2–6, so at most 6 × 6), `lit` {min, max} (from 1, fewer than the cells). `study` is the grid with the lit cells only; `ask` the dark grid — every cell in reading order, with ids of its own, built before the lit cells are drawn, so nothing in it depends on which were lit — and `pick` = the number lit; the solution is the question's ids of the lit cells.
- `next_in_sequence` — level: `rules` (of `arithmetic`, `geometric`, `alternating`, `squares`, `fibonacci`, `interleaved`; the names in `Content\Enums\RoomSequenceRule`, their arithmetic in `Rooms\Support\Mechanics\SequenceRules`), `terms` (how many are shown, at least what each named rule needs to be told from the others), `range` (every number a round shows, from 1 to 999). The row is `ask.shown`; the four options in `ask.items`: the next term and three near misses, all different, in an order drawn from the seeded source. A round whose shown terms fit any two of the six rules with different next terms is built again, so no rule the game knows continues it otherwise.
- `odd_one_out` — level: `source` (`numbers`: three or four share a property — a multiple, a parity, a square — and one does not, and no other property the builder knows singles out another number; `words`: from one group of `categories.json` and one from another, compared by `Az::lower`), `options` (4 or 5).
- `anagram` — level: `word_length` {min, max} (3–12). A word of the list in that range, none twice in a game while the level's words last; its letters are the question's tiles (`letter`, cased by `Az::upper`: i → İ, ı → I), never in the order of any word they spell; `pick` = its letters. The solution's `accept` holds every correct spelling of the list with those letters, compared by `Az::lower` (`alma` and `amal`), its `ids` the tiles in the drawn word's order, its `word` the drawn word in capitals.
- `spelling` — level: `word_length`, `swaps` (1 or 2). One word of the list written four ways (`word` items, as the list writes them, the right one's place drawn from the seeded source); the solution is the right one's id and its `word`. The wrong spellings swap letters for their confusables (`Alphabet::confusables()`), and none is a correct spelling — a word of the list, an `also_correct`, a first name — nor starts with «ğ». They are the ways of writing some letters of the word: with `swaps` 1, two letters, each written two ways, so two of the four differ in one letter or both; with `swaps` 2, three letters, every wrong spelling changing two, so any two differ in two. Either way, where they differ two spellings write a letter one way and two the other, and the right one is never the spelling the others agree with most. A word is drawn only if it makes three such wrong spellings.
- The rules both word builders follow and the validator counts by are `Content\Support\RoomWords` (`accepted()`, `forAnagram()`, `spellings()`, `forSpelling()`, `forRow()`), since the validator may not name `Rooms`.

**The contract** is the room's own: PHP DTOs in `Rooms\Data\*` (`RoomStateData`, `RoomRoundData`, `RoomItemData`, `RoomRevealData`, `RoomAnswerData`, `RoomAnswerReceiptData`), zod schemas in `resources/js/rooms/contract.ts` with the TypeScript types inferred from them, fixtures in `tests/Fixtures/rooms/` (`state.lobby.json`, `state.countdown.json`, `state.study.json`, `state.answer.json`, `state.reveal.json`, `state.over.json`, `answer.json`, `receipt.json`, `judge.json`) read by `RoomContractTest` and `contract.test.ts`. The schemas are strict, so a name or a solution in the wrong place fails them. `resources/js/game/contract.ts`, `RoundData` and `tests/Fixtures/contract/` are not touched.

**The pages** are React on a phone, without Phaser, and with the board's voice in two places only: the winner's cheer at the final, on the screen — or on every phone in a room without one ("A room without a screen") — the first name, then «Əla oynadın!» (ADR 030, below) — and, from T3, a taunt's phrase on its target's phone and the screen (ADR 029 as amended, "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)") (B4; `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 is the player shell). Every moment of a game moves as ADR 025 decides (rule 15 "Motion"): the movement follows the state and the viewer's own taps, a page animates only a moment it saw begin (`roomMoment()`), nothing moves differently for a right or a wrong answer before the reveal, numbers that count are the server's, and everything stands still under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The end of a game is a final (`FinalStanding`): the state's ranking — the room's standing — opening from the last place to the first, the first three emphasised, a short `canvas-confetti` burst on the screen and on the winner's own phone — on every phone in a room without a screen (ADR 030 as amended 2026-11-19). Sound follows ADR 025 "Sound" with the set of ADR 028 (rule 15 "Sound"): the screen is the room's speaker — the countdown, one 3 s file ending at `opens_ms`; `go` as round 0 opens; a later round's study and question opening; the clock, one 5 s file ending at `bell_ms`; the round closing, the ranking; in the final a hit as the third and the second places open, the roll and the fanfare, then the winner's cheer; and between games `join` when a player appears — and a phone plays only quiet cues of its own — its taps, its answer sent (one sound for every answer) and, from the reveal, its verdict; each device has a mute of its own (`localStorage`). When a cue plays is `roomCues()`'s (`components/rooms/sound-cues.ts`), from two consecutive states and the estimated server clock, once per moment the page saw begin, so a reload or a state fetched again plays nothing, and nothing audible tells right from wrong before the reveal: the clock is the same for everyone and only the round's close stops it. A long cue is placed on the server's clock and starts from its middle when the page is late; every cue stops when its moment ends or the page is hidden. Browsers allow no sound before a gesture: a page makes its audio in the first one (on the screen, its «Oyun səslərini aç»), and where audio is missing or refused the game plays on in silence. A page gets the files it may play as `sounds` in its props: `rooms/screen` the room's, `rooms/show` a phone's and the room's, which a phone plays only in a room without a screen, the cheer included (§12 "A room without a screen"). A mechanic's instruction is interface text — `rooms.mechanics.{mechanic}.{name,line,study,ask}` in `lang/az.json` and `lang/en.json`, with no `study` for a game that is not studied — and what a round shows is content, Azerbaijani in every locale and cased on the server.

**The winner's cheer** (ADR 030). At the final the screen ends the game as a home board does: the first name of the account that holds the first place, from the clip of the built-in name list (§3 "Voice", `content/voice-names/`), then `PHRASE_GAP_MS` (150 ms) later the board's «Əla oynadın!» — «Nihat! … Əla oynadın!» — from `winnerAt + FANFARE_MS` (the fanfare's 2.30 s, `scripts/room-sounds/README.md`), at the screen's level times `VOICE_GAIN` (the clips are −16 LUFS, the room's set −20 LUFS). Whether a name can be said is `Content\Support\NameVoices::file(users.name)`, as for a display name; a name that cannot be said leaves the phrase alone. A first place shared by two or three is said name by name in the state's ranking order, `NAME_GAP_MS` (300 ms) apart, then «Əla oynadınız!»; four or more, «Əla oynadınız!» alone; a holder without a clip is skipped. The phrases are the board's built-in public clips (`voice.json`'s `finish`, `finish_class`), which only the screen's props carry, as `finish` (`{one, many}`, `Rooms\Support\RoomFinishVoice`). The cue is `roomCues()`'s like every cue of the final (`{sound: 'cheer', players, many}`, only for the screen and only for a page that saw the final begin); the player (`rooms/sound.ts`, `say()`) fetches the clips when it hears the final begin, only while its sound is on, keeps them decoded in memory, skips one that has not arrived `NAME_LEAD_MS` (250 ms) before its time, and stops them with the moment. A clip comes only from **`GET /rooms/{room}/members/{member}/voice`** (`rooms.members.voice`, `RoomMemberVoiceController`, `Rooms\Support\RoomMemberVoice`): ids only, `{member}` scoped to its room, `can:screen` (the host of an open room, who may open the screen); a removed row, a row without an account, or a name without a clip is a 404 before a file is read; the answer is `audio/mpeg` with `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `nosniff` and no header naming the file. The state does not change: a 404 is silence. Never at a reveal, in the lobby, for another place or on a phone.

**No promise.** No interface text, landing text or README says that a game improves memory, intelligence, attention or health; the games are named by what one does.

### Room content

`content/rooms/` (ADR 023, ADR 004), read by `ContentRepository` (`roomLevels()` with `roomLevel()` and `roomVersion()`, `roomWords()`, `roomCategories()`, `roomShapes()`, `roomSounds()`), cached with the rest by `content:cache`, and checked by `ContentValidator` with one broken fixture per rule:

| File                                  | Step             | Holds                                                                                                                                                                                                             | The validator requires                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `levels.json`                         | B3               | `version` (the room content's, kept as `content_version`); per level `mechanic`, `difficulty`, times and its numbers                                                                                              | a known mechanic; difficulty 1–4; one level per pair, and all four or none for each mechanic (`RoomPlayableTest` checks that each mechanic `RoomPlayable` lists has them, since the validator may not name `Rooms`); `answer_ms` 5 000–60 000; `study_ms` 1–60 000 for a memory mechanic and 0 otherwise; the numbers inside what the builder can build; no key it does not know |
| `shapes.json`, `assets/rooms/`        | B4               | `version` (its URLs carry it); `shapes`, each a `key` and an `image`: plain shapes of our own for memory rounds                                                                                                   | a non-empty list, keys unique; every file an SVG under `assets/rooms/` that exists, is used once, holds no text, is not another shape's drawing in other colours, passes the SVG safety check and has a `credits.json` entry (§8); no SVG of `assets/rooms/` outside the list; a row of shapes no longer than the list                                                           |
| `categories.json`                     | B5               | `version`; `groups`, each a `key` and its `words`: groups of words for `odd_one_out`, far apart in meaning                                                                                                        | at least 4 groups of at least 8 words, keys unique; every word one lowercase word that passes `WordRules`; a word is in exactly one group, by `Az::lower`; no word is a first name of `content/names.json`                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `words.json`                          | B6               | `version`; `words`, what the word games draw; `also_correct`, spellings that are correct too and never drawn                                                                                                      | lowercase words of 3–12 letters of the alphabet (`WordRules`) in Azerbaijani order, unique by `Az::lower`, none a first name of `content/names.json`; 60 words of every length a word level asks for, and 60 per `spelling` level that make three wrong spellings; each `also_correct` a spelling a listed word makes with its confusables; a credit                             |
| `sounds.json`, `assets/rooms/sounds/` | ADR 025, ADR 028 | `version` (its URLs carry it; 2 from ADR 028); `sounds`, one MP3 per `Content\Enums\RoomSound`, each played by one speaker (`RoomSound::speaker()`: the screen or a phone), made by `scripts/room-sounds/make.py` | every cue and no other; each an MP3 under `assets/rooms/sounds/` that exists, is used once, holds at most 64 KB (`MAX_ROOM_SOUND_BYTES`, fetched once sound is unlocked) and has a `credits.json` entry; each speaker's files together at most `MAX_ROOM_SPEAKER_BYTES` (the screen 320 KB, a phone 64 KB); no file of `assets/rooms/sounds/` outside the list                   |
| `rivalry.json`                        | P2               | `version` (its URLs carry it); `icons`: `clash`, `leader`, `overtake`, each a Fluent Emoji (Color) file under `assets/fluent/` (`26A1.svg`, `1F451.svg`, `1F525.svg`, unmodified, ADR 027 decision 10)            | exactly those three keys; each an SVG under `assets/fluent/` that exists and passes the SVG safety check; the files are covered by the Fluent entry of `credits.json`                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**No name in room content, and no voice but the taunts'.** The winner's cheer at the final (ADR 030) is not room content: the name is the clip of the built-in name list (§3 "Voice", `content/voice-names/`, made only by `content:voice`), which the screen gets by ids from `GET /rooms/{room}/members/{member}/voice`, and «Əla oynadın!» / «Əla oynadınız!» are the board's phrase clips (`content/assets/voice/`), whose URLs the screen's props carry. `content/rooms/` holds no name and no clip of a name, and `sounds.json` lists no name cue or voice. From T3 (ADR 029 as amended) the one voice in room content is the taunts': a clip per taunt in the board's voice under `content/assets/rooms/taunts/`, made only by `content:voice` (§3 "Voice"), named by `taunts.json` and never a room sound.

**The word list is our own** (ADR 023 decision 10): written in the repository under MIT with a `credits.json` entry "Elifba contributors" (`ContentValidator::CREDITED_LISTS`), starting from the words the theme packs already hold, reviewed by the owner (B7-O). Nothing reads the packs or a teacher's words at run time. Its `also_correct` holds the real words and word forms found among the spellings the confusable letters make of a listed word («babam», «durun», «arada») that are no word to draw: they are never offered as wrong, and an anagram accepts one its letters spell. The first stage plays only from a closed list, so a few hundred words are enough. A source from outside is copied only under a license `Content\Enums\License` allows and with its own credit: Wiktionary (CC BY-SA 4.0) would stay in `content/rooms/wiktionary/`; the Hunspell and Aspell dictionaries (GPL, MPL 2.0) and anything without a stated license are not copied.

### Adults' privacy

**Decision** (B2, B3, B7; ADR 024). In a room account holders see each other, so §7's rules get a third kind of data subject — one who uses the app and agrees for themselves.

| Rule                             | How                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
