    - _Finish line._ With the switch off, no page, prop, role card or route shows rooms and every room route is a 404. With it on, two accounts sit in one lobby and see each other's name, a third without the role is sent to take it, a removed one cannot return, and a guest board's bearer gets nothing; PHPStan level 8 passes with no ignore and no baseline; `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, first half: the area without rooms)._ Where the text above leaves a choice, B2 made it so:
        - **The switch first.** `rooms.enabled` is prepended to the middleware priority list before the cookies (`bootstrap/app.php`), so while the switch is off a room route answers as an unknown address does — a 404 to a visitor too, with no session cookie and no 419 for a missing CSRF token — before the login, a binding or the role could answer. `ELIFBA_ROOMS` is read as a yes or a no (`FILTER_VALIDATE_BOOL`: `off` is off). The group is the five middleware of §1; `RoleAreasTest` checks that `rooms.enabled` is on every player route and on no other, and `RoomsSwitchTest` that it runs first.
        - **`RoomsArea`** holds the area's few rules: `enabled()` (the one reader of `elifba.rooms.enabled`; `RoomsSwitchTest` checks that nothing else in `app/`, `routes/`, `bootstrap/` or `database/` names it), `holds(Route)` (the route names `role:player`), `toThePlayerRole()` and `pullIntended()` (the way in, below). `Role::available()`, `User::roleList()`, `RoleChoiceData::rules()` (`Rule::enum()->only()`) and `HandleInertiaRequests` (the route's own area) read the switch through it.
        - **The way in** (ADR 019 decision 6). The intended address is Laravel's `url.intended`, which login, registration, Google sign-in and e-mail confirmation already follow. A page visit of the player area by an account without a role is sent by `role.chosen` to `/role?role=player`; by an account with another role, by the 403 rule, to `/settings/roles?role=player`; both keep the page in `url.intended`. JSON and non-GET requests keep 409 and 403. Both pages take `?role=` as `chosen` (a role the page offers, else null): `/role` opens with that card picked, «Rollar» lists it first and puts its tick in focus. After the player role is granted, `RoleController` and `Settings\RolesController` go to `url.intended` only when it is an address of this app (`$request->root()` + `/`) that matches a route of the player area, and take it out of the session; otherwise they go where they went before, and a non-room address stays in the session. Taking another role never follows it.
        - **Settings in the player area** list profile, security, appearance and roles; «Oyun» stays in the teacher's and the parent's (ADR 019 decision 8). A page of the shared group (the board, the remote, `/pult`, a guest board's next game, «Oyun») is never in the player area: for an account whose `users.area` is `player` it shows the first of its teacher and parent areas (`HandleInertiaRequests`). The user menu's switch names roles, as it did, so the player's entry reads «Oyunçu» and opens «Otaqlar». The public pages' link to `/start` names the area's home (`areaHomeTitle`: «Siniflər», «Uşaqlarım», «Otaqlar»). The install banner does not show on `rooms/*` until B4 designs the player shell.
        - **Texts.** «Oyunçu» (`roles.player.*`) and its sentence (`roles.agreement.player`), `nav.rooms`, the plain `rooms.index.*`; `roles.choose.description`, `roles.errors.role` and `roles.settings.add.description` no longer count two roles.
        - **Tests.** `Tests\TestCase` sets the switch off whatever the shell or `.env` say; `tests/Support/Rooms.php` has `on()`, `routes()` (every route with `rooms.enabled`) and `uri()`. `RoleMigrationTest` rolls the player migration back before the roles migration it replays. `add_player_role`'s `down()` also moves an account in the player area to no area.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, second half: rooms and joining)._ Where the text above leaves a choice, B2 made it so:
        - **Routes and names.** `rooms.index`, `rooms.store` (`throttle:rooms-open`); `rooms.entrance` (`GET /otaq`), `rooms.join` (`GET /otaq/{code}`), `rooms.join.store` (`POST /otaq/{code}`), the three under one `throttle:room-join`; `rooms.show`, `rooms.state` (`throttle:room-state`), `rooms.leave`, `rooms.update`, `rooms.members.destroy`, `rooms.close`, `rooms.destroy`, `rooms.name.destroy`. `{room}` and `{member}` are `whereUlid`; `{code}` is six digits, so anything else at `/otaq/{code}` is a 404, and at `/otaq?code=` an error on `code` (`Rooms\Data\RoomCodeData`, `rooms.errors.code`). The `room-answers` limiter is defined now, for B3.
        - **`RoomPolicy`** has §12's three abilities and four that join one of them to the room's state, one per condition of the route table, so every condition is a policy and a refusal is a 403: `watch` (`host` or `play`: the state), `remove(room, member)` (`host`, and the row is not the host's own: a 403 for the host's row), `delete` (`host`, the room closed), `forget` (the room closed, the account has a row). None asks for a role. `{member}` binds through `Room::players()` (`childRouteBindingRelationshipName`), so a row of another room is a 404. `{room}` and the policies read a room as it is now: an idle room is closed for `delete` and `forget` before the scheduler writes it.
        - **The room's state decides what a host's tool does to a closed room** — an idle one too, by `isOpen()`, before the scheduler writes it: locking, removing and closing again change nothing and lead back (the page is the results by then), while `delete` and `forget` on an open room are refused by the policy. A locked or full room refuses a player who left as it refuses a new one: «Kilidlə» stops everyone coming in. A host who does not play may join their own room with its code and becomes a player. A host who takes their name out of a closed room keeps it as host; their row becomes «Oyunçu» with `host: false`.
        - **Binding before the role.** `{room}` binds before `role.chosen`, `verified` and `role:player` run, as `{class}` does (ADR 010), so an account without the player role is sent to take it from `/rooms` and `/otaq…` — the addresses an invitation carries — and gets a 404 for `/rooms/{room}` of a room it is not in. `JoinRoom`'s own not-found (a room closed, or the account removed, after the code was looked up) is answered by the controller as the same refusal as an unknown code (`rooms.errors.unknown` on `code`, §12 "Rooms and joining"), so every refusal has one body.
        - **Not signalled:** deleting an account. Its hosted rooms go and its rows elsewhere lose their account by the foreign keys, without a revision or `room.changed`, since nothing outside `Rooms` may call it; a page still open shows it at the next change, poll or reload.
        - **The revision** rises on a join (a return too), a leave, a removal, a change of the lock and the close — never on opening, reading, deleting or taking a name out; a join is the only one of them that is activity (`last_activity_at`). `Rooms\Support\RoomSignal::raise()` raises it on the locked row and sends `room.changed` with `DB::afterCommit` — after the commit, never on a rollback — reporting a failure as `RoomChangeNotBroadcast` (room id, revision, the cause's class).
        - **Locks.** `Rooms\Support\RoomLock`: `room()` (the row, read again `FOR UPDATE`, for every change of a room) and `host()` (the `users` row, by `Content\Support\TeacherLock`, for `OpenRoom`). `RoomCode::issue()` relies on the column's unique index alone, so a code still held by an idle room is skipped too.
        - **The channel.** `routes/channels.php` checks the switch (`RoomsArea::enabled()`), so that `Rooms` names nothing of `App\Support`; `RoomChannel::allows()` checks the role through `User::roleList()`, the open room and the host or a present player. Since the channel takes the `web` guard alone, a guest board's bearer is never read there: beside a session, the session answers.
        - **The state** is `Rooms\Data\RoomStateData` with `RoomSummaryData` (`room`), `RoomViewerData` (`me`) and `RoomPlayerData` (`players`) and the enum `Rooms\Enums\RoomStatus`; `game` and `round` are null and `ranking` is `[]`, and `fromArray()` refuses anything else until B3. Players are the rows not removed, by `joined_at`. The ids are Laravel's lowercase ULIDs, as in the fixture.
        - **The pages** (plain; B4 designs them). `rooms/index` gets `open` (rooms the account hosts or is present in: id, code, host, present players) and `past` (the others it is still in: id, the day by `DateLabel` in Baku time, host, players), newest first, nothing else (`Rooms\Support\RoomList`). `rooms/join` gets `code` (null at `/otaq`) and the account's `name`. `rooms/show` gets `room` (`id`, `status`), `state` (the `RoomState`, for the host and a present player of an open room and for everyone who was in a closed one; null for a player who left an open room) and `can` (`host`, `leave`, `delete`, `forget`). `resources/js/rooms/live.ts` holds `watchRoom()`: a higher revision fetches the state; a signal sent before the channel is subscribed would be lost, so the page polls every 3 s from the start until the subscription succeeds, fetches once on every subscription (the first and each after a reconnect), and polls again whenever the socket drops or the subscription fails; a fetched state older than the one shown is dropped. `resources/js/rooms/socket.ts`, beside it, is the Echo adapter with the session's XSRF token and one listener, `.room.changed`; nothing of `resources/js/game/` is imported.
        - **Tests.** `RoomsApartTest`'s "keeps the domains apart" checks the whole order of §1 for every file of `app/Domain`, `Rooms`' own list (`Language`, `Content`, `DenseRank`, `User`, `Role`), that nothing outside `app/Domain/Rooms` and `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms` names `Rooms`, and that `resources/js` imports `@/rooms` only from the room pages and nothing of the board's from them. `GameCatalogTest`'s "lists no room game" compares the catalog with the pinned list and `Mechanic` with the six room mechanics §12 names, or with `Content\Enums\RoomMechanic` once it exists — B3 drops the names. `TeacherIsolationTest`'s accounts A and B hold the player role with the switch on, A hosts an open room and a closed one; a case per room route, and "refuses a removed player on every room route". `BearerReachTest`'s world has an open room with a player, and every room route answers its bearer with the visitor's 401. `Tests\Support\Rooms` has `open()` and `join()` (through the actions); `playing()` is B3's.
- **B3 — The race, and the first game.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "The race", "Scoring and what is kept" (the rule, the ranking) and the B3 parts of "Room games" and "Room content": `room_games`, `room_rounds`, `room_answers`; `RoomFamily`, `RoomMechanic`, `content/rooms/levels.json` with the four levels of `recall_order` (digits and letters) and its validator rules; `StartRoomGame`, `EndRoomGame`, `RecordRoomAnswer`; `RoomClock`, `RoomTimetable`, `RoomJudge`, `RoomPointsRule`, `RoomRanking`, `RoomPlayable`, the whole `RoomState`; the contract with its fixtures on both sides; the early close in `LeaveRoom` and `RemoveRoomPlayer`. The room page plays `recall_order` plainly — enough to race in two browsers; B4 designs it.
    - _Files._ migration `create_room_games_tables`; `config/elifba.php` (`rooms.rounds`, `countdown_ms`, `reveal_ms`); `app/Domain/Content/Enums/{RoomFamily,RoomMechanic}.php`, `Content/Data/RoomLevelData.php`, `Content/Support/{ContentRepository,ContentValidator}.php`; `content/rooms/levels.json`; `tests/Fixtures/content/rooms/**` and `broken.json`; `app/Domain/Rooms/Actions/{StartRoomGame,EndRoomGame,RecordRoomAnswer,LeaveRoom,RemoveRoomPlayer,CloseRoom}.php`, `Rooms/Models/{RoomGame,RoomRound,RoomAnswer}.php`, `Rooms/Data/*`, `Rooms/Support/{RoomClock,RoomTimetable,RoomState,RoomJudge,RoomPointsRule,RoomRanking,RoomPlayable}.php`, `Rooms/Support/Mechanics/{RoomRoundBuilder,RecallOrderBuilder}.php`; `routes/rooms.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/{RoomGameController,RoomAnswerController}.php`; `resources/js/rooms/{contract,clock,live}.ts`, `components/rooms/mechanics/recall-order.tsx`, `pages/rooms/show.tsx`; `tests/Fixtures/rooms/*`; factories; `lang/*`.
    - _Tests._ `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomTimetableTest.php` ("derives every phase from the start, the durations and the facts", "opens the next round a reveal after an early close", "is over at the host's end", "is over by itself after the last reveal"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomJudgeTest.php` ("judges a choice, a set and a sequence from the shared cases", "takes tiles that say the same as interchangeable"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomPointsRuleTest.php` ("gives 20 at once and 10 at the bell", "gives nothing for a wrong answer", "never goes below zero or above 20"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/RecallOrderBuilderTest.php` ("shows one kind per round within the level's length", "never asks in the order shown", "builds the same rounds from the same seed"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomContractTest.php` ("round-trips every fixture", "has no solution in any state before the reveal"); `tests/Feature/Rooms/RoomRaceTest.php` ("serves nothing of a round before it opens", "serves the study material only while it is studied", "serves the question only from the answer phase", "never serves the solution or a result before the round closes", "counts each player's answer once, whatever is sent twice", "refuses a second answer of the same player", "refuses an answer after the bell", "refuses items that are not the round's", "measures the time on the server's clock", "never says right or wrong in the receipt", "closes the round when every present player has answered", "does not wait for a player who left or was removed", "lets a late joiner answer the open round", "runs to its end with nobody watching", "ends when the host ends it", "starts no second game while one is running", "ends the game when the room closes"); `tests/Feature/Rooms/RoomRankingTest.php` ("sums only closed rounds", "shares a place on a tie and skips none", "orders names inside a place by the Azerbaijani alphabet", "leaves out a removed player", "keeps a nameless player's points"); `RoomSignalsTest` ("signals a game's start, every answer and an early close"); `RoomsApartTest` ("leaves the game tables untouched by a whole room game"); `ContentValidatorTest` (a broken fixture per `levels.json` rule) and `ContentRepositoryTest` ("reads the room levels"); Vitest `resources/js/rooms/contract.test.ts` (every fixture; "refuses a solution outside the reveal") and `clock.test.ts` ("estimates the server's clock from a state", "asks again at each moment of the timetable", "polls when there is no socket").
    - _Finish line._ Two accounts in two browsers play a whole `recall_order` game and see the same ranking; a third joins in the middle; the network tab of a playing browser shows no solution before a reveal; `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 1 of 3: everything of a game that needs no request)._ The tables with their models and factories, the room content and its validator, the clock, the timetable, the judge, the points rule, `RoomPlayable` and the `recall_order` builder; no action, route, state or page yet. Where the text above leaves a choice, B3 made it so:
        - **The level's key is the pair.** `levels.json` gives a level no key of its own, and one mechanic has one level per difficulty, so `room_games` stores `difficulty` (tinyint 1–4) beside `mechanic` where §1 had `level` (string); the state's `game.difficulty` reads it, and a game keeps its difficulty whatever the file says later. `ContentRepository::roomLevel(mechanic, difficulty)` finds the level, `roomVersion()` is `levels.json`'s `version` (the game's `content_version`); the compiled file holds the file under `rooms.levels`.
        - **The enums.** `RoomFamily` has its three families now; `RoomMechanic` has `recall_order` only and grows with B4–B6, as their file lists say, each case arriving with its builder and its validator rules. `GameCatalogTest`'s "lists no room game" reads `RoomMechanic::cases()` instead of six names, and also checks that no game or card of the catalog is a room mechanic, so it covers each mechanic as it comes. `Content\Enums\RoomItemKind` holds the six kinds of `Item.kind`; `RoomMechanic::kinds()` lists what a level may name — `digit` and `letter` for `recall_order`, until B4 adds `shape` and B6 `word` — and `family()`, `studies()` say what a mechanic is.
        - **"All four" without naming `Rooms`.** `ContentValidator` (in `Content`) gives every mechanic that has a level all four difficulties; `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomPlayableTest.php` ("lists only mechanics with all four levels", "has a builder for every mechanic") checks that each mechanic `RoomPlayable` lists has levels. `RoomPlayable::mechanics()`, `plays()`, `builder()`; it lists `recall_order` now, whose view part 3 builds.
        - **`reveal_ms` is 5 000** (ADR 021 gives no number): long enough to read the solution, one's own points, the round's results and the ranking on a phone. _3 000 from 2026-11-18, the owner's decision with ADR 021's amendment; a game keeps the reveal it started with._ `rooms.rounds` 5–20 and `countdown_ms` 3 000 are §12's.
        - **The round's data** is `Rooms\Data\{RoomItemData, RoomStudyData, RoomAskData, RoomSolutionData}` with the enums `RoomAnswerShape`, `RoomLayout` and `RoomPhase`; a builder returns `BuiltRoomRoundData` (study, ask, solution, `studyMs`, `answerMs`), whose `payload()` is `room_rounds.payload` = `{study, ask}` and whose solution is `room_rounds.solution` = `{shape, ids, accept?}`. `RoomRound::studyData()`, `askData()`, `solutionData()` read them back; `solution` is `$hidden`. `RoomItemData::says()` is what a sequence compares: the text, else the asset, else the id. Part 2 builds the state's `RoomRoundData` and `RoomRevealData` from these.
        - **The timetable** is `RoomTimetable::from(started, reveal, ended, rounds)`, `::of($game)` (the game's rounds by position, loaded if they are not) and `::at($game, $nowMs)`, which gives `RoomMomentData` (`phase`, `round`; `isOver()`, `answers($round)`); `opens()`, `answerFrom()`, `bell()`, `closes()`, `next()` (when the round's reveal ends) and `overMs()` give the moments. Each bound belongs to the later phase (at the bell the round is revealed). Two guards keep the moments in order whatever a row holds: `closed_ms` counts only inside its round's answer phase, and an `ended_ms` after the timetable's own end is the end. `RoomGame::timetable()` is a shortcut.
        - **The clock** is `RoomClock::nowMs()` (`CarbonImmutable::now()`, so `travelTo` moves it to the millisecond) and `RoomClock::at($ms)` (the moment as a date, for `travelTo`); `RoomState`'s `server_ms` reads it. Timestamps that scoring never reads (`joined_at`, `closed_at`…) stay `now()`.
        - **Points** are whole-number arithmetic (`intdiv`), with `elapsed_ms` held to 0…`answer_ms`; a round without time to answer is refused.
        - **Ids.** A row and its question get separate ids, so no answerable id is ever shown in the order to recall; whether sorted ids happen to fall into the order to tap is chance (`RecallOrderBuilderTest` checks that it is not the rule).
        - **Content.** `content/rooms/levels.json` (version 1): «Asan» digits, 3–4, 3 s to study, 10 s to answer; «Orta» digits and letters, 5–6, 4.5 s, 15 s; «Çətin» 7–8, 6 s, 20 s; «Çox çətin» 9–10, 7 s, 25 s — the owner may tune them as data. The fixture root's file is `tests/Fixtures/content/base/rooms/levels.json`, inside the valid root `ContentFixture` copies, rather than a `tests/Fixtures/content/rooms/` beside it; `broken.json` has a case per rule (21, `rooms levels: …`). The rule "`study_ms` 0 for a mechanic that is not a memory game" is in the validator but gets its fixture with B5's first such mechanic.
        - **Factories.** `RoomGame::factory()` is the room's next game (`position`), started now at `recall_order` «Asan» with the configured times; `->withRounds($n)` builds its rounds with the builder from its seed, `->ended()` ends it. `RoomRound::factory()` is a built «Asan» round (`->built($round)`, `->closedAt($ms)`). `RoomAnswer::factory()` is a right answer 2.5 s in by a new present player of the round's room (`->by($row)`, `->wrong()`). A test reaches a phase with `$this->travelTo(RoomClock::at($game->timetable()->answerFrom($i)))`. Relations: `Room::games()`, `RoomGame::room()`, `rounds()` (by position), `RoomRound::game()`, `answers()`, `RoomAnswer::round()`, `player()`, `RoomPlayer::answers()`.
        - **Tests.** The four unit tests named above (with "has a right and a wrong case for every shape", "takes now only from the room clock…" and "builds only a level of its own with rows it can shuffle" besides), `RoomPlayableTest`, `tests/Feature/Rooms/RoomGameTablesTest.php` (the columns, whole milliseconds beyond 32 bits on both engines, the solution in no serialization, one answer per player per round and per event, a game at any moment by its timetable), `ContentRepositoryTest` "reads the room levels", `ContentCommandsTest` (the compiled room levels), `ContentValidatorTest` (the broken cases), `GameCatalogTest`, and `RoomLifetimeTest`'s pruning and account deletion over a played game's rows. `resources/js/rooms/contract.test.ts` leaves `judge.json` out of its strict state parse, as the board's test does for its own; part 2 reads its items with the `Item` schema.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 2 of 3: the race on the server)._ `StartRoomGame`, `EndRoomGame`, `RecordRoomAnswer`, the early close in `LeaveRoom` and `RemoveRoomPlayer`, the game `CloseRoom` ends, `RoomRanking`, the whole `RoomState` with its DTOs, schemas and fixtures, and the three routes; no page plays yet (part 3). Where the text above leaves a choice, B3 made it so:
        - **Routes and policies.** `rooms.games.store` (`POST /rooms/{room}/games`, `RoomPolicy::start`: the host of an open room with no game running), `rooms.game.destroy` (`DELETE /rooms/{room}/game`, `end`: the host of an open room while a game runs), `rooms.answers.store` (`POST /rooms/{room}/answers`, `play`, `throttle:room-answers`); `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/{RoomGameController,RoomAnswerController}.php`. "Running" is `Room::runningGame($nowMs)`: the latest game (`Room::latestGame()`, by `position`) until its timetable says it is over. `StartRoomGame` checks again under the room's lock and refuses with the policy's 403 (`AuthorizationException`), so two starts at once make one game; `EndRoomGame` on a game already over changes nothing. Starting and ending answer 204; the receipt is JSON with `no-store`. `RoomGameSetupData` takes `mechanic` and `difficulty` of a level `RoomPlayable` plays and `rounds` 5–20; a pair that is no level is an error on `difficulty`.
        - **The answer's checks** run in §12's order under the room's lock and then the round's (`RoomLock::round()`, taken only by a holder of the room's row): `duplicate` is any stored `event_id`, whoever's (an id never leaves its device, so this says nothing); `answered` is this account's row (not removed) with an answer to that round id; `late` covers a player who left or was removed meanwhile, a round of another room or game, and anything outside the answer phase; then 422 on `picked` (`rooms.errors.picked`) for a wrong count or an id that is not the question's — before the question a foreign id is only `late`, so nothing is learnt of a round early. An id picked twice is the round's own and the right count: judged, and wrong. The receipt's moment is `RoomClock::nowMs()` read as soon as the room's lock is held, so an answer that waited for the lock past the bell is `late`, and nothing is stored after a reveal may have been served. Ids are read in lower case, as rows store them. A body's other keys (a player, a time) are never read; the size of `picked` is checked before its items, so a huge list costs nothing. The same `event_id` sent to two rooms at once (only a device's own fault) stores one and answers the other `duplicate` instead of a unique-index error.
        - **The early close** (`Rooms\Support\RoomRoundClose`) counts the present rows at that moment: a host who does not play has none, a late joiner counts from the join. It is checked after an answer, a leave, a removal and a return (`JoinRoom`: a player who had answered comes back to a round nobody else is in). A round with no present player left does not close early — it runs to its bell, as the rounds after it do; there is nobody to show it to sooner. One leave, removal or return that also closes the round raises the revision once. An account deleted mid-round is not seen (nothing outside `Rooms` may call it, and B2 left account deletion unsignalled): its round runs to its bell. A closed round stays closed for answers whatever the clock does later (`closed_ms` set → `late`).
        - **Ending** sets `ended_ms` to now; `CloseRoom::close()` (the host's close and `CloseIdleRooms`) ends a running game the same way inside its one revision. A round that the end cut off never counts in a ranking and its reveal is never served; the state is `over` at once.
        - **The ranking** (`RoomRanking::ofGame()`, `ofRoom()`): a round counts once its close (the early close or the bell) is at or before now and before the game's end, by the timetable — a round whose close is the end itself was never revealed and counts no more than one the end cut off. The rows are the room's players not removed, nameless ones included; points are summed in PHP over `room_answers` rows. Before the first game the state's ranking is `[]` (B2's lobby is unchanged); once a game is over it is the room's standing, which lists every player, those without points after the places.
        - **The state per phase** (`Rooms\Data\{RoomGameData, RoomRoundData, RoomRevealData, RoomMineData, RoomResultData, RoomRankedPlayerData}`, enums `RoomGamePhase`, `RoomAnswerStatus`): `closes_ms` and `next_ms` are null until the reveal, since an early close may come before the bell; `answered` is false and `answered_count` 0 before the answer phase; `answered_count` counts present players who answered; `reveal.results` lists the players (not removed) who answered, in the players' order — no answer, no result; `mine` is null without an answer; `word` is null until B6. A closed room, an idle one too, has no live game. Every DTO refuses a key in the wrong phase, and `RoomStateData` a round without a live game; zod has the same as a union by `phase` and a union of the three state shapes. `rooms/show` also gets `place_labels` (as many as `max(max_players, the room's rows not removed)`).
        - **The seed** (`Rooms\Support\RoomSeed`) is never served (`RoomGame::$hidden`; `RoomRound::$hidden` holds `payload` beside `solution`, so no serialization of a round carries its material or question). The rounds are built from a keyed source, `Rooms\Support\RoomRandomEngine` — HMAC-SHA-256 of a counter, 8 bytes a step, under a key derived from the seed and `app.key`: a 31-bit seed alone could be found by trying every one against round 0's shown items and ids, and a statistical generator's state can be worked back from enough of its output (each id character shows 6 of its bits); either would tell the later rounds before they open. The same seed on the same instance builds the same rounds (`RoomSeedTest`); the factory builds from the same source.
        - **Rows without a repeated item** (a departure from part 1, changed above in "Room games"): the reviewer for leaks found that a repeated item and the rule "never beside its equal" let the question alone decide the order in about 6 % of «Asan» rounds. `RecallOrderBuilder` now draws a row's items without repeating one, `RoomItemKind::distinct()` says how many a kind has (10 digits, the alphabet's letters), `ContentValidator` refuses a row longer than that (`broken.json`: "a row of digits longer than the digits") and a row shorter than three (`MIN_ROOM_ROW`), since two items asked in another order are the row reversed. `content/rooms/levels.json` needs no change (3–10); the fixture root's levels are 3–3 digits and 12 letters.
        - **Tests.** `RoomRaceTest` (the 17 cases above, plus "closes the round when a player who answered comes back to it alone", "takes the player from the session, never from the body" and the limiter), `RoomRankingTest` (5), `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomSeedTest.php`, `RecallOrderBuilderTest` (no item twice; a row of two and more digits than there are refused), `ContentValidatorTest` on the new case, `RoomGameTablesTest` (no `payload` in a serialization), `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomContractTest.php` (both, with "has every phase's fixture"), `RoomSignalsTest` "signals a game's start, every answer and an early close", `RoomsApartTest` "leaves the game tables untouched by a whole room game"; the three routes in `TeacherIsolationTest` (A also hosts a factory-made room with a game running, since one open room per host is `OpenRoom`'s rule and ending needs a running game), `BearerReachTest`, `RoleAreasTest` and `RoomsSwitchTest`, whose snapshots now hold the game tables. `tests/Support/Rooms.php` has `playing()`, `at()`, `round()`, `right()`, `wrong()` and `answer()`. Fixtures `state.{countdown,study,answer,reveal,over}.json`, `answer.json`, `receipt.json`; `contract.test.ts` parses every one and has "refuses a solution outside the reveal".
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 3 of 3: the room page plays)._ The page plays `recall_order` plainly; B4 designs it. Where the text above leaves a choice, B3 made it so:
        - **The page by phase** (`pages/rooms/show.tsx`, `components/rooms/{live-round,standing,game-setup}.tsx`, `use-server-now.ts`, `mechanics/recall-order.tsx`). The lobby: the code, the players, the host's tools and the host's «Yeni oyun». The countdown: the seconds until `opens_ms`. The study: the row, drawn only from a state in `study` and only while the estimated server clock is before `answer_from_ms`. The answer: the question's items in the server's order; each tap adds one, «Geri» takes back the last, and the answer is sent once it holds `ask.pick` items; the count of present players who answered. The reveal: the right order as the question's items, the viewer's own answer, right or wrong with its points and time, each player's result, the game's ranking. Over: the room's standing and, for the host, «Yeni oyun» again. The host also has «Oyunu bitir» while a game runs; a host who does not play sees the rounds without buttons. The seconds are the estimated server clock, for show only. A mechanic's view is one entry of `Record<RoomMechanic, …>` in `live-round.tsx` (study, question, reveal), so B4 adds `recall_cells` by its view.
        - **`setup`** in the props of `rooms/show`: `Rooms\Data\RoomGameSetupData::options()` — each mechanic `RoomPlayable` lists with the difficulties of its levels, and `rounds` `{min, max}` — for the host of an open room, null for everyone else, so the page names only what `rules()` accepts. The rounds start at the minimum. Starting and ending go over `fetch` (they answer 204), and the page then fetches the state.
        - **The server's clock** (`resources/js/rooms/clock.ts`, `serverClock()`): each fetched state bounds the difference of the clocks to [`server_ms` − received, `server_ms` − sent] on the browser's monotonic clock; the bounds of every state are intersected and the estimate is their middle, and a state whose bounds miss the others' (the device slept, its clock jumped) starts it again. A state of the props has no send time and gives only the lower bound, so the page fetches once on mount.
        - **When the page asks** (`followRoom()`): one planned request at a time, at the end of the shown phase plus 60 ms (`LATE_MS`); told the same phase after its end, again after 250, 500, 1 000, 2 000 and then every 3 000 ms (`AGAIN_MS`); without the channel also every 3 s where `pollsWithoutChannel()` says so — the lobby, a game that is over, a round the viewer answered, and every round for a host who does not play. A request that failed, whoever made it (a signal, a subscription, an answer's receipt, the host's start), is made again at the same steps until a state is shown: the browser check found a page left in the lobby by a failed fetch after the start's signal, until the next signal (fixed, with "asks again when a request fails, whoever made it" first). Requests give up after 10 s (`resources/js/rooms/http.ts`). A fetched state replaces the shown one only with a higher revision, or the same revision and a later `server_ms`, since a phase that time changes raises no revision. `watchRoom()` takes `onListening`: given it, it reports whether the channel is heard and leaves the polling to `followRoom()`; without it, it polls as B2 built it.
        - **The answer** (`resources/js/rooms/answer.ts`): `eventId()` is a lowercase ULID whose time part is the estimated server clock, never the device's; one per answer, sent again unchanged. `sendAnswer()` sends the same body again after 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 s when nothing usable comes back (no connection, a server error, the limiter) while the page still shows that round's answer phase, then offers «Yenidən göndər» with the same body. `accepted` and `duplicate` read «Cavabınız qəbul olundu», `answered` «Bu raunda artıq cavab vermisiniz», `late` «Vaxt bitmişdi»; a 422 lets the player choose again; 401, 403, 404 and 419 reload the page. Right or wrong is read only from the reveal.
        - **Texts.** `rooms.mechanics.recall_order.{name,line,study,ask}`, `rooms.difficulty.{1,2,3,4}` («Asan», «Orta», «Çətin», «Çox çətin»), `rooms.setup.*`, `rooms.game.*`; no text promises a benefit. The time of an answer is formatted by `Intl.NumberFormat` in the page's locale; the places come from `place_labels`.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, three isolated browser contexts, a scratch SQLite database and server with `ELIFBA_ROOMS` passed as an environment variable, a Reverb of its own): three player accounts; the host opened a room, the second joined with the code, the host started «Orta» with 6 rounds; both saw the countdown, every study, question and reveal; the third joined with the code during round 3's study and answered it while it was open; round 5 ran with Reverb stopped and every page still went from study to answer to reveal by the timetable (the failed broadcast was reported as `RoomChangeNotBroadcast` and the write kept); all three ended on the same ranking. Every room response the second and third contexts received (39 and 26: the page, its Inertia visits, `/state`, `/answers`) was checked: no reveal, solution, right or wrong, result or `study` before its phase, no ordered solution early, no address but the viewer's own. With several PHP workers on SQLite (no busy timeout), the `database` cache store's limiter writes sometimes answered "database is locked" — the dev stack only; the page's retries now ride it out.
        - **Tests.** Vitest `resources/js/rooms/clock.test.ts` ("estimates the server's clock from a state", "asks again at each moment of the timetable", "polls when there is no socket", and "takes a state of the props as read when it was there…", "starts again when a state misses the bounds of the others", "ends each phase at its moment of the timetable", "asks again shortly, a little later each time, when it was early", "asks again when a request fails, whoever made it…", "stops"), `answer.test.ts` (one event id on every retry, each receipt passed on as given, no retry of a refusal or a room that is gone, no receipt that says more), `live.test.ts` ("tells a page that polls by itself whether it hears the channel…"); Pest `RoomRaceTest` "offers the setup of a game to the host of an open room alone, as the server accepts it".
- **B4 — The phone pages, the screen and the second memory game.**
    - _Scope._ The player area's interface, phone first: «Otaqlar» (open a room, the code field, the account's open and past rooms), the join page, the room page (the lobby with the code and its QR, the host's tools; the setup of a game: family, game, difficulty, rounds; the countdown, the study and the answer views with the time bar; the reveal with one's own points; the ranking; the end of a game and the room's standing), a closed room's results with «Adımı sil» and the host's «Otağı sil», the screen page; `recall_cells` and the shapes for `recall_order`, proving that a mechanic is a builder, a shape and a view. No server rule changes but the new mechanic.
    - _Files._ `resources/js/pages/rooms/{index,join,show,screen}.tsx`, `components/rooms/*`, `components/rooms/mechanics/{recall-order,recall-cells}.tsx`; `app/Domain/Content/Enums/RoomMechanic.php`, `Rooms/Support/Mechanics/RecallCellsBuilder.php`, `Rooms/Support/RoomPlayable.php`; `content/rooms/{levels,shapes}.json`, `content/assets/rooms/*.svg`, `content/credits.json`, `CREDITS.md` (generated); `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/RoomScreenController.php`; `docs/frontend.md` (rule 15, the player shell); `lang/*`.
    - _Tests._ `tests/Feature/Rooms/RoomPagesTest.php` ("lists an account's open and past rooms and nobody else's", "gives a closed room its games and rankings by name", "sends no account id, address or solution to any room page", "takes a player's name out of a closed room and keeps the points", "deletes a closed room for everyone", "gives the screen only to the host"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/RecallCellsBuilderTest.php` ("lights fewer cells than the grid has", "asks for exactly the lit cells"); `RoomJudgeTest` on the `set` cases; `ContentValidatorTest` (the shapes rules) and `CreditsDocumentTest`; Vitest `resources/js/rooms/clock.test.ts` ("draws the same bar on a slow clock") and `components/rooms/standing.test.ts` ("shows a shared place once").
    - _Finish line._ Checked in a browser at phone width (375 px) with three accounts on three devices or profiles, and the screen on a laptop: open a room, join by QR and by typed code, play both memory games at two difficulties, read the ranking, close the room, read its results, take a name out; with screenshots.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 1 of 2: the server side)._ The second memory game, the shapes, the screen's route and the pages' data, with plain views that play; part 2 designs the pages. No server rule changed but the new mechanic. Where the text above leaves a choice, B4 made it so:
        - **`recall_cells`** (`RoomMechanic::RecallCells`, `Rooms\Support\Mechanics\RecallCellsBuilder`). A level writes `grid` and `lit` as objects, like `length` (`Content\Data\GridData`, `RangeData`), where "Room games" first wrote pairs. Each side of a grid is 2–6 (`ContentValidator::MIN_ROOM_GRID`, `MAX_ROOM_GRID`): one row would be a row. The question is built first — every cell in reading order, each with a new id — and only then are the count lit, the lit cells and the study's ids drawn, so the question's ids, order and every other key are the same whatever was lit, and only `pick` says how many, which the study showed. The study holds the lit cells only, in reading order. `RoomMechanic::kinds()` is empty for it: its items are cells, not a kind a level names. The four levels: «Asan» 3 × 3 with 3–4 lit, 2 s to study, 10 s to answer; «Orta» 4 × 4, 5–6, 2.5 s, 15 s; «Çətin» 5 × 5, 7–8, 3 s, 20 s; «Çox çətin» 6 × 6, 9–11, 3.5 s, 25 s — the owner may tune them as data.
        - **Shapes** (`content/rooms/shapes.json`, version 1; `ContentRepository::roomShapes()`, `Content\Data\RoomShapeData`). Twelve own SVGs in Fluent's manner (§8: the 32 grid, a radial light → base → shade fill and a shade from the bottom right, one blurred highlight, no outline), each in a colour of its own and told apart by its form: dairə, kvadrat, üçbucaq, ulduz, ürək, romb, altıbucaq, aypara, damcı, ox, bulud, ildırım (keys and files in ASCII). Credited MIT, "Elifba contributors". A shape item has `text` null and `asset` the file's URL with `shapes.json`'s `version`, so a sequence's reading (`RoomItemData::says()`) is the picture. The validator refuses a `<text>`, `<tspan>` or `<textPath>`, and two files that are the same drawing once their colours and the ids their file names prefix are left out; it counts the shapes in the root it checks, so `RoomItemKind::distinct()` stays null for them. `RoomPlayable::builder()` gives `RecallOrderBuilder` the shapes.
        - **The content version.** `levels.json` is version 2 (ADR 004: bumped on every change): the four `recall_cells` levels, and «Orta», «Çətin», «Çox çətin» of `recall_order` name `shape` beside digits and letters («Asan» stays digits). A game keeps the version it was built with; `shapes.json` has a version of its own for its URLs, as `sounds.json` has.
        - **The screen** (`rooms.screen`, `RoomScreenController`, `RoomPolicy::screen`: the host of an open room, playing or not). Props: `room`, `state` (the host's `RoomState`), `place_labels`, `join`. A closed or idle room's screen is a 403, a room the account is not in a 404. The page follows the state as the room page does; both use `components/rooms/use-live-room.ts`, taken out of `rooms/show` unchanged but for `onClosed`: a screen still open when the room closes goes to the room's page, its results, instead of reloading into the 403.
        - **The join link** (`Rooms\Support\RoomJoinLink`): `{url, entry_url}` — `{APP_URL}/otaq/{code}` and `{APP_URL}/otaq`, on `APP_URL` as a pairing card's are (§4) — on the screen, and as `rooms/show`'s `join` for whoever gets the live state of an open room (null otherwise). The lobby draws its QR with `qrcode.react`.
        - **Past rooms** (`RoomList`): a past room adds `games` (`{mechanic, difficulty}`, in order) and `mine` — the account's points and its place in the room's standing (`RoomRanking::ofRoom()` at the request, labelled by `Ordinal::label()`; place null without points), null for a host who did not play. The rooms come with their rows (not removed), games and rounds at once, and each past room asks once more for its answers' points; `RoomRanking::ofRoom()` uses the games when they are loaded.
        - **A closed room's results** (`Rooms\Support\RoomResults::games()`): `rooms/show` gets `games`, each `{id, mechanic, difficulty, rounds, ranking}` with the ranking of `RoomRanking::ofGame()` as the state's rows, for a closed room, and null for an open one. The page names the rows from `state.players`, so a name taken out is null and reads «Oyunçu». The place labels moved to `Rooms\Support\RoomPlaces::labels()`.
        - **The views** (plain): `mechanics/recall-cells.tsx` (the lit grid, the dark grid to tap, the lit cells with the viewer's own taps), `recall-order.tsx` draws a shape's picture, and `components/rooms/mechanics/views.ts` holds `MECHANIC_VIEWS`, through which the room page and the screen draw a round. «Otaqlar» shows a past room's games and place, a closed room each game's ranking, the lobby the QR and the host's «Böyük ekran».
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, three isolated contexts at 375 px and the host's screen at 1280 px, a scratch SQLite database and `php -S` with `ELIFBA_ROOMS` passed as an environment variable, Reverb refusing the 127.0.0.1 origin, so the pages polled): a room opened, two players joined with its code, «Xanaları yadda saxla» «Orta» and «Ardıcıllığı yadda saxla» «Çətin» played round by round by tapping the DOM — two players right, one wrong, every round judged as tapped, rows of shapes among them — then closed; the screen went to the results, «Otaqlar» showed both games with the place, and a name taken out read «Oyunçu» with its points. Joining by QR and the page design are part 2's.
        - **Texts.** `rooms.mechanics.recall_cells.{name,line,study,ask}` («Xanaları yadda saxla»), `recall_order.line` with shapes; `rooms.game.{picked,lit_cells,yours_marked}`, `rooms.show.{qr,games,rounds,screen}`, `rooms.screen.*`, `rooms.index.{place,no_points}`.
        - **Tests.** `tests/Unit/Rooms/RecallCellsBuilderTest.php` (the two named, "asks the same question whichever cells were lit", "builds the same rounds from the same seed", "builds only a level of its own with a cell left dark"); `RecallOrderBuilderTest` (a level of every shape, "shows a shape as its picture, with no text, and judges a row of shapes by the pictures"); `tests/Feature/Rooms/RoomPagesTest.php` (the six named); `RoomRaceTest` ("plays a game of cells to its ranking and names no lit cell before the reveal", "plays a row of shapes and judges it by their pictures", and the setup offers both mechanics); `judge.json` (two sequences of shapes); the fixtures `state.{study,answer,reveal}.cells.json` and `state.study.shapes.json` in `RoomContractTest` and `contract.test.ts`; `ContentValidatorTest` (24 broken cases: 9 for grids, lit cells and rows of shapes, 15 for `shapes.json` and its files; "shapes before the shapes" went), `ContentRepositoryTest` ("reads the room levels" with both mechanics, "reads the room shapes"), `RoomPlayableTest`, the screen in `TeacherIsolationTest` and `RoleAreasTest`, `OpenRoomTest` (a past room's new keys). `Tests\Support\Rooms::playing()` takes a mechanic, and `wrong()` answers a set.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 2 of 2: the pages)._ The player area's interface, phone first, by `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 (the player shell), written before the pages. No server rule, contract, judge, points, clock or timetable changed. Where the text above leaves a choice, B4 made it so:
        - **The pages.** «Otaqlar» (`rooms/index`): the account's open room first, with «Otağa keç»; «Otağa qoşul», the code field; «Otaq aç» with «Mən də oynayıram», not offered while the account hosts an open room; «Keçmiş otaqlar» with each room's games as chips and the account's own place and points. The entrance (`rooms/join`): the code, the account's name as the room will show it with «Dəyiş» (Settings → Profil), «Qoşul» in a bar at the bottom. The room page (`rooms/show`): the lobby (the code card with its QR, the players, «Otağın idarəsi» — lock, «Böyük ekran», «Otağı bağla» — and a player's «Otaqdan çıx»), the host's «Yeni oyun» or a player's waiting line in `RoomActionBar`; the setup in a dialog, full screen on a phone (the games under their family, the difficulty as four segments with what the level holds, the rounds as 5, 10, 15, 20); a round with a compact head (the round, the game, the code chip, the time bar and the seconds) that fits 375 × 667 with the shell around it; the reveal with the viewer's verdict, the solution beside the viewer's answer, and the ranking with each player's result of the round; after a game «Oyun bitdi» and the room's standing; a closed room's results with each game's ranking opening under it, «Adımı sil» and the host's «Otağı sil». The screen (`rooms/screen`): no shell (`app.tsx`), sizes in `vh`, the QR and the code while the room waits, the round as the players see it with «:count / :total» answers, the reveal beside the ranking, «Oyun bitdi» with a small QR for whoever comes next, «Tam ekran» and «Otağa qayıt»; nothing on it writes. Closing, ending a game, removing a player, deleting and taking a name out ask in `RoomConfirm` (44px buttons, a form or a `fetch` callback); leaving does not.
        - **A `set` answer can be taken back tap by tap** (`components/rooms/taps.ts`, `tap()` and `untap()`): a second tap on a chosen cell un-chooses it, until the tap that makes `ask.pick` cells, which sends the answer; there is no «Göndər». A `sequence` keeps «Geri», which takes back the last item, and fills a row of slots above the items. One attempt, all or nothing, as before: nothing can be taken back once sent. A muted line says that a second tap takes a cell back, once a cell is chosen.
        - **The time bar** (`TimeBar`) is `timeLeft(from, to, now)` of the stretch `barStretch(round)` gives — the study from `opens_ms` to `answer_from_ms`, the answer to the bell, the reveal from `closes_ms` to `next_ms`, none in the countdown — on the estimated server clock (`resources/js/rooms/clock.ts`), redrawn every 200 ms with a linear transition, red in the last 3 s of an answer. It decides nothing.
        - **The ranking** (`Standing`) gathers the rows of one place under its label once (`components/rooms/standing-groups.ts`, `standingGroups()`), players without points last under «–»; in a reveal each row also has the player's result of the round from `reveal.results` (✓ +points, ✗ +0, «—» without an answer). The place's label is not set in tabular figures: Inter's `tnum` widens its hyphen.
        - **The setup's prop.** `RoomGameSetupData::options()` gives each mechanic also its `family` and `levels` — per difficulty `study_ms`, `answer_ms`, `count` (`{min, max}`: a row's length, the lit cells) and `grid` — so the setup names the family and says what a level holds («3 × 3 cədvəl, 3–4 xana · 2 san. baxmaq · 10 san. cavab»); `difficulties` stays. The rounds still start at the minimum.
        - **Seconds** (`components/rooms/seconds.ts`) are written with `rooms.decimal_mark` («,» in Azerbaijani, "." in English), not `Intl.NumberFormat`: Chrome's bundled ICU has no Azerbaijani number symbols and writes `new Intl.NumberFormat('az').format(3.3)` as «3.3» (Node writes «3,3»).
        - **Colours and symbols.** Right is `emerald-600` (`emerald-400` dark), wrong `red-600` (`red-400` dark) on a `destructive` tint — the dark `destructive` token is too dark for text — a lit cell `amber-400`, the viewer's taps `primary`; right, wrong and waiting always carry a word with `Check`, `X` or `Hourglass`. (§8's "state symbols" are the State Flag and the national symbols; none is used in a room.)
        - **The install banner** shows on `rooms/index` only (`bannerAllowedOn()`); a banner over «Qoşul» or a round would hide the action of the moment.
        - **Hooks for checks.** A round's section carries `data-phase` and `data-round` (the room page and the screen), a cell `data-cell="col:row"` and a lit study cell `data-lit`; they say nothing the page does not show.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, as B3 part 3 did it): a git worktree of this change built with `VITE_REVERB_*` pointing at a Reverb of its own (`127.0.0.1:8093`), a scratch SQLite database and `php -S 127.0.0.1:8127`, with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` passed as an environment variable (never in `.env`); three player accounts in three isolated browser contexts at 375 × 667 (touch, 2×) and the host's screen in a fourth at 1280 × 800, each page in light and in dark. The host opened a room; the second account joined by the QR — read back with Chrome's `BarcodeDetector` from the screenshot of the host's phone, and from the dark screen's, both `http://127.0.0.1:8127/otaq/{code}` — and the third by typing the code with a space in it. Four games of five rounds, each phone tapping through the DOM as it followed `data-phase`: «Xanaları yadda saxla» «Asan» (3 × 3) and «Çətin» (5 × 5), «Ardıcıllığı yadda saxla» «Asan» (digits) and «Çox çətin» (rows of 9–10 digits, letters and shapes); right, wrong and missing answers, a first place shared by two shown once on the phones and the screen. The lock, the code chip's QR during a round and the «Oyunu bitir» confirmation were opened too. Then the host closed the room and every page went to its results (the screen to the room's page); a player took their name out (the host's results then read «Oyunçu» with the points, and the room answered that player 404); the host deleted the room (404 for the others, gone from their «Otaqlar»). On every phone page: no sideways scroll; no control under 44px (a checkbox counted by its 44px label row); and above the tab bar without scrolling «Yeni oyun», «Oyunu başlat», «Qoşul», the question's first and last item and the answer's note, on the 5 × 5 grid too. The first runs found and this change fixed: the grid and the answer's note under the tab bar at 667px (the compact round head, the grid's height cap, the note in place of the taps' row), «1.9» for «1,9», «Səhvdir» too dark in dark mode, the end-game icon read as a checkbox, an inline «Profil» link under 44px, the screen's «0 / 3» wrapping and its ranking cutting names short, the place label's hyphen spread by `tnum`. The 169 screenshots are kept outside the repository.
        - **Tests.** Vitest `resources/js/rooms/clock.test.ts` ("draws the same bar on a slow clock": two phones fetch the same state, one clock 37 s behind, and draw the same bar, where the device's own clock would not; "is full before the first state and empty after its end, never beyond"; "measures the study, the answer and the wait to the next round"), `components/rooms/standing.test.ts` ("shows a shared place once", "labels each place from the props and never makes a label", "lists nothing for an empty ranking"), `components/rooms/taps.test.ts` (4), `components/rooms/seconds.test.ts` (2), `lib/install-banner.test.ts` (`rooms/index` allowed); Pest `RoomPagesTest` "names the family of each game the setup offers and what each level holds", and `RoomRaceTest`'s setup case compares the mechanics, families, difficulties and rounds.
- **B5 — «Məntiq və ardıcıllıq».**
    - _Scope._ `next_in_sequence` and `odd_one_out`: the builders, the six sequence rules, `content/rooms/categories.json`, the eight levels, the two views. The judge needs nothing new (`choice`).
    - _Files._ `app/Domain/Content/Enums/RoomMechanic.php`, `Content/Data/RoomCategoryData.php`, `Content/Support/{ContentRepository,ContentValidator}.php`; `content/rooms/{levels,categories}.json`; `app/Domain/Rooms/Support/Mechanics/{NextInSequenceBuilder,OddOneOutBuilder,SequenceRules}.php`, `Rooms/Support/RoomPlayable.php`; `components/rooms/mechanics/{next-in-sequence,odd-one-out}.tsx`; `tests/Fixtures/rooms/*`, `tests/Fixtures/content/rooms/**`; `lang/*`.
    - _Tests._ `tests/Unit/Rooms/NextInSequenceBuilderTest.php` ("continues every rule of the level", "offers four different numbers with one right", "builds no row that two rules continue differently"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/OddOneOutBuilderTest.php` ("takes all but one word from one group", "leaves exactly one number without the shared property"); `ContentValidatorTest` (a word in two groups, a group too small, an unknown rule); `RoomRaceTest` ("plays a logic game to its ranking"); `contract.test.ts` on the new fixtures.
    - _Finish line._ Both logic games play in a room at every difficulty from the phone (seen in a browser); `content:validate` passes; `composer ci:check` passes.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 1 of 2: the server side)._ The two logic games, the six rules, the groups of words and the eight levels, with plain views that play; part 2 designs the views and the setup. The judge, the points rule, the clock and the timetable did not change. Where the text above leaves a choice, B5 made it so:
        - **The question's `shown`** (a change of the contract, written above in "The state" and "Room games"). A logic round has no study, and `ask.items` are exactly what an answer is made of, so the row `next_in_sequence` continues has its own key: `ask.shown`, items shown with the question that cannot be picked, with ids of their own; `[]` for every other mechanic. `RoomAskData::ids()` stays the items', which `RecordRoomAnswer` checks `picked` against (a term of the row picked is a 422). The question's keys are `{shape, pick, layout, grid, shown, items}`; `RoomAskData::fromArray()` reads a round stored without `shown` as `[]`, so the rounds of B3 and B4 still play. The fixtures `state.{answer,reveal}.json` and `state.{answer,reveal}.cells.json` gained `"shown": []`; zod's `RoomAsk` requires it.
        - **The rules** are named in `Content\Enums\RoomSequenceRule`, which the validator reads, and computed in `Rooms\Support\Mechanics\SequenceRules`: `continuation()` is what a rule makes of a row's last terms (the same step; `last² ÷ before` when whole; the step two back; the square after a square; the sum of the last two; the other row's step), `fits()` says that every term from the rule's `needs()` on is what it makes of the terms before, and `draw()` makes a row inside the range — a step from 1 or a whole factor 2–10, up or down; two different steps by turns that do not cancel out; consecutive squares from 1² up; two first terms and their sums; two rows, each with a step of its own. A row too short to check a rule fits it trivially, as a player could argue it does.
        - **No arguable row.** Every row is checked against all six rules, not only the level's, and built again when any rule that fits it continues it with another term; so no rule the game knows reads it otherwise. That sets how many terms a rule needs (`RoomSequenceRule::minTerms()`): an arithmetic row 4 (three equal steps; an alternating or interleaved reading of it agrees), geometric, alternating, squares and Fibonacci rows 5, interleaved rows 6 — four squares 1, 4, 9, 16 are also two rows of a step each (→ 17, not 25). The validator requires `terms` at least that for every rule a level names, and at most 7. A row whose next term is also one of its terms is built again too: it would be the one option that repeats the row.
        - **The near misses** are three: misreadings first — what a rule that does not fit the row makes of its last terms, and one step too far — then numbers 1–5 or 10 off; each inside the range, never a term of the row, all four different. Every rule that fits the row continues it with the right term, so no near miss is what such a rule continues with; a misreading of a rule that does not fit is a wrong reading, and it is offered. How many options are smaller than the right one is drawn first (0–3) and the order on the page after, both from the seeded source: with near misses a few off on both sides, the right term would otherwise be the middle of the options most of the time (`NextInSequenceBuilderTest` sees it at every place and every rank by size).
        - **`range`** bounds every number of a round, the row and the options: 1–999. Whether a rule fits a range is the builder's arithmetic, which the validator may not reach: the builder draws up to 2 000 rows a round and then refuses the level, and `RoomPlayableTest` "builds a game at every level of every mechanic it lists" builds every level of the content (a game of the most rounds, from five seeds). The validator also refuses a range of one number.
        - **`odd_one_out`, numbers**, are 2–99 (`OddOneOutBuilder::LEAST_NUMBER`, `MOST_NUMBER`), in code: the text gives a level only `source` and `options`. What all but one share is drawn among even, odd, a square and a multiple of 3–10; a property singles out a number when it is the only one with it or the only one without it, and a draw where any property the builder knows — those, and being a prime — singles out another number than the odd one is drawn again (4, 9, 16, 12: the squares leave 12, the parity leaves 9). **Words**: two groups are drawn, all but one word from the first; a word is shown as the file writes it, lowercase, and the builder refuses groups that share a word by `Az::lower`.
        - **`categories.json`** (version 1, `ContentRepository::roomCategories()`, `Content\Data\RoomCategoryData`): eight groups of 12–14 common words — heyvanlar, meyvələr, nəqliyyat, geyim, mətbəx əşyaları, musiqi alətləri, idman növləri, məktəb ləvazimatı — far apart in meaning (no vegetables beside the fruit; no body parts beside the clothes; no professions, which touch every other group; no flowers, many of which are first names), keys in ASCII. The validator's rules are in "Room content" above, with 15 broken cases. It has no `credits.json` entry: like the packs' words it is our own text under the repository's MIT licence, and `credits.json` covers the files under `content/assets/` and the fonts — an entry for a JSON file would match no file. ADR 023's entry is for B6's `words.json`, which an outside source may join.
        - **The levels** (`levels.json` version 3; the owner may tune them as data). `next_in_sequence`: «Asan» arithmetic, 4 terms, 1–50, 15 s; «Orta» arithmetic, geometric, squares, 5 terms, 1–300, 20 s; «Çətin» those, alternating and Fibonacci, 5 terms, 1–500, 25 s; «Çox çətin» all six, 6 terms, 1–999, 30 s. `odd_one_out`: «Asan» 4 words, 10 s; «Orta» 4 numbers, 20 s; «Çətin» 5 words, 12 s; «Çox çətin» 5 numbers, 25 s. `study_ms` is 0 for all eight; the validator refuses anything else (its B3 rule got its broken case).
        - **The setup's prop.** `RoomGameSetupData::options()` gives a level also `rules`, `terms`, `range`, `source` and `options`, null for a mechanic they do not belong to; `count` is null for a logic level. The setup writes «5 ədəd · ədədi silsilə, həndəsi silsilə, kvadratlar · 20 san. cavab» and «5 variant · ədədlər · 25 san. cavab».
        - **Texts.** `rooms.mechanics.{next_in_sequence,odd_one_out}.{name,line,ask}` («Sıranı davam etdir», «Artıq olanı tap»; no `study`: the phase never comes), `rooms.game.{right_option,next_term}`, `rooms.setup.levels.{sequence,odd}`, `rooms.setup.rules.*`, `rooms.setup.sources.*`; no text promises a benefit.
        - **The views** (plain): `components/rooms/mechanics/choice.tsx` (`NoStudy`; `ChoiceOptions`, the options as buttons two to a row, a tap sends; `ChoiceSolution`, «Düzgün cavab» and «Sizin cavabınız»), `next-in-sequence.tsx` (the row with a «?» in its last place, the right number there in the reveal), `odd-one-out.tsx`; `MECHANIC_VIEWS` has both, and the setup's `MechanicPicture` a plain one for each. On a 375px phone the «?» after six three-digit terms drops to a line of its own: part 2 fits a row of seven and the «?» on one line.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, three isolated contexts at 375 × 667 with touch, a scratch SQLite database and `php -S 127.0.0.1:8128` with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` as an environment variable; the dev Vite served the pages, Reverb refused the 127.0.0.1 origin, so the pages polled): the host opened a room, two players joined with its code, and the three played both games at every difficulty, five rounds each, tapping options with touch events — every page went from the countdown straight to the question (no study phase), every option stood above the tab bar, no page scrolled sideways, every reveal named the same right option and each phone its own choice, and the ranking followed. None of the 232 states the phones received before a reveal held a solution, a result or one's own answer. Screenshots are kept outside the repository.
        - **Tests.** `tests/Unit/Rooms/NextInSequenceBuilderTest.php` (the three named, over the four levels and each rule alone at its fewest terms, plus "tells a row that two rules continue differently from one they agree on", "builds the same rounds from the same seed", "builds only a level of its own that leaves a row to build"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/OddOneOutBuilderTest.php` (the two named, plus "singles out a number as the only one with a property or the only one without it", the seed, the levels it refuses); `RoomRaceTest` "plays a logic game to its ranking" (a row of numbers at «Çətin» and «Çox çətin», words, numbers: nothing of a round a moment before it opens, the question at its opening, a term of the row refused as an answer, the right option's id only as an item's id before the reveal) and "reads a round stored before the question had shown items", the setup case with four mechanics; `RoomPagesTest`'s setup case; `RoomPlayableTest` (four mechanics, every level built); `RoomSeedTest` for every mechanic; `ContentValidatorTest` (33 new broken cases: 18 for the levels, 15 for `categories.json`); `ContentRepositoryTest` ("reads the room levels" with the logic levels, "reads the room categories"); the memory builders' tests expect an empty `shown`; `RoomContractTest` and `contract.test.ts` read `state.{answer,reveal}.{sequence,odd}.json` ("shows a logic round with its question, the row apart from the options"); `taps.test.ts` ("takes no tap on an item shown with the question"). `Tests\Support\Rooms::wrong()` answers a choice with another option. `scripts/test-pgsql.sh --filter=Rooms`: 459 passed.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 2 of 2: the views and the setup)._ The two logic games designed on the phone and on the screen, by `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 ("Taps", "The reveal", "The setup"). No builder, level, group of words, contract, judge, points rule, clock, timetable or prop changed. Where the text above leaves a choice, B5 made it so:
        - **The options** (`components/rooms/mechanics/choice.tsx`, `ChoiceOptions`) are buttons of one size (52px high, `rounded-2xl`), in the server's order, with the text as the server sent it — a number at `text-2xl`, a word a step smaller (`text-xl`), so «gimnastika», «helikopter» and «motosiklet», the longest words of `categories.json`, fit two to a row at 375px. Four options stand two by two, five in one column (`optionColumns()` in `choice-layout.ts`): a fifth centred under a 2 × 2 grid would stand apart, which in «Artıq olanı tap» reads as a hint. Before the reveal the options differ in nothing but their text: no attribute, class or id of an option reaches the DOM.
        - **How the answer goes.** One tap chooses and sends (`tap()`, `pick` 1): the chosen option fills with `primary`, the others fade to half and are disabled, and the taps' row gives way to the note («Göndərilir…», then «Cavabınız qəbul olundu.» with «Cavab verib: :count / :total»). A `choice` has no «Seçilib: 0 / 1» in that row: one tap is the whole answer (`live-round.tsx`, the only shared change).
        - **The row** (`next-in-sequence.tsx`) is `ask.shown` and a dashed «?» cell on one line, as a grid of equal cells; its text is sized to the row's container (`@container`, `cqw`, `rowFont()`), at most 24px on a phone and `7vh` on the screen, so six three-digit terms and the «?» stay on one line at 375px (18.5px). The «?» carries «Növbəti ədəd» for screen readers.
        - **The reveal** (`ChoiceReveal`) draws the options again in their places: the right one, from `reveal.solution`, green with `Check` «Düzgün»; the viewer's own with «Sizin» — green with `Check` or red with `X`, as `mine.correct` says (`optionMark()`), never by comparing on the device — or «Düzgün · sizin» when they are one; the rest faded. In two columns an unmarked option keeps an empty mark line, so the texts of a row stay level. In `next_in_sequence` the row's «?» becomes the right number, green. The verdict above and the ranking below are B4's. The screen shows the same with no viewer, so only «Düzgün».
        - **The screen** (`big`) draws the row and the options as the phones do, without buttons, beside B4's «Cavab verib» card; five options in one column are 8.5vh high so that the question fits 800px with the head.
        - **The setup** writes what a logic level holds: «4 ədəd, 1–50 · ədədi silsilə · 4 variant · baxmaq yoxdur · 15 san. cavab», «5 variant · sözlər · baxmaq yoxdur · 12 san. cavab» (`rooms.setup.levels.{sequence,odd}` gained `:min`–`:max` of `range` and «baxmaq yoxdur»; «4 variant» is interface text, as «Dörd ədəddən birini seçin» already was). Its drawings: a rising row of bars and a dashed place; three dots and an amber diamond.
        - **Texts.** `rooms.game.{row,mark_right,mark_yours,mark_right_yours}` added; `rooms.game.right_option`, which only part 1's plain reveal used, removed.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, as B4 part 2 did it): a git worktree of this change built with `VITE_REVERB_*` pointing at a Reverb of its own (`127.0.0.1:8095`), a scratch SQLite database and `php -S 127.0.0.1:8131` from `public/`, with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` as an environment variable (never in `.env`); three player accounts in isolated browser contexts at 375 × 667 (touch, 2×) and the host's screen at 1280 × 800. Every page held a websocket to that Reverb (42–44 frames each). The host played too; eight games of five rounds — each game at each difficulty — with each phone tapping by touch events to a plan of right, wrong and no answer (the check read the right option from the scratch database, never the page): every reveal marked the right option the server stored and each phone's own with the verdict planned; every page went from the countdown straight to the question (no study), with the round head, the time bar and the instruction line. The rows came from arithmetic (both directions), geometric (288 → 9, 224 → 7), squares (9…49 → 64, 196…361 → 400), Fibonacci (40 31 71 102 173 275 → 448) and interleaved rows (738 703 508 724 278 745 → 48); no alternating row came up in those twenty. `odd_one_out` with words at «Asan» and «Çətin» and with numbers at «Orta» and «Çox çətin»; none of the forty rounds read as arguable. After each game the room's standing was read («Oyun bitdi», «Otağın sıralaması»). On every question and reveal no phone scrolled sideways, and on the host's phone, at every question: no control under 44px, the row on one line, no text overflowing its option, the options and the answer's note above the tab bar without scrolling (five words included), the options alike in markup before the reveal and in the server's order, and the screen never scrolled. The first runs found and this change fixed the texts of a reveal row standing at different heights when one option had a mark. The 184 screenshots are kept outside the repository.
