- **B6 — «Söz oyunları».**
    - _Scope._ `anagram` and `spelling`: the builders, `content/rooms/words.json` (our own list, ADR 023 decision 10), its validator rules and its credit, the eight levels, the two views; `word` becomes a kind `recall_order` may show. If the own list cannot fill a level, the step stops and reports it rather than copying a source.
    - _Files._ `app/Domain/Content/Enums/RoomMechanic.php`, `Content/Data/RoomWordData.php`, `Content/Support/{ContentRepository,ContentValidator}.php`; `content/rooms/{levels,words}.json`, `content/credits.json`, `CREDITS.md`; `app/Domain/Rooms/Support/Mechanics/{AnagramBuilder,SpellingBuilder}.php`, `Rooms/Support/RoomPlayable.php`; `components/rooms/mechanics/{anagram,spelling}.tsx`; fixtures; `lang/*`.
    - _Tests._ `tests/Unit/Rooms/AnagramBuilderTest.php` ("never shows the letters in the word's own order", "accepts every listed word the letters spell", "cases the letters with Azerbaijani rules"); `tests/Unit/Rooms/SpellingBuilderTest.php` ("makes the wrong spellings from confusable letters only", "offers no wrong spelling that is a listed word", "offers four different spellings with one right"); `ContentValidatorTest` (a word with a foreign letter, a duplicate by Azerbaijani casing, a first name of the name list, too few words for a level); `ContentRepositoryTest` ("reads the word list"); `CreditsDocumentTest`; `RoomRaceTest` ("plays a word game to its ranking", "stores no text a player wrote"); `contract.test.ts` on the new fixtures.
    - _Finish line._ Both word games play in a room at every difficulty; the word list has its credit under MIT and passes `content:validate`; `composer ci:check` passes.
    - _As built (2026-11-17, part 1 of 2: the server side and the content)._ The word list, both builders, the eight levels and the contract, with plain views that play; part 2 designs the views and the setup. Built in a worktree beside B5 and brought onto its commits: a word game's question has B5's `shown` as `[]`. No judge shape, points rule, clock or timetable changed. Where the text above leaves a choice, B6 made it so:
        - **The contract.** `RoomSolutionData` gains an optional `word` (a round stored without it reads null; `toArray()` leaves it out when null), and `RoomState` takes the reveal's `word` from it: an anagram's drawn word in the capitals of its tiles («ALMA»), a spelling's right spelling as the list writes it («kitab») — the round's own writing, so the page cases nothing. Fixtures `state.answer.{anagram,spelling}.json`, `state.reveal.{anagram,spelling}.json` and `answer.{anagram,spelling}.json`; `judge.json` has an anagram whose letters spell «ALMA» and «AMAL», both right, and a spelling.
        - **The word list** (`content/rooms/words.json`, version 1; `Content\Data\RoomWordData`, `ContentRepository::roomWords()`): `words` — 652, by length 3 → 24, 4 → 112, 5 → 140, 6 → 113, 7 → 95, 8 → 92, 9 → 64, 10 → 10, 11 → 2 — and `also_correct` (19), both lowercase in Azerbaijani order. It keeps 213 of the packs' 281 words: not the 13 first names, the places and planets («Bakı», «Uran»), the dinosaurs' genus names, the two-letter words, and the words whose spelling varies in use or that the author was not sure of («feyxoa», «qreypfrut», «komet», «orbit», «fosil», «göyqurşağı»). The other 439 are everyday nouns and adjectives written for Elifba from the author's knowledge, none copied from a dictionary or a list; three were added as anagram partners of listed words («amal», «sıra», «kürə»). It credits "Elifba contributors" under MIT; `ContentValidator::CREDITED_LISTS` lets `credits.json` name a list beside the asset folders, and requires its entry.
        - **The variants were read.** Every spelling one confusable letter makes of every word of 5–9 letters, and every one two letters make of the words of 5–7 letters and, of 8–9, those two consonants make — about 12 000 — was read for real words, and so were a found word's own. Those that are nouns entered `words` («nağara», «qaynaq», «cəngəl», «balapan», «hakim», «heyət», «bəniz», «qasıq», «dalış», «kələm», «kənar», «dama»); the forms and the words of another kind went to `also_correct` («babam», «boyum», «doyun», «durun», «dərim», «qarım», «qoyum», «qovum», «səkər», «qavalı», «dadan», «bəzər», «doyum», «durum», «bürün», «arada», «kəlam»), with «səhər» and «həyat», which are also first names. A first name of `names.json` is never offered as wrong either (`RoomPlayable` gives the builders the names), and no wrong spelling starts with «ğ», which no Azerbaijani word does.
        - **One rule in one place** (`Content\Support\RoomWords`): what an anagram accepts and may be drawn from, the ways to make a spelling's wrong spellings and the words that have one, the short words of a row. The builders use it and the validator counts by it, so a level never asks for what its builder cannot build.
        - **`swaps` is how close the four spellings are**, not how many letters each wrong one changes. Were each wrong spelling one swap away from the right one, the right one would be the spelling the others agree with most, letter by letter — found without reading the word. So the four are the ways of writing some letters: `swaps` 1, two letters of the word each written two ways (two spellings differ in one letter or both); `swaps` 2, three letters, every wrong spelling changing two (any two differ in two). At each letter where they differ, two spellings write it one way and two the other (`SpellingBuilderTest` "never makes the right spelling the one the others agree with most"). A word is drawn only if some such set has no correct spelling in it; «yol» never is.
        - **The anagram's order.** A shuffle that reads like an accepted spelling is turned by one place at a time; if every turn does, the orders of the letters are walked from the first until one reads like none. `RoomWords::forAnagram()` draws only words with such an order (more orders than accepted spellings). Both word builders draw a level's words without repeating one while they last (`RoomWordDraw`).
        - **The levels** (`levels.json` version 4, after B5's 3; ADR 004): «Anaqram» 4–5 letters and 20 s, 5–6 and 30 s, 6–7 and 40 s, 7–8 and 50 s; «Düzgün yazılış» 5–6 letters with `swaps` 2 and 15 s, 7–8 with 2 and 15 s, 5–6 with 1 and 12 s, 7–9 with 1 and 12 s — the owner may tune them as data. Every level draws from 187–253 words; every length a level asks for has at least 64 (`ContentValidator::MIN_ROOM_WORDS` = 60).
        - **The validator** (`ContentValidator::roomWords()`, `wordLevel()`): `words` lowercase words of the alphabet with a vowel (`WordRules`), 3–12 letters, none twice by `Az::lower`, none a first name, in Azerbaijani order; `also_correct` lowercase, no word of `words`, none twice, in order, and each a spelling a listed word makes with one or two confusable letters (else it keeps nothing from being offered); a level's `word_length` 3–12 with 60 words of each length (an anagram's counted by `forAnagram()`), a spelling's `swaps` 1 or 2 and 60 words in its range that make three wrong spellings with them. `broken.json` gains 26 cases (`rooms words: …` and the word levels'; B5's "a logic game studied" covers a word level's `study_ms` too), and "words before the word list" became "a kind recall_order cannot show".
        - **Words in `recall_order`.** `RoomMechanic::kinds()` lists `word`; the builder draws words of 3–5 letters (`RoomWords::ROW_LETTERS`, 276 of them) as the list writes them, and the validator bounds a row of words by them. No level names `word` yet: the view draws an item in a 60px square tile and a 44px slot, which a word does not fit at 375px; part 2 gives a word a tile of its own and then adds `word` to «Orta» as data (a row of five words of five letters fits a 375px row only as tiles about 64px wide).
        - **The setup** (`RoomGameSetupData::levelOption()`): each level also gives `letters` (a word game's `word_length`) and `swaps`; the plain setup says «4–5 hərfli söz · baxmaq yoxdur · 20 san. cavab» and, for a spelling, how close the four spellings are.
        - **The views** (plain): `mechanics/anagram.tsx` taps the letters like a row (`RecallOrderAsk`) and reveals the word and the viewer's letters; `mechanics/spelling.tsx` is B5's `ChoiceOptions` and `ChoiceReveal` with the four spellings, so it is already laid out as the logic games' options are; both have B5's `NoStudy`. `MechanicView.Solution` takes the reveal's `word`, which the room page and the screen pass. Texts `rooms.mechanics.{anagram,spelling}.{name,line,ask}` (no `study`, as for B5's games: the phase never comes), `rooms.game.word`, `rooms.setup.levels.{letters,spelling_1,spelling_2}` with B5's «baxmaq yoxdur».
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, as B5 did it): this worktree built with `vp build`, a scratch SQLite database and `php -S 127.0.0.1:8133` from `public/`, with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` as an environment variable (never in `.env`); Reverb refused the 127.0.0.1 origin, so the pages polled. Two player accounts in isolated contexts at 375 × 667 with touch: the host opened a room, the second joined with its code, and the two played «Anaqram» and «Düzgün yazılış» at all four difficulties, five rounds each, tapping by touch events to a plan of right, wrong and no answer (the check read the solution from the scratch database, never the page). Every page went from the countdown straight to the question; the setup said what each level holds; the tiles and the options came in the server's order, no tile row read like a word it spells, and the state each phone fetched in an answer phase held no solution, result or `word`; every reveal named the word and gave the verdict the plan expected, and the room's standing added up (80 and 40 points a game). No page scrolled sideways, and the last tile or option stood above the tab bar. Anagram tiles of six letters and more wrap to a second line of the plain row (60px tiles, five to a line), which part 2 designs.
        - **Tests.** `tests/Unit/Rooms/AnagramBuilderTest.php` (the three named, and "never shows the order of another word the letters spell…", "draws no word twice…", the seed, a level of its own); `tests/Unit/Rooms/SpellingBuilderTest.php` (the three named, and "never makes the right spelling the one the others agree with most", "draws only words that make three wrong spellings…", the seed, a level of its own); `RecallOrderBuilderTest` "shows a row of short words of the list…"; `RoomRaceTest` "plays a word game to its ranking" and "stores no text a player wrote" (both games), with `raceWithholds()` reading a choice's one id as an option the question shows; `ContentValidatorTest` (26 broken cases), `ContentRepositoryTest` "reads the word list", `ContentCommandsTest` (the compiled list), `CreditsDocumentTest`, `RoomContractTest` and `contract.test.ts` ("names the word of a word game in the reveal alone"), `RoomJudgeTest`, `RoomPlayableTest`, `RoomSeedTest` (every mechanic), `RoomPagesTest` (the setup's `letters` and `swaps`). `Tests\Support\Rooms::wrong()` answers a choice with another option and turns an anagram until it spells no accepted word. `scripts/test-pgsql.sh --filter=Rooms`: 537 passed.
    - _As built (2026-11-18, part 2 of 2: the views and the setup)._ The two word games designed on the phone and on the screen, by `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 ("Taps", "The reveal", "The setup"). No builder, level, word, contract, judge, points rule, clock, timetable or prop changed. Where the text above leaves a choice, B6 made it so:
        - **«Anaqram»** (`components/rooms/mechanics/anagram.tsx`). A row of the word's places (one per letter, up to 44px wide and 48px high, `text-2xl`; narrower for a long word — 38px at eight letters on a 375px phone) over the tiles: 60px, `text-3xl` semibold, in the server's order and case, shared into even rows of at most five (`tileRows()` in `mechanics/word-layout.ts`: four letters on one row, six as three over three, eight as four over four), so the longest word of «Çox çətin» stands in two rows with no sideways scroll. A tap moves the letter into the next place, whose dashed border is thicker and `primary`, and leaves a dashed gap where the tile stood (the letter now reads in the word above, so the tile carries no number as a row's does); «Geri» takes back the last; the tap that fills the last place sends the word. On the screen the places stand empty — the room sees how long the word is, never anyone's taps — over tiles of up to 13vh, at most six to a row.
        - **The anagram's reveal** draws the round's word under «Söz» in green cells from `reveal.solution` (as the tiles write it, so the page splits no string; `reveal.word` is read only when the tiles are missing) and, by the server's verdict, the viewer's word (`anagramMine()`): the same word is that one row marked «Düzgün · sizin»; another word the letters spell, taken by the server (`mine.correct`), stands green under it with «Sizin · bu söz də düzgündür» (`rooms.game.mark_also_right`), never drawn as a mistake; a word the server refused stands red with «Sizin». The two words are compared whole only to know whether there is a second row; right or wrong is `mine.correct` alone. On the screen the word fills its column (`cqw`), eight letters included. `data-word` and `data-tone` on a row are hooks for checks and say what the row already shows.
        - **«Düzgün yazılış»** (`mechanics/spelling.tsx`) does not use B5's grid of two by two: its four spellings stand in one column, full width (56px high, 28px semibold, `tracking-wide`; on the screen 11vh high and up to 7vh), so each is set large and the four read letter under letter — what the round asks about is «ı» or «i», «ə» or «e», «ş» or «s». Before the reveal they differ in nothing but their text; a tap chooses and sends. The reveal is B5's marks (`optionMark()`, `Mark`, now exported with an optional wording) in the same column, each row keeping its mark line so the words stay level.
        - **Casing.** No room file changes the case of a text: no `uppercase`, `lowercase` or `capitalize` class and no `toUpperCase()` or `toLowerCase()`, comments aside (`tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomCasingTest.php`). The tiles are the server's `Az::upper` capitals and the spellings its lowercase, the same in the English locale.
        - **The letters told apart.** Inter, the committed face, holds every Azerbaijani letter at the weights used (checked in the woff2 files), and at 30px on a tile, 28px in a spelling and 6–8vh on the screen, semibold, «İ» and «I», «ı» and «i», «ə» and «e», «ö», «ü», «ş», «ç» and «ğ» read apart in both themes (İŞIQLI, PİNQVİN, ZƏLZƏLƏ; qəribə / qərıbə / qəripə / qərıpə; yağış / yağıs / yəğış / yəğıs). Andika, the board's face, was not taken: the rooms are the adults' area and keep the shell's face.
        - **The setup** keeps part 1's lines and drawings; the spellings' wording became «4 yazılış, bəziləri bir hərflə fərqlənir» (`swaps` 1) and «4 yazılış, istənilən ikisi iki hərflə fərqlənir» (`swaps` 2).
        - **Words in `recall_order`** still wait: no level names `word`, and a word's tile was not drawn, since the levels were not this part's to change.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, as B5 part 2 did it): a git worktree of this change built with `VITE_REVERB_*` pointing at a Reverb of its own (`127.0.0.1:8097`), a scratch SQLite database and `php -S 127.0.0.1:8135` from `public/`, with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` as an environment variable (never in `.env`); three player accounts in isolated contexts at 375 × 667 (touch, 2×) — the third in the English locale — and the host's screen at 1280 × 800; every page held a websocket to that Reverb (45–47 frames each). The host played too; eight games of five rounds, each game at each difficulty, each phone tapping by touch events to a plan of right, wrong and no answer, with «Geri» once a game (two tiles tapped, one taken back, the place emptied); the check read the answers from the scratch database, never the page (a wrong anagram is the tiles in their shown order, which spells no accepted word). Because a five-round game rarely draws one of the list's six pairs, the check put a round the real `AnagramBuilder` built from another seed in place of the third round of «Asan» and of «Çox çətin»: ÜRƏK / KÜRƏ and AEROPORT / OPERATOR, the longest word of the hardest level. The player who spelled KÜRƏ and OPERATOR got «Düzdür» and both words in green, the one who spelled the round's word «Düzgün · sizin». On every question the tiles and the spellings came as the server stored them, letter for letter and in its order, in both locales, with `text-transform: none`; no tile row read like an accepted word; the first and the last tile, the last spelling and the answer's note stood above the tab bar without scrolling; no control under 44px; no page scrolled sideways; the screen never scrolled and no cell overflowed. Every reveal named the server's word and the planned verdict, and the room's standing added up. No finding. The 161 screenshots, the script and its log are kept outside the repository.
        - **Tests.** Vitest `components/rooms/mechanics/word-layout.test.ts` ("keeps five 60px tiles to a row at 375px and shares the letters out evenly", "fits the longest word of the hardest level on a 375px phone", "keeps a word of up to nine letters at the large size", "writes the letters as the tiles have them, cased on the server", "shows another word the letters spell as right, never as a mistake", "takes the same word with the other «A» first as the same word", "reads right and wrong from the server alone"); Pest `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomCasingTest.php` ("changes the case of no text on a room page"); `RoomPagesTest`, `RoomsApartTest` and `TranslationsTest` unchanged and passing.
- **B7 — The privacy page, the landing page, the READMEs and the deploy.**
    - _Scope._ `/privacy`'s section «Otaqlar» in both languages (§12 "Adults' privacy"), shown when the switch is on; a section for rooms on the welcome page and in both READMEs, on the page only when the switch is on and with no promise of a benefit; `ELIFBA_ROOMS` in the install sections; elifba.io updated with the switch still off — the owner turns it on (B7-O).
    - _Files._ `PrivacyController`, `resources/js/pages/privacy.tsx`; `WelcomeController`, `resources/js/pages/welcome.tsx`, `components/welcome/*`; `README.md`, `README.az.md`, `docs/screenshots/`; `.env.production.example`; `lang/*`.

    - _As built (2026-11-18)._ The repository side; the deploy is the step's second session. Written from what B2–B6 built — the tables, the actions, `RoomPolicy` and §12's notes — not from the design alone. Where the text above leaves a choice, B7 made it so:
        - **`/privacy`.** `PrivacyController` sends `rooms: {idleHours}` (`elifba.rooms.idle_hours`) only while `RoomsArea::enabled()`; with the switch off the page gets no `rooms` prop. The section «Otaqlar: böyüklərin oyunları» / "Rooms: games for adults" (`id="rooms"`, so `/privacy#rooms` lands on it) stands after the parents' section and before Google's and the operator's, in nine parts — `privacy.rooms.{what,stored,not_stored,why,who,contact,how_long,delete,where}` — and closes with the operator (`Operator`, as the page's own section has it). The introduction is `privacy.intro` unchanged and, while the switch is on, `privacy.rooms.intro` after it. What it says is what the code keeps: the role and `player_agreed_at`; `room_players` (who, joined, left, removed) and `room_answers` (what was picked, right or wrong, the time, the points); no room table with a name, a device or a place; a name read from the account each time, seen by the hosts and players of the rooms the account joined (a removed player's name is no longer shown; a player who left an open room sees its results once it closes); the code cleared on the close or after `idle_hours` without activity; `ForgetRoomPlayer` and `DeleteRoom` only for a closed room, named by their buttons («Adımı sil», «Otağı sil»); the account's deletion by the foreign keys; `PruneOldRooms` from the room's `created_at`. The teachers' and the parents' sections, the class note and the three agreement sentences are unchanged.
        - **The welcome page** follows `docs/frontend.md` rule 10, written first. `WelcomeController` adds `rooms` only while the switch is on: `families` (each `RoomFamily` with its `RoomMechanic` cases that have levels in `content/rooms/levels.json`, in the enums' order — `WelcomeController` may not name `Rooms\Support\RoomPlayable`, and `WelcomePageTest` compares the two), `difficulties` (those of the levels), `maxPlayers` and `rounds`. The section is `components/welcome/rooms-section.tsx`; the page's `Picture` moved to `components/welcome/picture.tsx` to serve both. Its pictures are screenshots of a «Düzgün yazılış» game at «Orta» among four made-up adults — the host's screen at the third round's reveal with the room's ranking (`rooms-screen.webp`, 1280 × 720) and the host's phone at the same moment, «Düzdür» over the right spelling (`rooms-phone.webp`, 540 × 566, cut above the screen's ranking). A difficulty's name keeps its two words on one line. An account in the player area found «Rolunuzu seçin» on the page since B2; it now finds «Otaqlar» in the header and «Otaqlarınıza keçin» (`welcome.rooms.hero`) as the main action.
        - **The READMEs** have «Otaqlar, böyüklər üçün» / "Rooms, for adults" after the parents' section (a room, the six games in their families, the limits, what is kept, the screen, the switch) with `docs/screenshots/room-screen.png` (1280 × 720) and `room-phone.png` (a player's phone at the same round's question, 750 × 1334); `ELIFBA_ROOMS` in the install sections (what it opens; `docker compose up -d --wait` applies it, as for the operator) and in Develop; `Rooms` in the domains line; one sentence in Privacy. `.env.production.example`'s comment says what the switch opens and which command applies it; the switch stays `false` there and in `.env.example`.
        - **Tests.** `PrivacyPageTest` "has the section for rooms in both languages when the switch is on" (every part, the parts the page lists, the stored facts, who sees a name, the hours and the days, no age and no contact, the four ways out by their buttons' names, the operator in the section) and "says nothing of rooms when it is off" (no `rooms` prop for a visitor or a player, every `privacy.rooms.` key drawn only under it); `WelcomePageTest` "shows rooms only when the switch is on"; `LandingTest` "makes no third-party request from the welcome page" with the switch off and on; `tests/Unit/RoomsWordingTest.php` "promises no benefit in any room text" (`rooms.*`, `welcome.rooms.*`, `privacy.rooms.*`, the player role's texts and the READMEs' room sections, lowered both the Azerbaijani way and the plain way) and "finds a promise however it is cased, and nothing in a plain name"; Vitest `resources/js/pages/rooms-switch.test.ts` renders both pages with `react-dom/server` in both languages and reads what they draw — nothing of rooms without the prop, the whole section with it, the player area's action. `TranslationsTest`, `RoomsSwitchTest` and `RoomsApartTest` unchanged and passing.
        - **Seen in a browser** (headless Chrome over CDP, as B6 part 2): this worktree built with `VITE_REVERB_*` at a Reverb of its own (`127.0.0.1:8099`), a scratch SQLite database, `php -S 127.0.0.1:8141` with `ELIFBA_ROOMS=true` and `127.0.0.1:8142` without it, both as environment variables (never in `.env`). Both pages at 375 × 667 (touch, 2×) and 1280 × 800, light and dark, az and en, switch on and off: with it off the welcome page's sections and links and `/privacy`'s headings and introduction are what they were, with it on the section stands between the children's data and the price; no page scrolled sideways, nothing in the section is transformed in case, every request went to the app's own host (and its Reverb), `/privacy#rooms` lands on the section, «Otaq aç» and «Otağa qoşul» take a visitor to the login, a teacher to «Rollar» with «Oyunçu» chosen and a player to «Otaqlar». No finding after the phone picture was cut above the ranking it first covered and «Çox çətin» was kept on one line.
    - _The owner's live check (B7-O, 2026-11-18)._ Before switching the area on, a live check in the owner's Chrome (four accounts, the screen, six games) found that «Cavab verib X / N» stood still in the answer phase while Reverb worked: an answer raised no revision, and a page that hears the channel asks for the state only at a phase's end, so the big screen showed «0 / 3» while two had answered. Fixed: every accepted answer raises the revision and signals (`RecordRoomAnswer` step 5; the last answer's early close is the same revision), as §12 "The channel" and ADR 021 decision 8 now list; the receipt still says nothing of right or wrong, and the count was already in every state. Since a burst of answers is a burst of signals, a room page has at most one state request in flight and folds the signals that arrive meanwhile into one more (`use-live-room.ts`). Tests: `RoomSignalsTest` ("signals a game's start, every answer and an early close"), `RoomRaceTest` (the receipts' revisions).
    - _The game flow after going live (2026-11-18, `fix/rooms-game-flow`)._ From the owner's reports on elifba.io, in two sessions. The first made a round wait only for the phones still in the game and the reveal 3 s (ADR 021 "Amendment of 2026-11-18: a round waits for the phones that are still in the game", "Whom a round waits for" above). The second:
        - **The reveal is never a study** (the owner: in «Xanaları yadda saxla» a player who had not answered took the right cells, drawn in the study's amber, for the next round's cells). `RecallCellsSolution` draws the cells that were lit as the right answer — under «Düzgün xanalar» with `Check`, green cells each with a check, the others only a dashed outline, the viewer's taps ringed in `primary` — and never the study's lamp; `RecallOrderSolution` draws the right order in green tiles under «Düzgün ardıcıllıq» with `Check`; the round's head says «Raund :index / :total bitdi» in every reveal, on the phone and the screen (`rooms.game.{right_cells,round_over}`; `lit_cells` went). The four other reveals were checked for the same confusion — their right option or word is already green with «Düzgün» and the rest faded — and needed only the head. docs/frontend.md rule 15 says so.
        - **When a page asks** (the owner: "sometimes the question reaches the other side late while I am already playing"). Measured first, as ADR 021's second amendment of the day records: a socket open and deaf, no socket at all, and the screen's own `answered`, which stays false while its host answers on a phone, left pages on a question the room had closed — two whole rounds lost to a deaf socket, 11 of 20 questions and 14 of 20 reveals missing on a screen without a socket. `followRoom()` now sends a moment's request half a round trip ahead (`ServerClock.trip()`, `LEAD_MAX_MS` 250), asks again after what the server said was left when told the same phase, polls every 1.5 s without a socket only in the lobby and while it waits for the others (`waitsForOthers()`; the screen passes `answers: false`), asks every 3 s while it waits even with a socket (`WAIT_POLL_MS`), and pusher-js finds a deaf socket within about 15 s (`SOCKET_TIMEOUTS`). After: with the channel heard every device showed every question 0.08–0.15 s after `answer_from_ms`, and 0.32–0.43 s with a 300 ms round trip added (0.44–0.48 s before); without a socket no device missed a countdown, a study, a question or a reveal, the shortest reveal 1.46 s; with a socket deaf for 20 s the player answered every round. The table is `~/elifba-shots/rooms-flow/measure/table.md` on the owner's machine.
        - Tests: Vitest `clock.test.ts` ("asks again when the server says how much of the phase was left, then a little later each time", "sends a moment's request half a round trip ahead, so it reaches the server just after the moment", "polls through every question on the screen, whose viewer answers on a phone", "asks every 3 s while it waits for the others, even while it hears the channel", the no-socket test at 1.5 s), `live.test.ts` ("finds a socket that hears nothing within about 15 s, not a minute"). Nothing changed on the server.
        - **Two more, the owner's choice after the check.** A socket deaf in the lobby still cost a fast game's first three rounds, so a page that hears the channel asks every 3 s in the lobby and after a game too (`waitsForSignal()`: the lobby, a game that is over and a page waiting for the others; every 1.5 s without the channel, every 3 s with it); and every page asks at once when it is shown again (`onShown()` in `live.ts`, `visibilitychange` to visible). ADR 021's amendment says so. Tests: Vitest `clock.test.ts` ("asks every 3 s in the lobby and while it waits for the others, even while it hears the channel"), `live.test.ts` ("asks at once when the page is shown again, never when it is hidden").

### Games first (G1–G3)

G1 (2026-11-18) drew three clickable prototypes, took the owner's choice and wrote ADR 026, §12 "Games first", `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Games first" and the glossary's rows; it changed no code. Each step below changes the contract first if it has to depart from it, runs `composer ci:check`, keeps `ELIFBA_ROOMS` off wherever it is not testing rooms, and is done only when its named tests exist and pass on SQLite and PostgreSQL. No step changes a file of `Classroom`, `Family`, `Play` or `Rankings`, a children's page, or the content. Both rebase onto whatever has landed in §12 "Rooms and joining" by then (the code field's refusals and limiter are that subsection's; only the address is this roadmap's).

- **G2 — The server and the state.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "Games first": `rooms.setup_*` and `Room::setup()`; `OpenRoom` with a mechanic and its three cases; `ChooseRoomGame`, `SetHostPlays`, `RoomDefaults`; `StartRoomGame` without a body; `setup` in the state, `v: 2`, the fixtures; `/oyun` with the redirects from `/otaq`, `RoomJoinLink`; `GET /rooms/past`; the props `catalog`, `open` (with the mechanic and whether a game runs) and `entrance`. The pages only as far as the routes need them, plain, so the area keeps working behind its switch: `rooms/index` posts a mechanic, `rooms/show`'s dialog writes through `rooms.setup.update` and starts without a body, `rooms/past` lists. G3 designs them.
    - _Files._ A migration `add_setup_to_rooms`; `config/elifba.php` (`rooms.defaults`); `app/Domain/Rooms/{Actions/{OpenRoom,ChooseRoomGame,SetHostPlays,StartRoomGame},Data/{OpenRoomData,RoomSetupData,RoomStateData},Models/Room,Support/{RoomDefaults,RoomState,RoomList,RoomJoinLink}}`; `routes/rooms.php`; `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/*`; `database/factories/RoomFactory.php`, `tests/Support/Rooms.php`; `resources/js/rooms/contract.ts` and what reads the state; `tests/Fixtures/rooms/state.*.json`; `php artisan wayfinder:generate --with-form`; `lang/*` only for `rooms.errors.game_running`.
    - _Tests._ `tests/Feature/Rooms/ChooseGameTest.php` (the cases of §12 "Guard tests", and "takes the difficulty and the rounds of the host's newest game, else the defaults", "refuses a game that cannot be played", "refuses a change while a game runs and in a closed room", "signals every device when the choice changes", "plays again with the same choice", "says played only while the choice is the game just played"); `RoomHostTest` ("lets the host step out of playing and back in", "does not let the lock keep the host out"); `JoinRoomTest` ("joins at /oyun", "sends the old /otaq addresses to the new ones"); `RoomStateTest`, `RoomContractTest` and `contract.test.ts` with `setup`; `RoomPagesTest` ("sends the home the catalog and the open rooms, and the past rooms to their own page", "sends the screen what it needs to choose and start"); the isolation, area, switch and bearer tests with the new routes.
    - _Finish line._ With the switch on, an account chooses a game and is in a room with that game chosen; a second device of the host and a joined player read the same choice from the state, and a visitor to the join page reads nothing; the screen's session can change the choice and start; «Yenidən oyna» starts the same game again; `/otaq/482913` arrives at `/oyun/482913`. `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
- **G3 — The pages and every word.**
    - _Scope._ `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Games first": «Oyunlar» with the grid and the code field with its camera; «Keçmiş oyunlar»; the start screen on the phone and on the screen; «Oyun ayarları»; a player's start screen; after a game; the tools sheet; the scanner. Every text of ADR 026 decision 4 in both languages, the welcome page's section, `/privacy`'s section with `privacy.rooms.camera`, both READMEs and their screenshots, `CLAUDE.md`'s lines that name «Otaqlar».
    - _Files._ `resources/js/pages/rooms/{index,past,join,show,screen}.tsx`; `resources/js/components/rooms/*` (the grid and its tile, the code field, the scanner, the start screen, the settings, the tools; `game-setup.tsx` gives its drawings to the grid and goes); `resources/js/rooms/scan.ts`; `components/app/nav-areas.ts`; `lib/install-banner.ts` if a page is added to its list; `components/welcome/rooms-section.tsx`, `pages/privacy.tsx`; `lang/az.json`, `lang/en.json`; `README.md`, `README.az.md`, `docs/screenshots/`; `package.json` if a decoder is bundled.
    - _Tests._ Vitest `resources/js/rooms/scan.test.ts`; `RoomPagesTest`; `RoomsApartTest`; `tests/Unit/RoomsWordingTest.php` (the new keys, the READMEs' new heading); `TranslationsTest`; `PrivacyPageTest` ("says what the camera does with the picture"); `WelcomePageTest`; `tests/Unit/Rooms/RoomCasingTest.php` unchanged and passing.
    - _Finish line._ Seen in a browser at 375 × 667 and 1280 × 720, light and dark, az and en: from «Oyunlar» a tile and «Başla» start a game; the screen and a player's phone show the chosen game before it starts and follow a change; the screen changes the game and starts; «Yenidən oyna» and another game follow a final; the word «otaq» stands only where ADR 026 decision 2 allows; a scanned QR of the room joins and another QR is refused; every request goes to the app's own host. `composer ci:check` passes.

### Rivals face to face (P1–P2)

P1 (2026-11-18) drew three clickable prototypes, took the owner's choice — variant A, «Zolaq», in blue and orange, then its finished look with three Fluent emoji — and wrote ADR 027, §12 "Rivals face to face (P2)", `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Rivals face to face" and the glossary's rows; it changed no code. The prototypes and the three downloaded Fluent files are outside the repository (`~/elifba-shots/rivalry/`: `prototypes/rivalry-a-zolaq-v2.html` is the accepted look, `fluent-dl/` the files of commit `1ffb34c`).

- **P2 — The strip, the lanes and their words.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "Rivals face to face (P2)" and rule 15 "Rivals face to face": `rivalPair()` and `overtakeOf()`; `RivalryStrip` on the phone (`LiveRound`) and the screen; `RaceLanes` in the screen's reveal with three players or more; the motion of decisions 14–16 on ADR 025's helpers; the grid's new limit; `content/rooms/rivalry.json` with the three Fluent files, `RoomRivalryData`, the prop `rivalry`; the `rooms.rivalry.*` words. Not the state, the protocol, the points, the sounds or the final.
    - _Files._ `resources/js/components/rooms/{rivalry.ts,rivalry.test.ts,rivalry-strip.tsx,race-lanes.tsx,live-round.tsx}`, `resources/js/pages/rooms/{show,screen}.tsx`, `resources/js/components/rooms/mechanics/*` only for the grid's limit, `resources/css/app.css` (`--animate-room-shine`); `content/assets/fluent/{26A1,1F451,1F525}.svg`, `content/rooms/rivalry.json`; `app/Domain/Content/{Data/RoomRivalryData,Support/ContentRepository,Support/ContentValidator}.php`, `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/{RoomController,RoomScreenController}.php`; `lang/az.json`, `lang/en.json`; the tests of §12's table.
    - _Tests._ §12 "Rivals face to face (P2)", "Guard tests"; `composer ci:check`; the `pgsql` job.
    - _Finish line._ Seen in a browser at 375 × 667 and 1280 × 720, light and dark, az and en, with motion and with reduced motion, for two players and for eight: the strip stands still through a question and moves in the reveal; an overtake shows its ribbon on the phones of the one who took the lead and the one overtaken and on the screen, and not on a page opened on the reveal; a 4 × 4 grid and an 8-letter anagram fit without scrolling; a rival who left shows «çıxıb»; a host who does not play sees the screen's pair. `composer ci:check` passes.
    - _As built._ Landed 2026-11-18: §12 "Rivals face to face (P2)", "As built (P2)", ADR 027 "As built (P2)" and rule 15's "As built (P2)" — the grid limit `100svh − 28.5rem`, the screen's `ScreenFit`, a race with one place held.

### Taunts (T1–T2)

T1 (2026-11-18) drew three clickable prototypes outside the repository (`~/elifba-shots/taunts/prototypes/`), took variant A, «Zolaqda», and the list of ten under the owner's standing rule of that day, and wrote ADR 029, §12 "Taunts (T2)", `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Taunts", the glossary's rows and the amended lines of §1, §12 and ADRs 021, 022, 024 and 025; it changed no code. T2 builds on P2 (ADR 027's strip) and rebases onto it; it changes the contract first if it has to depart from it, runs `composer ci:check`, and is done only when its named tests pass on SQLite and PostgreSQL.

- **T2 — Taunts.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "Taunts (T2)": `rooms.taunts` and `room_taunts`; `RoomPointsRule::TAUNT_COST`, `RoomTauntBalance`, `RoomRanking` less the counted taunts; `ThrowRoomTaunt`, `SetRoomTaunts`, their routes and the limiter; the state `v: 3` with `room.taunts`, `taunts` and `reveal.spent`, `?for=screen`; `content/rooms/taunts.json` with nine new Fluent files and its validator rules; the prop `taunts`; `PruneOldRooms` emptying the target; the pages of rule 15 "Taunts" beside P2's strip and lanes; every key of ADR 029 decision 33 and the two `/privacy` sentences, in both languages.
    - _Files._ A migration `add_taunts_to_rooms` (the column and the table); `config/elifba.php` (`rooms.taunts`); `app/Domain/Rooms/{Actions/{ThrowRoomTaunt,SetRoomTaunts},Data/{RoomTauntData,RoomTauntReceiptData,RoomTauntsData,RoomTauntSeenData,RoomSpentData,RoomStateData,RoomRevealData},Models/{Room,RoomTaunt},Support/{RoomPointsRule,RoomTauntBalance,RoomRanking,RoomState},Policies/RoomPolicy}`, `Actions/PruneOldRooms` (or the command that prunes); `app/Domain/Content/{Data/RoomTauntData,Support/ContentRepository,Support/ContentValidator}`; `content/rooms/taunts.json`, `content/assets/fluent/` (nine SVGs at commit `1ffb34c`); `routes/rooms.php`; `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/*`; `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` (`room-taunts`); `resources/js/rooms/contract.ts`; `resources/js/components/rooms/` (the tray, the card, `taunts.ts`, the strip's row, the standing's and lanes' cost, the screen's toggle, the host's switch); `resources/js/pages/rooms/{show,screen}.tsx`; `resources/css/app.css` (the taunt colour and keyframes); `lang/az.json`, `lang/en.json`; `tests/Fixtures/rooms/*`, `tests/Support/Rooms.php`; `php artisan wayfinder:generate --with-form`.
    - _Tests._ §12 "Taunts (T2)" "Guard tests", and `TranslationsTest`, `RoomsWordingTest` unchanged and passing.
    - _Finish line._ With the switch on, two phones and the screen (light, dark, reduced motion; az and en): a player with points opens the tray beside the strip and throws; the balance drops at once and the cooldown ring runs; the rival's phone shows the card over the strip for 4 s without covering the question, at 375 × 667 with the 4 × 4 grid and the 8-letter anagram; the screen shows it over its strip; nobody can throw in the first round; the reveal shows «−1» and the totals; the host switches taunts off and the tray goes; a closed room shows only net points. `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _As built._ Landed 2026-11-18: §12 "Taunts (T2)", "As built (T2)", ADR 029 "As built (T2)" and rule 15's "As built (T2)" — two support classes (`RoomTauntCost`, `RoomTauntWindow`), the screen's `?for=screen`, the popover open until the receipt, the strip's count from before the cost.

### Live voice (C1–C5)

C1 (2026-11-18) verified the voice server against its source, drew three clickable prototypes and wrote ADR 031, §10 "Live voice", §12 "Live voice", `docs/frontend.md` rules 9 and 15 "Live voice" and the glossary's rows; it changed no code. Each step below changes the contract first if it has to depart from it, runs `composer ci:check`, keeps `ELIFBA_ROOMS_VOICE` off wherever it is not testing voice, and is done only when its named tests exist and pass on SQLite and PostgreSQL. No step changes a file of `Classroom`, `Family`, `Play` or `Rankings`, a children's page, a board or the content. C2 and C3 run in parallel and meet only in the names of §10 "Live voice" and §12 "Live voice": the `.env` keys, `http://voice:7880`, `/rtc`, the voice room's name `{rooms.id}.{voice_epoch}` and the six API calls.

- **C2 — The voice server in Docker.**
    - _Scope._ §10 "Live voice": the `voice` service behind the Compose profile, its config with no third party, the two published ports, Caddy's `/rtc` route, the `.env` keys and their generator, the smoke checks, `composer dev:voice`, the https try-out for a real phone, and the operator's lines in the READMEs' hosting part. No PHP of the app, no page, no text of the interface.
    - _Files._ `docker-compose.yml`; `docker/voice/livekit.yaml`, `docker/voice/livekit.dev.yaml`; `docker/caddy/Caddyfile`; `.env.production.example`, `.env.example` (the block of `ELIFBA_ROOMS_VOICE*` keys, switched off); `scripts/docker-env.sh`, `scripts/docker-smoke.sh`, `scripts/dev-voice.sh`; `composer.json` (`scripts.dev:voice` only); `README.md`, `README.az.md` (hosting); `.github/workflows/ci.yml` only if the `docker` job needs the profile named.
    - _Tests._ `scripts/docker-smoke.sh` with and without the profile (§10 "Smoke test"); outside the repository, two headless Chromes with fake microphones join one voice room with tickets a script mints from the development key, each receives the other's audio track, every `RTCPeerConnection` was built without a public ICE server, and a packet capture during the joins shows no packet to UDP 3478 or 19302.
    - _Finish line._ `docker compose up -d --wait` without the profile starts today's six services with today's ports; with it, `voice` is healthy, `/rtc` answers through Caddy and the API does not; the server started with no route out; the laptop try-out lets two browsers hear each other. If a `stun_servers` entry that answers nothing slows a join, or bridge networking loses UDP, C2 says so in this section before it changes the config. The `docker` job is green.
    - _As built (2026-11-18)._ Neither condition arose: the page never uses the server's STUN entry, and the bridge network kept UDP (two browsers heard each other in 1.5–2 s through `composer dev:voice` and through the https try-out), so the config stands. Beside the files above: `docker/voice/start.sh` (the address into the config, the refusal Compose cannot make) and `default_sni` in the Caddyfile. The smoke ran 30 checks without the profile (a fresh `.env` and one written before C2) and 50 with it; CI's own run comes after a push. ADR 031 "As built (C2)".
- **C3 — The server and the contract.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "Live voice": the switch and `RoomsArea::voice()`, the five columns, the four actions and what `RemoveRoomPlayer` and `CloseRoom` gain, the policy's two abilities, the three routes with their limiter, the ticket, `RoomVoiceServer` and `RoomVoiceCut`, the sweep and its schedule, the state at `v: 5` with its fixtures, `voice: true` in the `rooms` prop of the privacy and welcome controllers. The pages only as far as the state needs: `contract.ts` parses `v: 5` and nothing is drawn yet.
    - _Files._ A migration `add_voice_to_rooms`; `config/elifba.php` (`rooms.voice.*`); `composer.json` (`firebase/php-jwt` in `require`); `app/Support/RoomsArea.php`; `app/Http/Middleware/EnsureRoomVoiceIsEnabled.php`, `bootstrap/app.php`; `app/Domain/Rooms/{Actions/{SetRoomVoice,SetRoomPlayerVoice,JoinRoomVoice,SweepRoomVoices,RemoveRoomPlayer,CloseRoom,CloseIdleRooms,LeaveRoom},Data/{RoomVoiceSwitchData,RoomPlayerVoiceData,JoinRoomVoiceData,RoomVoiceTicketData,RoomVoiceData,RoomPlayerData,RoomStateData},Enums/RoomVoiceStanding,Exceptions/{RoomVoiceNotCut,RoomVoiceUnreachable},Models/{Room,RoomPlayer},Policies/RoomPolicy,Support/{RoomVoice,RoomVoiceTicket,RoomVoiceServer,RoomVoiceCut,RoomState}}`; `app/Models/User.php`; `app/Http/Controllers/Rooms/{RoomVoiceController,RoomPlayerVoiceController,RoomVoiceTicketController}.php`, `PrivacyController`, `WelcomeController`; `routes/rooms.php`, `routes/console.php`; `app/Providers/AppServiceProvider.php` (`room-voice`); `resources/js/rooms/contract.ts` and what reads the state's version; `tests/Fixtures/rooms/state.*.json`; `tests/Support/{Rooms,FakeVoiceServer}.php`; `php artisan wayfinder:generate --with-form`; `lang/*` only for `rooms.voice.errors.{agreement,busy}`; §1 "Tables".
    - _Tests._ `RoomVoiceSwitchTest`, `RoomVoiceTest`, `RoomVoiceTicketTest`, `RoomVoiceCutTest` and the cases of §12 "Live voice" "Guard tests" in `RoomRaceTest`, `RoomStateTest`, `RoomContractTest`, `contract.test.ts`, `TeacherIsolationTest`, `RoleAreasTest`, `RoomsSwitchTest`, `BearerReachTest` and `RoomsApartTest`.
    - _Finish line._ With both switches on and the voice server faked, a host turns the voice on and every device reads it from the state; a present player gets a ticket whose every grant a test reads, a newcomer gets none until admitted, a muted player's ticket holds no microphone; a removal, the voice going off and a close each delete the voice room under a new epoch after the commit; a whole game is played with the voice server down and nothing differs. `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _As built (2026-11-19)._ On `main` with taunts, the winner's cheer and a room without a screen: the state is `v: 5`. The four guard test files and the cases of the table above pass on SQLite and PostgreSQL; the voice server was faked from its real answers and tried once for real (§12 "Live voice", "As built (C3)"). Beside the files above: `tests/Support/FakeVoiceServer.php` and `Rooms::voiceOn()`; `resources/js/components/rooms/rivalry.test.ts` only for the player's new key. Nothing is drawn: a page reads the state's `voice` and shows nothing of it until C4.
- **C4 — The pages and every word.**
    - _Scope._ `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Live voice", variant A: the voice bar on every moment of `rooms/show`, the sheet «Canlı səs», the sheet with the sentence, the switch in «Otağın idarəsi», the line for a waiting newcomer, every failure of ADR 031 decision 26, the screen's chip and marks. Every text of ADR 031 "Words" and decisions 14–17 in both languages, the cue toggle's new name, `/privacy`'s part and the welcome page's line, both READMEs' sections for adults' games, `CLAUDE.md`'s lines if a name changed.
    - _Files._ `package.json` (`livekit-client`, an exact version, with a credit where the other bundled libraries have theirs); `resources/js/rooms/{voice,voice-view}.ts` with their tests; `resources/js/components/rooms/{voice-bar,voice-sheet,voice-consent,use-room-voice}.tsx`, `room-tools.tsx`, `live-round.tsx`, `start-parts.tsx`, `sound-toggle.tsx`; `resources/js/pages/rooms/{show,screen}.tsx`; `components/welcome/rooms-section.tsx`, `pages/privacy.tsx`; `lang/az.json`, `lang/en.json`; `README.md`, `README.az.md`.
    - _Tests._ Vitest `voice.test.ts`, `voice-view.test.ts`; `RoomsApartTest` ("keeps the voice library inside the room's voice module"); `tests/Unit/RoomsWordingTest.php`; `TranslationsTest`; `PrivacyPageTest`, `WelcomePageTest`; `RoomPagesTest`; `sound-cues.test.ts` and `sound.test.ts` unchanged and passing.
    - _Finish line._ Seen in a browser at 375 × 667 and 1280 × 720, light and dark, az and en, with `composer dev:voice`: the host turns the voice on → a player reads the sentence, joins and answers the browser's question → the bar names who speaks → the host mutes a player and lets a newcomer in → the voice goes off; the tallest rounds (a 4 × 4 grid, an 8-letter anagram) fit with the bar; with the voice switch off no page differs from today by a pixel or a request; the voice library is one chunk from the app's host, loaded only on «Səsə qoşul». `composer ci:check` passes.
    - _As built (2026-11-19)._ In two sessions on `feat/rooms-voice`: the words first, then the pages (§12 "Live voice", "As built (C4, words)" and "As built (C4, pages)"). The files are `components/rooms/{use-room-voice.ts,voice-bar.tsx}` — the consent and the sheet live in `voice-bar.tsx` beside the bar — and `room-action-bar.tsx`, `mechanics/recall-cells.tsx` and `app.css` beside the ones named; `start-parts.tsx` and `sound-toggle.tsx` did not change. Every named test passes; the finish line was seen in a headless browser against `composer dev:voice`, with the measurements in §12.
- **C5 — Hardening and audit.**
    - _Scope._ ADR 031 decision 31's table, measured: on two real phones (an iPhone and an Android, on the https try-out of §10) and in a headless harness with twenty fake microphones — a row only a real phone or a mobile network can answer (the join on a mobile network, Wi-Fi to mobile, a hidden page or a locked phone on iOS Safari, a cue beside an open microphone on an iPhone) gets the harness's closest number, marked "harness", and waits for the phones, which are the owner's checklist (C5's third part); the failure drills of decision 26 (the voice server killed in the middle of a game, a network change, a removal, two devices, a hidden page and a locked phone on iOS Safari); the third-party audit of decision 2; what the voice server logged during it, read line by line for a name, a ticket or a whole address. It fixes what misses a target, or changes the target here with the measurement beside it.
    - _Files._ Whatever the measurements require of C2–C4's files; "As built" under ADR 031 and §12 "Live voice"; the measurements' table outside the repository, as `rooms-flow` has its own.
    - _Tests._ Every guard test of §12 "Live voice" still green; a test for each defect found.
    - _Finish line._ Every row of decision 31's table has a number beside its target; the audit found no request and no packet to a host that is not the instance's; and **the owner has read the sentences of ADR 031 decisions 14–17, the newcomer rule and the variant, and said yes (C5-O)** — only then is the ADR "Accepted" and may `ELIFBA_ROOMS_VOICE` be turned on anywhere. The release — the firewall's two ports, the VPS's size, the switch — is its own plan after the merge.
    - _As built (part 1 of 3, 2026-11-19)._ Measured in a harness outside the repository (`~/elifba-shots/voice/c5/`), every row with at least 20 runs and the load for its full 10 minutes; two defects fixed with a test each, three targets changed with the measurement beside them, the network change and the rows of real phones left to them (§12 "Live voice", "As built (C5, measurements)"; ADR 031 decision 31 and its "As built (C5, measurements)"). The audit and the checklists are the step's second and third sessions.
    - _As built (part 2 of 3, 2026-11-19)._ Audited: three blocking findings (the ticket in Caddy's error log, the voice server's `info` log, the ticket limiter's rows in the cache and its backups) and four major ones (a replay after a mute or a leave, `/rtc?publish=`, a video track named the microphone, the account in a reported failure) fixed with a test each, the smoke extended with and without the profile; sixteen minor ones listed (§12 "Live voice", "As built (C5, audit)"; ADR 031 "As built (C5, audit)"; `~/elifba-shots/voice/c5/audit.md`). The checklists are the third session.
    - _As built (part 3 of 3, 2026-11-19)._ The checklists: [`docs/live-voice-checklists.md`](live-voice-checklists.md) (§12 "Live voice", "As built (C5, checklists)"). With it the step is built as far as a harness reaches: every row of decision 31 and every drill of decision 26 is measured (harness) or a step of the real-phone checklist, and none was taken on a phone. Still open, in this order, and all the owner's: C5-O (the sentences of decisions 14–17, the newcomer rule, the variant, and the audit's questions) — until then ADR 031 is "Proposed" and `ELIFBA_ROOMS_VOICE` off everywhere people play; the real-phone checklist; the merge into `main`; the release's own plan, which starts at the release checklist.

### Taunts redesigned (T3–T4)

T3 (2026-11-19) drew the owner's asked-for variants as one clickable page — three places for the throw, three motions for a reaction in the middle of the screen, three opacities — and two ElevenLabs auditions (four voices × three phrases; then Nigar × three tones × ten new phrases), outside the repository (`~/elifba-shots/taunts-v2/`, `~/elifba-shots/taunt-voice/`; the page is the private Artifact <https://claude.ai/artifact/VRn6EDp9BZDFwWzKkQ5GTJ>). It took the owner's choices — C «Rəqibə toxun», 1 «Partlayış», 85 %, Nigar's first take, twelve phrases with the owner's two — and wrote ADR 029 "Amended 2026-11-19 (T3)", §12 "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)", the lines of §3 "Voice", §12 "Room games", "Room content" and "Adults' privacy" it changes, `docs/frontend.md` rule 15 "Taunts" and "Sound", the glossary's rows, CLAUDE.md's "Rooms" and the "Amended by" lines of ADRs 015, 024, 025, 027 and 030; it changed no code, content or `lang`. T4 builds on `main` with T2 and the iOS delivery fix of the same day (`fix/rooms-taunt-ios`) in it; it changes the contract first if it has to depart from it.

- **T4 — The strip's throw, the reaction in the middle, the voice.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)": the strip's rival side as the button with its badge, the fan of twelve, the reaction layer and its motion on the phone and the screen; `content/rooms/taunts.json` version 2 (`voice` per taunt, the `voice` block), two Fluent files, the twelve phrases and `rooms.taunts.at`, the clause of `privacy.rooms.contact`; `content:voice --only=taunt` and its clips, list, credits and prune; the prop's `voice`; the taunt cue in `roomCues()` and the player. Not the state, the protocol, the points or the room's sound set.
    - _Files._ `resources/js/components/rooms/{taunt-parts.tsx,taunts.ts,taunts.test.ts,rivalry-strip.tsx,live-round.tsx,sound-cues.ts,sound-cues.test.ts,use-room-sound.ts}`, `resources/js/rooms/sound.ts`, `resources/js/pages/rooms/{show,screen}.tsx`, `resources/css/app.css` (`--taunt-reaction-opacity`, `--animate-room-taunt-pop`); `content/rooms/taunts.json`, `content/assets/fluent/{1F92A,1F602}.svg`, `content/assets/rooms/taunts/*.mp3`, `content/voice-clips.json`, `content/credits.json`, `CREDITS.md`; `app/Domain/Content/{Actions/{GenerateVoiceClips,WriteVoiceClipList,PruneVoiceClips},Data/RoomTauntData,Support/{ContentRepository,ContentValidator,VoiceClips}}.php` (or a `TauntVoices` beside `NameVoices`) and the `content:voice` command; `lang/az.json`, `lang/en.json`; the tests of §12's table and `tests/Fixtures/content/*` for the validator.
    - _Tests._ §12 "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)", "Guard tests"; `TranslationsTest`, `RoomsWordingTest` passing; `composer ci:check`; the `pgsql` job.
    - _Finish line._ The twelve clips made once with `content:voice --only=taunt` and their review page given to the owner; the build goes on under the owner's standing rule, and a take the owner later dislikes is made again as content. With the switch on, two phones and the screen (light, dark, reduced motion; az and en; at 375 × 667 with the 4 × 4 grid and the 8-letter anagram; 1280 × 720): a player taps the rival's side of the strip, the fan opens, a taunt is thrown; the target's phone shows it in the middle at 85 % for 3 s and says it in Nigar's voice, the cells under it still take taps; the screen shows and says the same; the thrower and a third phone say nothing; a room without a screen says it on the target's phone only; «Mənə atılanları gizlət», the screen's toggle and each mute silence it; an untouched iPhone shows it in silence. `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _Deploy._ The owner asked on 2026-11-19 («bitəndə sonuncu sesiyaya de serverə deploy etsin») that T4 itself ship it: once `main` is fast-forwarded and both gates are green, T4 pushes `main` and updates elifba.io by the README's "Updating" routine (a backup first, `git pull`, `docker compose up -d --build --wait`), then checks the room pages there — the prop `taunts` with each clip's URL, a clip served as `audio/mpeg` — and tells the owner to reload room tabs opened before it (the state's `v` does not change, so an open page keeps the old bundle).
    - _As built._ Landed 2026-11-19 on `main` with live voice (C2–C5) under it: §12 "Taunts in the middle, said aloud (T3)", "As built (T4)", ADR 029 "As built (T4)" and rule 15's T3 block — the fan closing with its phase, the reaction's portal, the cue's `until`, the player's own taunt channel, `TauntVoices`, twelve clips made once (443 characters); the iOS fix it expected did not land, since its session found no delivery bug.

### Invitation link (I1–I2)

I1 (2026-11-19) read the join, registration, role, Google and confirmation paths in the code, counted their taps at 375 × 667, took its recommended options under the owner's standing rule and wrote ADR 033, §12 "The invitation link (I2)", the lines of §1, §7 and §12 it amends, docs/frontend.md rules 1 and 15 "The invitation link", the glossary's rows, CLAUDE.md's "Roles" and "The invitation link", and the "Amended by" lines of ADRs 008, 019, 020, 024, 026 and 032; it changed no code, `lang` or test. The owner may reverse a decision before I2 starts — or take ADR 033 decision 15, an unconfirmed account joining by an invitation, instead of decision 14.

- **I2 — The share row, joining on open, the door.**
    - _Scope._ §12 "The invitation link (I2)": `ShareRow` and `shareWay()`; the tag in `join.url` and its check; `RoomInvitation`, `rooms.invitation` and `RoomJoinController::show`'s join on open; the door (`rooms/invited`, `CreateInvitedPlayer`, the limiter, `redirectGuestsTo`); the Google completion page's player tick; the notice's line and its look again; a player's «Dəyiş»; `privacy.rooms.share` and the changed `privacy.rooms.what`; decision 18's keys. Not the state, the protocol, `JoinRoom`, `/register`, a class game, a home game, a board or a child's page.
    - _Files._ ADR 033 "To build".
    - _Tests._ ADR 033 "To build", its table; `TranslationsTest`; `composer ci:check`; the `pgsql` job.
    - _Finish line._ With the switch on, on real phones (ADR 033 "To build", "Seen on devices"): the host sends the link by «WhatsApp» and by «Paylaş»; opened from WhatsApp on an iPhone and an Android phone, a signed-in player is in the game with that tap; a signed-out phone sees the door, and a newcomer gets in by Google without the mailbox and by e-mail through it — in the same browser and in another; a link preview, a prefetch and the code field still meet the join page; an old link of a closed room is refused; the headers each browser sent are written into ADR 033 "As built (I2)". `composer ci:check` passes and the `pgsql` job is green.
    - _As built._ Landed 2026-11-19 on `feat/rooms-invite-link`: §12 "The invitation link (I2)", "As built (I2)", ADR 033 "As built (I2)" and rule 15 "The invitation link", "As built (I2)" — `RoomInvitation`, `RememberRoomInvitation`, the tag, the join on open, the door, the Google tick, the notice's line and its look again, the share row and «Dəyiş»; seen in headless Chromium at 375 × 667 (`~/elifba-shots/invite/`). The real phones of the finish line — WhatsApp on an iPhone and an Android phone, WhatsApp Web, WhatsApp's own preview fetch — are still the owner's to run.

## Open questions for the owner

The owner answered the five questions A1 raised on 2026-11-09 by accepting the recommendations, and the sections above follow them:

| #   | Question              | Answer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Where              |
| --- | --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------ |
| 1   | Letter order          | No textbook order: the teacher switches on the learned letters one by one                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | §3 Learned letters |
| 2   | Voice                 | No recorded voice in v1; the teacher reads the prompt; `prompt.audio` stays null. Reopened 2026-11-11: prerecorded prompts and praise in a synthetic voice (ADR 015). Reopened 2026-11-12 for names: a first name on the built-in name list is said from its own prerecorded clip, any other stays silent (ADR 016) | §3 Voice           |
| 3   | Operator and consent  | Operator name and e-mail from `.env` on `/privacy` (filled for the public instance at O5); teachers accept the privacy note at registration; a parent note on `/privacy`; no consent collected in the app                                                                                                           | §7                 |
| 4   | Shared computers      | A guest board claimed from the teacher's phone, next to the teacher board (A7)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | §4 Pairing         |
| 5   | Points and visibility | 3 / 2 / 1 per solved round, never negative, none in whole-class mode; the full ranking only on the teacher's pages; the board shows only the podium                                                                                                                                                                 | §5                 |

Raised by R1 (2026-11-11). None blocks a step: each has a recommendation, and R2–R7 follow it unless the owner says otherwise.

| #   | Question                                         | Recommendation (what the design follows)                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Where               |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| 6   | The wording of the three agreements              | The sentences of §7 "Agreements". They are the owner's promise to schools and parents, so the owner (or a lawyer) should read them before R2 ships; changing them is two lines in `lang/`.                                                                      | §7, ADR 013         |
| 7   | «rəqəm» or «ədəd» for 10                         | Keep «rəqəm» in `«{x}» rəqəmini tap!`, as parents say it to a small child. Strictly 10 is an «ədəd»; if the content reviewer prefers it, the template changes in `prompts.json` (R6), no code.                                                                  | §3, ADR 012         |
| 8   | Which themes offer the picture and number levels | `animals` and `fruit` (named and counted), then `colors` (pictures) and `numbers` (numbers) in R6. Not the cities (every picture is the same map), nor train, balloons and symbols for now (people, rooms, feasts): adding one later is one word in its pack.   | §3 "Units", ADR 012 |
| 9   | The colours and the things counted               | Eleven colours: qırmızı, sarı, yaşıl, göy, narıncı, bənövşəyi, çəhrayı, qəhvəyi, qara, ağ, boz — without «mavi», which a small child cannot tell from «göy» in one round. R6 proposes the things to count; the owner reviews both packs as for the others (O2). | §3 "Units", R6      |
| 10  | How many children a parent may add               | 10 (`elifba.children.max`): a family fits, a class does not.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | §7, ADR 011         |
| 11  | No archive for a child                           | A child is deleted (with a confirmation), never archived; the roadmap's R4 line said "archive". Deleting removes the child's games and stars at once.                                                                                                           | §7, ADR 011         |
| 12  | Own words for parents                            | Not in this roadmap: «Sözlər» stays in the teacher area. A parent who is also a teacher gets their own words in home games too. A words page for parents can follow if parents ask.                                                                             | §3 "Teacher words"  |

Asked by B1 (2026-11-17) before it wrote, after the options were compared against the code; the owner's answers are fixed and §12 follows them:

| #   | Question                       | Answer                                                                                                                                                                   | Where        |
| --- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ |
| 13  | Who may open a room            | An account that holds a third role with its own agreement sentence — not any teacher or parent, and not an account without a role                                        | §12, ADR 019 |
| 14  | Does a joiner need an account  | Yes, every player; the advisor had recommended a code and a typed name. The same role then serves the host and the players                                               | §12, ADR 020 |
| 15  | What stays after a room closes | The results with names, for the retention period (400 days); the advisor had recommended keeping nothing. A player can take their name out, the host can delete the room | §12, ADR 022 |

Raised by B1. None blocks a step: each has a recommendation, and B2–B7 follow it unless the owner says otherwise when reading the design (B1-O).

| #   | Question                                   | Recommendation (what the design follows)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Where                 |
| --- | ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------- |
| 16  | The names and the sentence of the new role | The role «Oyunçu», the area «Otaqlar», and the sentence of §7 "Agreements". The sentence is the owner's promise to players, so the owner (or a lawyer) should read it and `/privacy`'s section before the switch is turned on; changing them is lines in `lang/`.                      | §7, §12, ADR 019      |
| 17  | Does speed count                           | Yes: 10 points for a right answer and up to 10 more for speed, in ten steps; a wrong answer gives 0. Without speed a race ends in ties. Changing the numbers is one class (`RoomPointsRule`); old rooms keep the points they were given.                                               | §12, ADR 022          |
| 18  | The name a room shows                      | The account's name as the account wrote it (`users.name`), not a nickname per room: nothing new to store or moderate, and "everyone under their own name". Showing only the first word of it is a small change if the owner prefers.                                                   | §12, ADR 020, ADR 024 |
| 19  | Confirmed e-mail before joining            | Required, as in every area (`verified`). It is the accountability the owner chose accounts for; Google sign-in confirms at once, the form needs the mail's link.                                                                                                                       | §12, ADR 020          |
| 20  | The limits                                 | 20 players in a room, one open room per host, closed after 3 idle hours, 5–20 rounds a game (`elifba.rooms.*`). One server carries this; larger rooms are a new decision (ADR 021).                                                                                                    | §12, ADR 020          |
| 21  | The big screen on a shared computer        | Not in the first stage: the screen is a page of the host's own session, and the guest board stays a children's board. If hosts ask for a screen without a login, it gets a credential of its own then.                                                                                 | §12, ADR 021          |
| 22  | The first games                            | Two per family: «Ardıcıllığı yadda saxla» and «Xanaları yadda saxla»; «Sıranı davam etdir» and «Artıq olanı tap»; «Anaqram» and «Düzgün yazılış». The names are working names for `lang/`; the owner reviews them with the content (B7-O).                                             | §12, ADR 023          |
| 23  | Where the adults' words come from          | Our own list under MIT, grown from the theme packs' words and reviewed by the owner. An open game («hərflərdən istənilən söz») waits for a full word list under an allowed license: the Hunspell list is GPL / MPL, Wiktionary is CC BY-SA and would need its own folder and a review. | §12, ADR 023          |
| 24  | A young player                             | No age is asked. The design is the same minimum for everyone: a name among people who share a code, no chat, no profile, every way out. If the owner wants the area's texts to say «böyüklər üçün» more firmly, that is wording, not a field.                                          | §12, ADR 024          |
| 25  | A share card of a room's winners           | Not in the first stage: it would carry adults' names out of the room, and §6's card is a class's. It can follow as its own decision if players ask.                                                                                                                                    | §12, ADR 022          |

Raised by T1 (2026-11-18). The owner's three answers of that day are fixed (the thrower pays 1 point and the target loses nothing; all game long, never covering the question; a ready list of 8–12 in az and en, nothing typed, no sound — the sound reversed by the owner in T3, below). The rest was taken under the owner's standing rule of the same day — a session takes its recommended option — and T2 follows it unless the owner says otherwise:

| #   | Question                      | Recommendation (what the design follows)                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Where        |
| --- | ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
| 36  | Which of the three prototypes | A, «Zolaqda»: the tray beside the strip, the strip's rival as the target, the card over the target's strip, the screen showing it over its own. B («Panel») lets a player choose any target; C («Meydan») is one tap and a stage on the screen but shrinks the grid. | §12, ADR 029 |
| 37  | The ten phrases               | ADR 029 decision 27, in az and en. A phrase or an emoji changes as content (`taunts.json`, two `lang` keys), no code.                                                                                                                                                | ADR 029      |
| 38  | When the cost shows           | At once on the thrower's own phone; in the ranking and the strip at the next close, as «−1» beside the round's result — so nothing moves during a question.                                                                                                          | §12, ADR 029 |
| 39  | The first round               | Nobody can throw before it closes (every balance is 0); a duel's tray says so, a race shows no tray yet.                                                                                                                                                             | §12, ADR 029 |
| 40  | How often                     | Once per 10 s per thrower (`rooms.taunts.cooldown_ms`); shown for 4 s (`show_ms`).                                                                                                                                                                                   | §12, ADR 029 |
| 41  | Ways out                      | «Mənə atılanları gizlət» on each device; the host's «Atmacalar» switch, on by default, at any time; the host's «Çıxar» as before.                                                                                                                                    | §12, ADR 029 |
| 42  | The big screen                | Shows each taunt over its strip for 4 s, with a toggle of its own.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | §12, ADR 029 |
| 43  | What is kept                  | The cost for the retention period; whom a taunt was aimed at for a day; who taunted whom shown nowhere after the game. `/privacy` says so in two sentences, which the owner reads before T2 ships; the player role's sentence does not change.                       | §12, ADR 029 |

Decided by the owner before C1 (2026-11-18); fixed, and §12 "Live voice" follows them:

| --- | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------ |
| 44  | Where the voice travels | Through a voice server the instance hosts itself: never a cloud service, never phone to phone                                                                                                                                                                      | §12, ADR 031 |

| 46  | The production release  | A separate plan after the merge                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | §10, ADR 031 |

Raised by C1 with three clickable prototypes opened for the owner. The owner's standing instruction of 2026-11-18 was not to wait for an answer, so C2–C5 follow each recommendation; none is the owner's yes, and all are read before the switch is turned on (C5-O):

| --- | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- |

B («Başlıq») and C («Sıra») stay in `~/elifba-shots/voice/prototypes/`; taking another is rule 15 "Live voice" and C4. | frontend 15, ADR 031 |
| 48  | A player who arrives after the voice is on | Waits: hears nothing and says nothing until the host taps «Səsə burax». Letting newcomers listen at once, or in at once, is ADR 031 decision 12 and one column.                                                                                                     | §12, ADR 031         |
| 49  | The sentences                              | The voice's consent, the welcome page's line and `/privacy`'s part as ADR 031 decisions 14–17 have them, with «uçdan-uca şifrələmə yoxdur» said plainly. They are the owner's promise; changing them is lines in `lang/`.                                           | §7, ADR 031          |
| 50  | The host's mute                            | Stays until the host lifts it, and lifting it never opens a microphone. A mute that the player can undo would be no tool for a host.                                                                                                                                | §12, ADR 031         |

| 52  | How many rooms may talk at once            | 5 (`elifba.rooms.voice.max_rooms`), until the release's plan has read the VPS's size and run a load test on it.                                                                                                                                                     | §10, ADR 031         |
| 53  | The cue toggle's name                      | «Oyun səsləri», so that «Səs» and «Canlı səs» are not confused.                                                                                                                                                                                                     | frontend 15, ADR 031 |
